# Backlog of the tools repository · `plan:toolsbacklog:superloop-tools-backlog`

This backlog holds the open work that a change to this repository discharges: its packages, its Meson layer, the linter, the public pack, the lane drivers and the capability door, with their tests and their documents. It holds open work only, and every item keeps the label it was minted with for life: the kind of the label says what the item is, and the first word of its first paragraph says where the item stands.

## Priorities · `sec:toolsbacklog:priorities`

**Register (Priorities)** · `reg:toolsbacklog:priorities`

The estate this backlog serves is becoming one where every producing worker runs in a box, Claude and Codex alike, under the confined and reviewable form whose every outward effect is a door request with a record, where a compute node is a root-held server installed from the estate's documents and kit, where every repository installs its documentation as a package does, and where every loop and worker corresponds through the mail system that landed as v0.4.0, with this repository publishing signed releases whose signatures the release tool verifies. The Claude boxed workers come first, because they are the estate's next large task and every Claude lane runs today outside the box, with its credential at the host uid and a second code path for every lifecycle concern; the removal of non-boxed worker support follows them, because the host form stays a second path to review and secure until it is gone, and a loop that needs a worker outside a box uses an in-harness subagent of its own session. The root-full node's programs, the documentation install with the contributing guide, the node routes and the cutover's follow-ups with the 0.4.1 release come third; the signed releases, the pack and the linter, the lanes on bare workspaces, the door, the box and the gateway, the tests under load with the hooks, watchers and desktop tools, and the loop homes, server packages, build and documents follow. A slice worker reads this register first and proposes a slice of twenty to thirty rows, under the slice form of the generic specification (``proto:dispatch:slice-dispatch``); every open item stands in exactly one row, in the order it is best taken, with the row's dependencies, its reading and its bar named and its items larger than one lane listed. While the root-full node's acceptance debt stands (`prob:gates:fixtures-find-the-posix-shell-instead-of-assuming-dash`) and (`claim:gates:confined-login-reds-are-namespace-and-terminal-differences`), the bar on that node for every row is the touched packages' tests plus the Meson lint and release suites, with the workspace run reported and its untouched reds named. The last column names the lane that holds a row or part of one, and a general brief's reader passes over held rows and picks the next unheld one (`conv:toolsbacklog:working-the-backlog`).

## Charter · `sec:toolsbacklog:charter`

**Convention (Status grammar)** · `conv:toolsbacklog:status-grammar`

Every item opens with one of three statuses in bold, followed by the evidence for it, so a reader can triage by the first word and grep it: **OPEN** for work not started or whose remainder is unscheduled, with whatever part landed stated; **ACTIVE** for work a lane carries now; and **PARKED** for a deliberate deferral carrying its reason and revisit condition. A status changes when its evidence does, and the evidence that changed it is stated in the same paragraph; the earlier status and its text stay beneath. These are the open statuses of the estate backlog's grammar, ``conv:backlog:status-grammar``, whose closed statuses, DONE, CLOSED and SUPERSEDED, are the words of the estate's record documents; this repository does not import the estate's corpus, so the grammar is stated here rather than cited.

**Convention (Item kinds)** · `conv:toolsbacklog:item-kinds`

An item's kind says what it is, and its status where it stands. A Problem (`prob`) is work assigned: a defect or a change, with its remedy where one is known; a Claim (`claim`) is a finding stated to be verified before it is relied on; an Open Problem or Open Question (`open`) is a design question whose answer is not yet known; a Question (`q`) asks a plain question of a named party; a Goal (`goal`) is a desideratum or a direction without one bounded piece of work; a Decision (`dec`) is a decided rule or form whose adoption or statement is still open; and a Record (`rec`) keeps a measurement or a piece of evidence until its revisit condition decides it. Work items carry Problem rather than To-do because the kind registry reserves `todo` for the machine-derived deficiency notices of the to-do profile (`[ORCHESTRATION-sig:todos:kind-extension]`), so no authored head may mint it.

**Convention (Intake and exit)** · `conv:toolsbacklog:intake-and-exit`

New items come from letters to the loop and from review findings, sorted at intake into the backlog of the repository whose change discharges them; an item that a change to the generic repository discharges belongs to its backlog, ``plan:genericbacklog:orchestration-backlog``, and one that an estate's operations discharge to that estate's backlog. An item that is met leaves this backlog: the loop's record of the landing names the commit that met it, and what the item established is folded into this repository's documentation rather than kept here as a closed entry. An item whose evidence names private details of an estate keeps those paragraphs in that estate's backlog and names the record that holds them.

**Convention (Working the backlog)** · `conv:toolsbacklog:working-the-backlog`

Every worker of every loop receives clones of the bare repositories it needs in its workspace and makes its own checkouts; the loop pushes into and fetches from those bares, and a superloop keeps its own bares of record with mirror bares on the compute server, so that a hand-over is a repository rather than a prepared checkout and a clone on the node is a delta (``rule:dispatch:workers-clone-from-bare-repositories`` of the generic specification).

This backlog is worked in slices (``sec:dispatch:slice-work`` of the generic specification): the loop sets the priorities and submits rows to the backlog manager by letter; a slice worker, briefed with the workspace, the repository's contributing guide and a budget, reads the backlog with its priorities register, proposes a slice of twenty to thirty rows, or the whole backlog where it holds fewer, and stops; the loop answers that proposal and steers by letter afterwards, never before it. A slice worker never rebases, squashes, amends or force-pushes what it has pushed (``rule:dispatch:lane-branch-merge-in`` there): when the base branch moves it merges the base in, and a correction after a review is a fixup commit on top, so that a review in flight keeps its anchors and the worker keeps its own trail. The loop stages the lane branch on a review branch, `review/<topic>`, which the worker never touches (``proto:dispatch:review-branch-landing`` there): basic rebasing of the staged work is the loop's, advanced rebasing is the reviewer's, done on the review branch with its trivial fixups named in the review, and a correction beyond trivial returns to the worker as a fixup under the merge-in rule. The loop alone makes the commits of record, remade onto the development branch with its own identity and messages, gates them, and pushes the base back into the workspace bares.

A working lane holds the rows it took, and the register's last column names the lane beside each row it holds, or the items it holds where it took part of a row, because a register that several workers read at once otherwise sends every general brief to its first row. A slice worker reading the register under a general brief passes over held rows and held items and proposes from the next unheld ones; the loop asks the backlog manager to set a holding when it accepts a worker's proposal and to clear it when the lane closes, so that the column states which work is free at the instant the register is read.

**Register (Areas)** · `reg:toolsbacklog:areas`

An item's area is the subject of what must change to discharge it, and the sections of the open work follow the areas in this order; an item keeps the area and slug of its label for life, so an area shared with the other backlogs names the same subject there.

|area|subject|
|----|-------|
|`mail`|the superloop mail system's implementation: the delivery core and its records, the folders at loop and worker birth, the box mount, the notice and waiting, the round transport and the one-time migration|
|`door`|the capability door: the door service and stub, compute through the door and its command grammar, the triage agent and the requests store|
|`lanes`|the lane lifecycle of `superloop-lane` and the drivers: launch, rounds, resume, settle, retirement and its scans, run directories, reports, letters and re-pin|
|`box`|the lane box: the container helper, the vendor proxy, the box image and the exercise cases|
|`gates`|the repository's tests where they go red under node load, fixture timing, and the speed of the test battery|
|`homes`|the loop entry and the loop-home package, the sign-in home commands, the reconcile and the repair, and their tests|
|`servers`|the server-side packages: staff provisioning and node status|
|`estate`|what the published tree of this repository carries|
|`deploy`|what the drivers report when a deploy changes a launch input|
|`build`|the Meson layer, build-sync and its census, the installer, binary names and release harnesses|
|`linter`|the label linter: its check, formatter, export, census and burn|
|`docs`|the repository's specifications, package READMEs and changelog where the fix is prose|
|`watchers`|the worker monitor of driver follow and the letters tool|
|`gateway`|the lane gateway and its runner|
|`hooks`|the heartbeat guard and the Claude hook|
|`pack`|the public pack: cut, replay and release|
|`desktop`|the chat export and the desktop session tools|

## Open work · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-work`

### The mail system · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-mail`

**Problem (The deployment plan states an absent previous image for a program new to the deployment)** · `prob:mail:deployment-plan-states-an-absent-previous-image`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-04, from lane r002g77's review N4) The version-one deployment plan requires a `previous` image for every linker, but a program new to the deployment, superloop-mail at #106, has none; the deploy retains a copy of the current image as its previous, which a rollback leaves installed and inert. An explicit absent-previous form in the plan, refused by rollback's digest check for that artifact and removed at rollback, states the truth instead of a stand-in that a rollback would keep.

**Problem (The mail journal's size and a prepare's name search stay bounded as the history grows)** · `prob:mail:journal-and-prepare-costs-bounded-by-history`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-03 09:05Z, from the review of lane r002g76's c02, F3) The mail journal re-walks the whole journal on every metadata write to measure its size; it keeps a running byte total instead. Lands after the slice; not blocking.

(2026-11-03 09:35Z, from the review of lane r002g76's c03, G5) Every mail `prepare` scans all retained records and searches temporary-name serials from 0, so its cost grows with the history; it is one row with the journal size re-walk.

Both costs sit in the journal and recovery module and the inbox publication adapter, whose lanes landed in v0.4.0 (``prob:mail:journal-records-recovery-and-retention`` and ``prob:mail:journaled-inbox-publication`` in its changelog), so nothing this item waited for is still open.

**Problem (The migration's retained-home witness binds the inode and a filesystem identity that survives a reboot)** · `prob:mail:retired-home-witness-binds-a-stable-filesystem-id`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, deploy #106, the mail cutover) The migration inventory's retained-home witness (``packages/superloop-lane/src/launch/migration_inventory.rs``) bound `st_dev`, and on btrfs the anonymous device numbers change at a reboot, so a witness taken before a reboot refused the same home after it; the hotfix 556b2744 compares the inode alone, which no longer tells two filesystems apart. The proper fix binds the witness to the inode and a stable filesystem identity, the filesystem UUID or the subvolume id, with a test that a changed device number under the same identity is accepted and a different filesystem refused.

**Problem (The migration reconciles run history that predates the import records instead of refusing it)** · `prob:mail:migration-reconciles-pre-record-run-history`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, deploy #106) `migrate prepare` refuses every loop with unattributed run names or open reservations, and retirement refuses reservations that predate the import records; no native route existed, so 1,389, 521 and 8 entries of three loops were archived by hand into a dated legacy directory under each loop's temporary root. Historical retired records with no surviving home (165 in one loop) and duplicate retirements of a reused run name, an ambiguous journaled lock, refused the census as well and were archived the same way.

The census classifies these shapes (an unattributed run name, a reservation older than the import records, a retired record without a home, a duplicate retirement) instead of refusing them, and the migration reconciles each by a native route recorded in its manifest, before the next estate migrates. The estate's hand-archived trees are reconciled through it once it lands (``prob:estate:legacy-run-archives-reconciled-through-the-native-route`` in the estate's backlog), and the historical retirement of old runs is the lanes' route beside it (`prob:lanes:historical-retirement-route`).

**Problem (The migration's configuration snapshot leaves the vendor homes out)** · `prob:mail:migration-snapshot-leaves-vendor-homes-out`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, deploy #106) The migration snapshots the loops' configuration tier whole, 14 GB because the loops' Codex and Claude vendor homes live there: `prepare` fingerprints it in 157 s and `apply` copies it with an fsync per file, 85 GB written. Either the snapshot excludes the vendor homes, which the migration does not touch, or the vendor homes move out of the configuration tier; the vendor-home move (`prob:homes:vendor-lane-homes-move`) places them beside the custody root inside that tier, so the second remedy reverses its destination, and the exclusion is the remedy that keeps both. The snapshot's size and wall are reported beside the step.

(2026-11-05, lane r002g81's reading) The cutover code snapshots the loops' whole configuration tree with only the private tier excluded, while the 0.4.0 changelog's entry says the snapshot covers only the mail configuration; the entry's correction is its own item (`prob:docs:changelog-migration-snapshot-entry-corrected`), and the exclusion here is the remedy.

**Problem (The migration declares the delivery durability before apply's final inventory, and the inventory prints its failures)** · `prob:mail:migration-apply-orders-durability-and-prints-failures`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, deploy #106) `apply`'s final endpoint inventory requires the durability flags true, but provisioning writes the mail service configuration with them false and the plan's ninth row sets them only before activation, so the inventory refuses at the end of an apply that was otherwise correct; the inventory also hides its failure list. The durability declaration is ordered before `apply`, and the inventory prints every failure it counts.

**Problem (The verify-only phase's missing-lock refusal has a named code that the custody harness pins)** · `prob:mail:verify-only-missing-lock-refusal-is-named`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-04, from the post review p04 of lane r002g76, N1) The two-account harness ``mail_custody.py`` checks the missing-lock verify-only refusal by its exit status 2 alone, and the refusal's text is the generic `io-failure: No such file or directory`, so another failure with the same status passes the check. The refusal gets a named code, or the harness pins it by the exact assertion the review gives, so that the check fails on any other refusal.

### The capability door · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-door`

**Problem (The door rubric custody rule refuses every rubric published under the owner's home)** · `prob:door:door-rubric-custody-admits-service-uid`

**OPEN.** The O_PATH rubric walk hotfix of lane r002d97 landed with ``entry:door:door-agent-runner-failure-evidence``, but ``config.rs`` still admits `rubric_root` by syntax only; the proper fix remains: the custody rule admits the door service's own uid as a custodian beside root and the loop uid, install-door checks the rubric with the same rule at install and refuses a root it would refuse at run time, and the fixture hands two uids.

The paragraphs of this item's evidence that name an estate's private details are kept in that estate's backlog as ``rec:door:door-rubric-custody-admits-service-uid``.

**Problem (The request record drops the triage agent's observations, so a failed agent cannot be read from it)** · `prob:door:request-record-keeps-agent-observations`

**OPEN.** A check of the tree found the request record still without the agent's `observations` or a `rubric_failure` field, so a failed triage agent cannot be read from it; the whole fix remains: a new strict schema carrying the agent evidence, a digest-bound one-time conversion of schema-four records, the rewrite path extended and every reader listed.

TOOLS (from the r002d97 investigation, LOW/MEDIUM, own lane): the request record drops the agent's `observations` (``requests/judgement.rs:51`` copies selected agent fields; strict schema four refuses extra keys), so a loop cannot read from the record why its triage agent failed; a new strict schema with the agent evidence (`rubric_failure` and the probe evidence), a digest-bound one-time conversion of schema-four records preserving their judgement with null evidence, the rewrite path extended, every reader (list, wait, status, triage, review, adjudicate, modes, rebuild-index, rewrite, the exercises) listed. Not a hotfix: the rewrite touches every door loop's store at deploy.

**Problem (The request reader accepts the upload bound and output count independently and on preflight records)** · `prob:door:request-upload-bound-and-count-together`

**OPEN.** Part landed through lane B: both or neither of `bound` and `output_bytes` are required and the maximum is `MAX_UPLOAD_OUTPUT_LIMIT` (``requests.rs:4`` and `:458` on tools main 3c0f1a13), while the two keys are still accepted on preflight records and the shared consumer was deferred to lane C. The recorded status note ends mid-word in the source table ("Requests re"), so its rest is lost; the earlier check that found the literal 1_073_741_824 at ``requests.rs:421`` predates the constant.

(2026-10-18) TOOLS (d98 round one note 8 as rebased, MEDIUM): ``requests.rs:409-422`` accepts bound and output_bytes independently, the maximum as the literal 1_073_741_824 (:414), and, now that the block follows main's preflight block, the two keys on a preflight-refused record that no writer produces; require both or neither, reject them on preflight records, use MAX_UPLOAD_OUTPUT_LIMIT

**Problem (The no-credential-bytes claim rests on the agent program never writing them to stderr)** · `prob:door:agent-stderr-tail-credential-filter`

**OPEN.** Lane A deferred it to the code lanes as outside its package-only scope; the README's claim still rests on the agent program never writing credential bytes to stderr, which is neither stated nor filtered. The recorded status note ends mid-word in the source table ("Failed agent stderr re"), so its rest is lost.

``packages/superloop-lane/README.md:65`` (e11): "credential bytes are absent from prompts, records and logs" now also rests on the configured agent program never writing them to stderr; state the dependence in the README, or filter the tail.

**Open Problem (the door refuses compute at once when its execution slots are taken under many lanes)** · `open:door:door-compute-capacity-under-many-lanes`

**OPEN.** The door still answers `door-capacity-refusal` at once when its execution slots are taken, and the loop's only interim is an in-round note that the refusal is transient and the request is repeated after 120 s; what remains is deciding where the per-loop compute slot count on a node is set and whether a request may wait a bounded time for a slot, and the worker charter stating that the refusal is transient with its retry cadence.

Door compute capacity under many lanes (torrust-index, fifteen lanes, measured 19:30Z to 19:50Z 2026-10-21): the door answers `door-capacity-refusal` locally (0.002 to 0.004 s, no wrapper report) as soon as its execution slots are taken, and most lanes met it on every gate request (five refused gates in a row on one lane); workers read the refusal as a blocker and published BLOCKED with their work pushed, costing a resume round each (the loop's interim: an in-round note that a capacity refusal is transient and the same request is repeated after 120 s). Two design questions for the door: the compute slot count per loop on a node (where it is set, whether the loop may raise it at its owner's word beside its lane count), and whether a request may wait a bounded time for a slot (queued behind the slots the way the gate script waits behind its flock) instead of refusing at once; and the worker charter says plainly that a capacity refusal is transient and names the retry cadence.

**Problem (the door's compute deadline is outlasted by a package suite)** · `prob:door:door-compute-deadline-per-form`

**OPEN.** A package suite (`cargo test --locked -p torrust-assayer`) now sits at the door's 1,800 s request bound and ends `door-request-deadline`, so a lane cannot finish its own package gate through the door; what remains is the door-family fix: the loop's `door.toml` registers long command forms with their own request deadline, the door reports the registered bound in its refusal, and the runbook's door section states the registration.

the door's compute request deadline is outlasted by a package suite (torrust-index letter 01792659409385954190, 2026-10-22T08:56Z): `cargo test --locked -p torrust-assayer` through the door ended `door-request-deadline` (transport exit 125) at 1,786.8 s, twice, with no RUN-REPORT and no failure, inside a research integration binary; the same suite on a slightly older base took 1,457.1 s, so it now sits at the 1,800 s request bound. A lane cannot finish its own package gate through the door, reports BLOCKED on the deadline rather than a defect, and the round is spent waiting. Fix (door family): the loop's `door.toml` registers long command forms with their own request deadline (owner-controlled per loop, the ordinary bound unchanged for every other form), the door reports the registered bound in its deadline refusal, and the runbook's door section states the registration; interim for a lane: split the suite by integration target (`--test NAME`) where the door's corpus admits that form, and report each target's wall.

**Problem (the triage agent's Codex home under the per-call temporary directory blocks its helper binaries)** · `prob:door:door-agent-codex-home-outside-temp`

**OPEN.** The door's triage agent still runs Codex with its home under the per-call temporary directory, so every call warns that it could not create its PATH-alias helper binaries and runs without them; what remains is the runner giving each call a Codex home outside that directory, with a fixture asserting the warning absent from the stderr tail.

the door's triage agent runs Codex with its home under the per-call temporary directory, so Codex refuses to create its PATH-alias helper binaries there and warns before proceeding (torrust-index record `hrestore:18e0d0ed731db9d19e6`, `agent_runner_stderr_tail`, 2026-10-23: `WARNING: proceeding, even though we could not create PATH aliases: Refusing to create helper binaries under temporary dir "…/tmp/door-agent/call-…" (codex_home: AbsolutePathBuf("…/call-…/config"))`; the mount is plain btrfs without noexec and the door unit carries no temporary-mount hardening, so the refusal is Codex's own policy keyed on the path, not an exec-denied mount); the agent proceeds, so the fatal cause of item ``entry:door:door-agent-reserved-provider-id`` was the provider id alone, but every call pays the warning and runs without the helpers Codex wanted. Fix: the runner gives each call a Codex home outside the directory Codex treats as temporary (the loop's door directory beside the per-call root, removed after reaping as the call directory is), with a fixture asserting the warning absent from the stderr tail; the record field of item ``entry:door:door-agent-reserved-provider-id`` shows the warning gone. Where: packages/superloop-lane/src/door/agent_runner.py, ``agent.rs``; with item ``entry:door:door-agent-reserved-provider-id``.

**Problem (the door's completion record can lack a measured wrapper wall)** · `prob:door:door-compute-wrapper-wall-measured`

**OPEN.** On the #51 door the null wall no longer occurs on the wrapped route: at 2026-10-24T04:35Z torrust-index's hprecdecl request record held 35 compute effects each carrying a `wrapper_wall_seconds` that agrees with the wrapper's `RUN-REPORT … wall=` line, with `compute_wall_seconds` about 1.4 s above it for checkout preparation and transport. The case still open is a wrapped command that prints no `RUN-REPORT` line, which needs the node script to publish its own measured wrapper wall and CPU.

(tools, superloop-lane door compute) the door's completion record carried `wrapper_wall_seconds: null` on every `door-complete` reply of one boxed run (torrust-index hrepro, ten of ten records, builds, linters and test runs alike, `wrapper_exit_status` set and `preparation_wall_seconds` measured, produced by the door build active since 2026-10-23 08:54Z, before deploy #49), so a command whose duration the worker's transcript lost has no record to recover it from and the lane's gate table reads "duration not retained" (``req:environment:wall-time-reported``). At tools main 63823b57 the door-side reader (``packages/superloop-lane/src/door/compute.rs:241-263``) fills the field from the wrapper's `RUN-REPORT … wall=Ns exit=N` line and refuses `door-compute-evidence-refusal` when the line is absent or its exit disagrees, so a `door-complete` with a null wall is not reachable there, while the node script (``door/compute_node.py:169-172``) measures `wrapper_elapsed` itself and does not publish it. Fix: the node script publishes its own measured wrapper wall and, from the child's resource usage, its user and system CPU in the completion detail, independent of the wrapped command's report line, with the door-side RUN-REPORT reading kept as the cross-check and named when the two disagree; a fixture whose wrapped command prints no RUN-REPORT line still yields a measured wall; torrust-index reports what the #50 build yields after its door restart. Source: torrust-index letter 01792795130912390755.

**Problem (the escalation process moves into one execute-command crate in bites)** · `prob:door:execute-command-extraction-bites`

**OPEN.** Part landed: bites 2, 3a, 4, 5 and 6 of the extraction are on tools main 3c0f1a13 (bite 2 b338ed6 with a85bc58 and ba3ae9b, bite 3a 0f73104 and b77de20, bite 4 48a7db8, bite 5 892fa53, bite 6 3c0f1a1) and were deployed as #57, #58 and #59; bite 3b, the external operations and owner escalation routes that ``req:executecommand:escalation-acceptance`` of tools `adr/038-execute-command-crate.md` requires before those routes may be claimed, is open. The library half of bite 2 came first, from lane r002f78 658374a, a7239d8 and 7c3e8e0, tools main 7c3e8e06: `checks` carries the hook's eight hygiene predicates, the read-only table, the door's learned exact-match evaluation under the door's `door-rubric-refusal` name, the scope floor and the correction refusals, and `policy` the mode floors, transitions, citation and setting evidence and the gateway's three admission refusals, every function pure with a parity fixture feeding the caller's own inputs byte for byte; callers unchanged, census consumers `[]`; the record 038 bite row names the half; ships in deploy #54; the caller cutover is the later lane under the record's hold; the exported hygiene table and the UTF-8 branch are items ``entry:door:hygiene-rules-crate-private`` and ``entry:door:non-utf8-shell-bytes-undecided``.

(tools, implementation) extract the escalation process of the door, the hook and the gateway into `packages/execute-command` in the bites item ``entry:door:execute-command-design-record``'s record names, each bite gated and deployable on its own, the three callers reduced to their transports and entries, and the crate callable by a loop's own session and lanes for their command requests; the acceptance per bite is the record's, measured through the built binaries

Folded in, the runbook says estate referrals need estate judgement through the door channel: recorded as Bite 3 of item ``entry:door:execute-command-extraction-bites``.

(tools, runbook) `docs/integration.md:356` states that estate referrals require estate judgement through the door's channel, which matches the door behaviour record 038 preserves at its baseline but contradicts rungs 5 and 6 of ``tab:executecommand:ladder`` once bite 3 of item ``entry:door:execute-command-extraction-bites`` routes estate-scope referrals to operations and the owner; bite 3 rewrites the sentence with its route

**Open Problem (the compute run --bin value admits a dash-led value)** · `open:door:compute-run-bin-value-rule`

**OPEN.** The door compute grammar's `run --bin NAME` still takes the `-p` value rule and so admits a dash-led value, and the dash-led selector refusal is pinned only for `check --bin`; what remains is deciding whether `run --bin` keeps the historic `-p` exception or takes the `--package` bound and pinning that choice, and a test that loops over the three verbs, the two selectors and two dash-led values.

(tools, superloop-lane door compute) r002f89 round-1 observation outside the rows: the existing `run --bin NAME` parameter takes the `-p` value rule and so admits a dash-led value (``compute.rs:88-97``), predating the selector work; decide whether the historic `-p` exception extends to `run --bin` or whether that value takes the `--package` bound, and pin it

The selector pin is narrower than the code: the dash-led selector-name refusal in the door compute grammar is pinned only for `check --bin` (``compute_tests.rs:681-682``, round 2 of lane r002f89, non-blocking) while the code covers `--bin` and `--test` on check, clippy and test; a test that loops over the three verbs, the two selectors and two dash-led values catches a later narrowing of `strict_name`.

**Problem (The door's compute grammar refuses a test-name filter after the test target)** · `prob:door:door-compute-admits-test-name-filter`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index (lane hslim, 2026-10-25T02:03Z, mediation wall 0.109 s).

`cargo test -p PKG --all-features --locked --test NAME FILTER` is refused `door-compute-command-refusal` because `validate_words` (``packages/superloop-lane/src/door/compute.rs:84-131`` at 5fe287a3) admits options and option parameters only and requires an empty suffix for `test`. The fix admits one positional filter word after `--test NAME` for `cargo test` (and decides on `-- FILTER --exact`), limited to `[A-Za-z0-9_:]` bytes, with a compute-test twin for the refusal of a second positional word and a runbook sentence for the focused form.

**Problem (The door's compute grammar refuses `cargo test --no-run`, so a lane cannot compile the default-feature test targets before a battery)** · `prob:door:door-compute-admits-test-no-run`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index on 2026-10-30 in lane hprecguard and reported in its letter of 22:56:51Z; no change has landed.

The boxed compute route refuses `cargo test -p torrust-assayer --locked --no-run` as `door-compute-command-refusal`, exit 125 with a mediation wall of 0.066 s, so a lane cannot compile its crate's default-feature test targets before it asks for a battery. hprecguard's first battery caught a default-feature dead-code error that the lane's all-features Clippy could not see, a class that a test build which compiles the targets and runs nothing would catch at the lane's own cost and ahead of the battery. The command classes admit `--no-run` for `cargo test` under the judgement tiers of the test forms already admitted ``entry:door:door-compute-no-default-features`` and (`prob:door:door-compute-admits-test-name-filter`), with a compute fixture for the admitted form and the README grammar line restated; it is a rubric change on the capability door, not a loop step.

**Problem (A read form shows an admitted door request's judgement fields)** · `prob:door:requests-show-exposes-judgement-fields`

**OPEN.** Measured at deploy #57: the live-request evidence of torrust-index (354ed0ca…, kind door-after-effect, filed) showed no `decision_tier`, `would_do_source` or agent-call count.

`superloop-lane requests list LOOP` carries id, kind, state and age; `requests review LOOP ID` refuses a filed record (`requests-state`); the door journal logs only `door-agent-metadata`; and the record lives in the loop's private tier behind the helper. A read-only `requests show LOOP ID`, or list fields for admitted records, exposes `decision_tier`, `would_do_source`, the agent calls, the rubric digest and the walls, so a deploy's live request measures the fast lane without a review.

**Problem (The request-record conversion preview omits files that convert nothing)** · `prob:door:requests-rewrite-preview-omits-unchanged-files`

**OPEN.** Measured at deploy #58 by naturrhythmuszentrum (two retired door custody files, 26 records already at schema four).

`superloop-lane requests rewrite LOOP --dry-run` lists every custody file it scanned under `plan.files` with full before and after images even when the two are byte-equal and `changed` is 0, so a reader compares images to learn that nothing converts. The preview omits byte-equal files or carries a per-file `changed` flag; `predecessor_schema` and `target_schema` read as a finding although they are the fixed schema pair, so the preview labels them as such.

**Problem (Learned-hit door judgements take three times their wall after bite 6)** · `prob:door:learned-hit-judgement-wall-after-bite-6`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index's read-back at 2026-10-25T18:46Z, after deploy #59, and again around deploys #60 to #62 (below), reaching 37,238 ms after #62; the lane measurement and the fix remain.

Learned exact-match judgements through the restarted door showed `judgement_wall_ms` of 32,465 and 27,065 against about 10,350 before the deploy, and one `requests list` refused `requests-record-refusal: request lock: Resource temporarily unavailable` at 18:45:35Z. A learned hit makes no agent call, so the extra wall is lock time; the suspect is the door service's snapshot republication on every learned-index change under the store lock (the writers of ``dec:executecommand:direct-roots``), serialising behind the request store. The measurement runs in a lane with the public door exercise and the gateway fixture (judgement walls per tier before and after), and if it confirms the cause, the smallest fix (publish once per batch, or outside the request lock) ships as its own hotfix deploy.

Measured again by naturrhythmuszentrum at 2026-10-25T20:00Z: a deterministic fast-lane refusal through the restarted door judged in 322 ms with no learned-index change, against torrust-index's learned hits at 27 to 32 s, a contrast that supports the republication-on-learned-change suspicion. On both sides of deploy #60 torrust-index's learned exact-match judgements still took 25 to 30 s (`judgement_wall_ms` 25,239 to 25,451 at 2026-10-25T22:59Z before it, and 29,989 at 2026-10-26T00:56:53Z after it), so the wall stands. The lock refusal of the request listing beside these judgements is (`prob:door:requests-list-waits-for-request-lock`).

Measured again by torrust-index after deploy #62: the learned exact-match judgement wall grew to 37,238 ms (2026-10-26T04:54:18Z), against 28,291 ms at 02:57Z and 29,989 ms at 00:56Z, while the execution stays near 1.4 s, so the growth is in the judgement and not in the command.

Measured again by torrust-index on 2026-10-29 at 18:20Z, over its run hpinwalls since the deploy #70 door step: 24 decisions, every one of verdict tier `learned-exact-match` with judgement `allow` on the reason that the applicable adjudicated labels agree, each completed with rationale `door-complete`; `judgement_wall_ms` had a mean of 22,382, a minimum of 19,006 and a maximum of 29,768, against an `execution_wall_ms` median of 2,298 (most between 1,600 and 3,300, the two long ones, 18,578 and 44,707 ms, compute requests), so the judgement took 537 s in total against 490 s of execution and is the larger part of the door's wall for an exact learned match, and it held hpinwalls' single admission long enough for the run's next call to meet `door-request-concurrent` at 17:30Z. Readings of 19,780 to 26,160 ms first attributed to hrestorecap were hpinwalls decisions already among the 24, as the loop's correcting letter said, so the finding stands on the 24 alone; an example is decision `hpinwalls:18e311276694c8605` (judgement 25,883 ms, execution 44,707 ms), and the records hold `context_digest` and `request_id` for each. An exact match is a lookup against recorded labels and should take milliseconds, so a wall of 19 to 30 s that grows with the run reads as an agent call, or a scan or lock wait whose cost grows with the request store; the lane that takes it measures where the time goes against one named decision before any change, together with the lock refusals of (`prob:door:request-record-lock-bounded-wait-for-concurrent-runs`).

**Problem (A read-only request listing refuses while a lane request holds the request lock)** · `prob:door:requests-list-waits-for-request-lock`

**OPEN.** Part landed: deploy #76 at 2026-10-30T14:32:22Z gives the request store's readers the writers' 15 s lock bound, with contention retried within the caller's own deadline ``entry:door:request-lock-reader-deadline-against-sync-under-lock``, so `requests list` now makes one 15 s acquisition before it refuses with the existing text (below). What remains: a listing measured after #76 while a learned-hit judgement longer than the bound is the suspected holder, which shows whether the bound suffices or the refusal still has to name the retry.

Earlier status: OPEN — `superloop-lane requests list LOOP` refuses `requests-record-refusal: request lock: Resource temporarily unavailable (os error 11)` after about 2.0 s when a lane request holds the lock (torrust-index, 2026-10-26T00:56Z, after deploy #60; the listing succeeded on the fifth try), as it did at 2026-10-25T18:45:35Z in the read-back of (`prob:door:learned-hit-judgement-wall-after-bite-6`), whose long learned-hit judgements are the suspected holders. A read-only listing waits a bounded time for the lock or names the retry in its refusal, so a reader is not left to retry by hand while a door judgement runs.

Measured again by torrust-index on 2026-10-29: its `requests list torrust-index` met the same refusal at 17:31Z and at 19:57Z while a run's request was in flight; the door's refusal of a second run's request in the same hour is (`prob:door:request-record-lock-bounded-wait-for-concurrent-runs`).

Measured again at deploy #76 on 2026-10-30: lane r002g14 raised the request store's reader bound from 2 s to 15 s, the writers' bound, and its first review read `requests list`, `status` and `get` as one-shot session readers that now make one 15 s acquisition and then refuse with the existing text, so the listing waits a bounded time as this item asks ``entry:door:request-lock-reader-deadline-against-sync-under-lock``. What stays to measure is a listing while a learned-hit judgement of about 22 s, longer than the bound, is the suspected holder, which a loop's listing after #76 shows or rules out.

**Problem (A no-op git push through the door ends when the transport closes its side instead of meeting the command deadline)** · `prob:door:no-op-push-ends-when-the-transport-closes`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-03 13:07Z, from torrust-index) A no-op `git push` through the door (the mirror already holds the pushed commit at the named ref; e43f58e54 on lane/hhostrecords, three times 12:55-13:05Z) exits 1 with `door-socket: door command deadline` while the door journal logs `door connection: Broken pipe (os error 32)`; pushes carrying new commits from the same box succeed, and host-to-node ssh is healthy. The pattern fits the relay waiting on a frame the git transport never sends when there is no pack (the deadline paths in superloop-lane's ``door.rs``, frame_read_until and write_all_until against PEER_DEADLINE).

Evidence caveat: torrust-index runs the pre-#105 door service with the #105 superloop-lane, stub and door-ssh (its #105 door step is owed at its stop word); a reproduction after that step says whether the skew is involved. Fix: the relay ends the door command when the transport closes its side, since a no-op push is a complete exchange, with a test of a no-op push through the stub.

**Problem (Every change to the lane binary re-stamps the door's runner template and forces both door loops through a door step)** · `prob:door:runner-template-keyed-on-embedded-bytes`

**OPEN.** Every change to the `superloop-lane` binary changes the door's codex runner template (`libexec/ssh`) and `door-install-generation.json`, so each tools deploy that touches the lane command (#60, #61 and the parser hotfix #62) obliges both door loops to a stop-install-restart-repin cycle even when the door's own behaviour is unchanged (measured 2026-10-26: #61 and #62 each changed three door files with `door-service` unchanged). The template is keyed on the bytes it embeds rather than on the lane binary's digest, so a lane change that leaves the embedded helper identical reports `changed=0` and the door loops owe no step; the re-pin the step carries is ``entry:deploy:door-deploy-names-per-run-repin``.

Measured again on 2026-10-29: deploy #66 changed `libexec/door-ssh`, `libexec/door-service`, `libexec/vendor-proxy` and `libexec/hooks/superloop-claude-hook` beside the lane command, and both door loops ran its door step at their stop words, naturrhythmuszentrum at 09:04:04Z and torrust-index at 09:21:46Z, each `changed=4` with its settled runs re-pinned ``entry:waves:deploy-66-repin-interrupted-settlement-2026-10-29``; deploy #67 changed `libexec/door-service`, `libexec/door-ssh`, `libexec/vendor-proxy` and `superloop-lane`, and both door loops ran its door step at their stop words, torrust-index at 09:51:40Z (`changed=4`, hsparsewall re-pinned) and naturrhythmuszentrum at 09:51:44Z (`changed=4`, no run to re-pin) ``entry:waves:deploy-67-repair-follow-ups-retire-scan-and-repin-boundary-2026-10-29``. Both deploys changed the door service itself, so their steps are owed whatever the template's keying; the keying spares only the steps of a change to the lane command alone.

Measured again on 2026-10-30: deploys #72 and #73 shipped changes to the lane command's crate only, lane r002g10's routes reader and lane r002g11's sign-in home check and Claude preview, and each moved the digests of `libexec/door-ssh` and `libexec/vendor-proxy` beside `superloop-lane` with `door-service` unchanged (#72 at 06:24:19Z: door-ssh 7d7246ce8744d7f9 and vendor-proxy 963b6e18f6b95d89; #73 at 08:32:06Z: door-ssh f839c3c2670b9ec9 and vendor-proxy 5b2c1bb77bc01a67), so both door loops owed a door step at their stop words for programs whose sources neither lane touched, the third deploy in a row; #72's steps ran at torrust-index at 06:29:29Z in 4 s and at naturrhythmuszentrum at 07:05:06Z in 7 s, each changing 3 files and re-pinning one run. A digest comparison of the built programs cannot tell a behaviour change in a door program from a rebuild of the crate they are built from, so either the door programs' digests are compared on the blobs of their own sources, or the door-step rule is stated as it stands, a step owed whenever the crate changes; these are the steps the keying would spare.

**Problem (A second run's door request is refused while another run's request holds the request-store lock)** · `prob:door:request-record-lock-bounded-wait-for-concurrent-runs`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index on 2026-10-29 at 19:54Z; no fix has landed, and one lane takes it together with the judgement wall of (`prob:door:learned-hit-judgement-wall-after-bite-6`), measuring first where that wall goes.

With two boxed runs of one loop active, hpinwalls' round work4 from 19:43:45Z and hcadencepub's dispatch from 19:51:37Z, hcadencepub's first door call, a push to its own mirror, was answered `door-request-refusal`, the door unit's journal reading at 19:54:31Z `door connection: requests-record-refusal: request lock: Resource temporarily unavailable (os error 11)`. The loop reads the record lock as refused when busy; the store's documented acquisition waits are 2 s for a reader and 15 s for a writer (`prob:gates:door-fixtures-request-lock-reader-bound`), both shorter than a learned exact-match judgement of about 22 s, so a busy lock is the ordinary state whenever one run is active, every other run's calls lose the race, and the refusal reads as a route failure: the worker stopped and asked, and the loop told it to treat the refusal as one of capacity, wait 60 s and retry once. The lane measures which wait the door's record path takes, and the record lock takes a wait bounded above the judgement it serves, as `requests wait` already retries lock contention inside its deadline, with a refusal after the bound that names the capacity; the same refusal on the request lock is among the load-shaped gate reds of ``prob:gates:gate-cpu-reservation-on-shared-node``, and the read-only listing's refusal is (`prob:door:requests-list-waits-for-request-lock`).

**Problem (The door holds a lane's admission for the whole of a long compute request, so the lane's git push waits behind it)** · `prob:door:push-admitted-beside-a-running-compute-request`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index on 2026-10-31 at about 10:16Z and reported in its letter of 10:26Z; no change has landed.

The boxed lane hspectral sent `cargo run … torrust-assayer-research -- check --all` through the door, a request of ten to twelve minutes under load and inside its 1,762 s bound, and while it ran every other request of the lane was refused `door-request-concurrent`, a `git push` of new commits to the lane's mirror among them, so prepared code could not ship for about ten minutes. The lane asked its loop to cancel the request; cancellation is the door's operation and not the loop's, and the loop did not work around it. The door holds one active request per box from admission through the final reply ``entry:door:door-request-concurrent-admission``, and a push neither reads nor writes the checkout that a compute request uses, only the lane's mirror, so holding the push behind the request serializes two operations that share no state. A tools lane weighs two answers together: admitting a `git push` beside a running compute request, the smaller change, since the push acts on the mirror alone, or a supported way for a lane to cancel its own request in flight. The neighbouring findings on the same admission are ``prob:door:admission-held-past-result-line`` and (`prob:door:request-record-lock-bounded-wait-for-concurrent-runs`).

**Problem (The door exercise's attribution probe budgets its calls so the admission wait fits)** · `prob:door:attribution-probe-budget-holds-the-admission-wait`

**OPEN.** Measured against tools 486558ce on 2026-11-03: lane r002g74's f98bba9d (let box probes observe admission refusals) raised the door-shape probe and the network probe to one 35-second call each, but the attribution probe still caps each of its calls at 40 seconds with no total budget inside the 60-second box run, so the follow-up below, a total budget with each call capped at the smaller of 35 seconds and the remainder less a margin, stands. The earlier status follows.

Earlier status: OPEN — r002g74 confirmation F7 (tools, door exercise): the attribution probe's 60 s box budget cannot hold a 30 s door admission wait on more than one of its seven calls (introduced by the bounded wait, c01); a follow-up gives the probe a total budget with each call capped at min(35 s, remaining minus a margin) and maps TimeoutExpired to the named concurrent-unmeasured reason, or raises the box run and the watch deadline together.

### Lanes and their lifecycle · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-lanes`

**Problem (The interrupted settlement's dead ends and the sweep's open rows)** · `prob:lanes:interrupted-settlement-dead-ends-and-open-rows`

**OPEN.** 2026-10-15, tools items recorded from a pilot loop's day and from the reviews of item ``entry:waves:twenty-lanes-between-the-door-and-the-budget-rule``, each with its lines at df262b6. A boxed run whose launcher died at the budget (item ``entry:lanes:budget-expiry-notifies-never-kills``'s cause) while its container worked on has no route through the record once the container has exited: the recovery launch's reaping finds the run's work scope already collected by systemd and `journal_interrupted_reaping` (``src/launch.rs:2583-2600``) accepts a collected measurement only with a prior verified-empty `reaping` field, which a launcher killed before its settlement never wrote, so the run goes to phase `failed`; `settle --round` then refuses on the absent field (``src/launch/settle.rs:208``), `checkpoint` and `checkout` need phase `settled` (``src/launch/checkpoint.rs:1551``), `send-letter` refuses the interrupted state so the working worker cannot be answered, `install-door`'s busy list names only runs with a live launcher (`busy_holder`, ``src/install_door.rs:1162``) so such a container's door call can be cut by a restart timed on that list, and `retire` refuses the failed run for its recorded settlement uncertainty (``src/launch/retire.rs:170-178``) with no form that clears it, so the run stays in the loop's count for good. The loop recovered the work by the data route (a bundle read of its own box home into a quarantined ref, text review, cherry-pick) and holds the run. Fix candidates, one lane after item ``entry:lanes:budget-expiry-notifies-never-kills`` lands: the recovery accepting a collected scope as the measurement when the container is absent and the launcher identity dead, or `settle --round` measuring afresh when the record has none; an owner-confirmed retirement that keeps the uncertainty in the retired record; the busy detail naming container-alive runs; a letter route or a `--letter` on the recovery launch for an orphaned working worker, decided with the recovery fix. The other open rows of the sweep: the settle set-aside form for an occupied report name; `follow`'s documentation of `start`; the door's `-F` reopen by path; retirement's missing check for waiting requests and pending corrections; the socat heartbeat noise in the box's logs; the `run-worker` budget timer under item ``entry:lanes:budget-expiry-notifies-never-kills``'s rule; the two timing-bound fixtures that still fail under gate load; the generic repository's log-eater wording; and a pilot loop's door note. Each becomes a lane in the order its risk warrants.

**Problem (A budget-killed first round without a reply has no resume route)** · `prob:lanes:killed-first-round-resume-route`

**OPEN.** A failed first round whose driver was killed by budget or suspend, with no reply of record, has neither a resume route nor a record route to the worker's work (run solving-t, 2026-10-15; rechecked, still refused on the collected scope); the three fixes below and the two records in the last paragraph are open.

After such a kill the recovery is the first-round launch form on the started reservation (`would-complete-interrupted-settlement`, then the completed report with the round `failed`); then `launch --round STEP` refuses (``round-state: run has no settled round to resume``) and `settle --round` refuses without a reply of record. The driver's signal 9 reaches only the host-side process holding codex's stdout, so `codex exec` and `codex-code-mode-host` keep running in the container with no reader, and the rollout under the lane's tmp home, not the driver's events.jsonl, is the live record a monitor or recovery could read. A launcher killed before settlement never measured its work scope, which systemd collects once its last process ends, so the recovery's reaping (`journal_interrupted_reaping`, ``launch.rs:2583-2600``: a `collected` measurement needs a prior verified-empty `reaping` field and an absent payload) refuses `run-reaping: collected scope lacks prior verified-empty measurement or absent payload` and fails the run; `settle --round` then refuses on `reaping_verdict` (``launch/settle.rs:208``), and `checkpoint` and `checkout` need phase `settled` (``launch/checkpoint.rs:1551``).

Fixes (code, design read-back, fixtures through the production entry): resume such a round as the same step under a new `--budget` on the worker's thread and home (the SUSPEND-KILLED shape of the host lanes), recorded as a new round row; make the kill reach the container's worker (stop the attempt through the helper, or signal the exec by the recorded container id) and record it; and let the recovery accept a collected scope when the container is absent and the launcher's identity is dead (the collection is itself the measurement), or let `settle --round` take a fresh scope measurement when the record has none.

Found 2026-10-15 (naturrhythmuszentrum, run solving-t): launched without --budget, so the default 3,600 s applied against a letter promising 10,800 s; the driver ended STALLED-KILLED exit 1 at 3,876 s, last event about two minutes earlier; stderr `helper-stop: .control-solving-t.sock: Connection refused`; the launch was a harness background Bash task. At 15:25Z `podman top` showed `codex exec` (pid 2, 1 h 13 m elapsed) still alive; at 15:27Z the rollout showed a second commit at 15:15:34Z and third-commit files at 15:25:28Z. At 16:50Z, after the worker finished and nrz removed the exited container, the recovery found the scope collected and the run failed; the worker's three commits and reply are read as data from the loop's own worker home (its own podman unshare, quarantined ref, text review, cherry-pick).

Still to record: the driver's verdict line and last event instants (the budget is a reporting threshold and a growing region is credited to four budgets, so STALLED at 3,876 s is checked against what the driver measured), and whether the harness-owned launch process caused the helper-stop refusal.

**Open Problem (A run in the interrupted-settlement state cannot be answered by letter)** · `open:lanes:interrupted-settlement-letter-route`

**OPEN.** `send-letter` still refuses a run in the interrupted-settlement state, so an orphaned but working worker cannot be answered by letter; the design question remains, to be decided with the recovery route of (`prob:lanes:killed-first-round-resume-route`): whether such a letter is a resume of the round or the recovery launch accepts `--letter` so the recovered round carries the answer.

In the interrupted-settlement state (a `started` reservation whose launcher is gone) `send-letter LOOP RUN --step STEP --letter FILE` refuses with `interrupted-settlement: started run awaits launch recovery and reply readback` (nrz 2026-10-15), so an orphaned-but-working worker cannot be answered by letter even though its inbox and container live; nrz's solving-t worker asked for authorization of a prepared patch and got no route. Design question, not a fix: whether a letter to a run in that state is a resume of the round (the reply readback and the round record move to the recovery launch, so the letter would belong to a round nobody follows) or whether the recovery launch should accept `--letter` so the recovered round carries the answer. Decide with item (`prob:lanes:killed-first-round-resume-route`)'s recovery route; after item ``entry:lanes:budget-expiry-notifies-never-kills`` the state arises only from a real launcher death.

**Problem (No fixture carries a resume past the door re-pin through the production entry)** · `prob:lanes:repin-door-resume-end-to-end`

**OPEN.** No fixture yet carries a resume past the door re-pin through the production entry, since the retained fixture stops at the helper accepting the new pin and the box probe's digest comparison runs only on a runtime path neither node can run; what remains is the first end-to-end measurement on the pilot loop's stranded run (the dry run, the apply, its resume and a door-shape exercise comparing the in-box stub digest with the new pin), with the walls and the exercise verdict recorded here.

TOOLS (r002d83 round one, next step): no fixture carries a resume past the door re-pin through the production entry, dry run or changing; the retained fixture stops at the helper accepting the new pin and the resume vector (``repin_door_tests.rs:230-248``), and the box probe's digest comparison against the re-pinned record (``exercise_door.rs:295-297``, :379) is reached only on the runtime path, which neither node can run (no container runtime). The first end-to-end measurement is the pilot loop's stranded run: `launch LOOP RUN --repin-door --dry-run`, the apply, its resume, then a door-shape exercise comparing the in-box stub digest with the new pin; record the walls and the exercise verdict on the backlog entry.

**Problem (The re-pin opens the reservation three times by path with no device and inode identity)** · `prob:lanes:repin-reservation-descriptor-identity`

**OPEN.** Lane B deferred it to lane C, noting that ``launch.rs:1049`` already opens the reservation data by descriptor; the re-pin still records no device and inode identity. The recorded status note ends mid-word in the source table ("opens reservation dat"), so its rest is lost.

TOOLS (r002d83 round one, LOW, reaches no one): the re-pin opens the reservation three times by a fresh path walk under the held runs-root descriptor (read_reservation ``launch.rs:1033-1085``, read_private ``repin_door.rs:321`` and :398, then a temporary file and rename), comparing type, owner and mode on each open and then the parsed value and exact bytes, with no device or inode identity recorded; this is retire's existing route (``retire.rs:198-221``) under the owner's private, owner-checked runs root. A later change can anchor on the held descriptor with the bound reader's device:inode:digest identity (``checkout.rs:209-253``).

**Problem (The runtime-file fixtures use one uid and no test calls the dispatch-side writer)** · `prob:lanes:runtime-fixtures-two-uids-and-dispatch-writer`

**OPEN.** Lane E landed the two-row negative at 9daa3817 and added a distinct owner and loop identity fixture, which stays ignored because it requires a provisioned namespace; what remains is that fixture run where a namespace exists and a test of the dispatch-side writer `private_prepare_runtime`, whose full coverage was deferred.

r002e05 reviewer remark: test A's fixture uses one uid for owner, loop group and run-directory owner, so the new uid/gid checks (``launch.rs:5601-5604``, `:5510`) are not told apart; and no test calls `private_prepare_runtime` (the dispatch-side writer; ``main.rs:1434`` its only caller, stubbed in ``src/tests/launch.rs:1615``); and `threadless_continuation` has no two-row negative (recovered dispatch then a non-recovered work-1). Fixtures with a supplementary-group gid or a privileged fixture; a dispatch-side writer test; one two-row negative.

**Problem (a run recovered by-report after a door pin change admits no next round)** · `prob:lanes:by-report-recovery-no-next-round`

**OPEN.** Partly landed in lane B; the legacy failed-plan re-pin is deferred to lane C. Open: the recovery's message and the runbook paragraph name the route left after a by-report settlement (the new round, not settle); a failed run's plan is repinnable or the new round's launch carries the current pin; a failed first round admits a resume letter on the same thread and home; and a helper-start failure settles the attempt as failed-at-start with its absence proof, so the round can be relaunched or the run retired.

After a launch-form recovery settles both rounds by-report, the message and runbook direct to `settle LOOP RUN --round STEP --dry-run`, which refuses ("selected round is not failed" / "run is not failed in the selected round"); the run reads as failed with no final round, and after a door pin change every onward route refuses: send-letter "run is neither live nor settled", launch --round "protected plan differs from the current pinned plan; differing keys: door" (pointing at repin), repin-door "run must be settled and not rotated", rotate "old run has a live or failed round", checkpoint "run has no settled final round". Only retire previews, so the worker's thread is lost (the pushed work survives on the mirror ref). A vendor refusal is a retryable outcome of a round, not a dead run, and with fifteen sessions on one vendor account it recurs.

Measured on torrust-index lane hpinroute, 2026-10-21, the refusal set of the new-round route at 18:40Z. On lane hguidance (19:31Z) a work-first dispatch whose turn ended on the vendor's capacity refusal (`turn.failed`, driver RUN-REPORT exit=1 wall=428s FAILED, nothing interrupted on the host) landed in the same dead state (phase failed, empty rounds, every identity absent): `settle --round dispatch` (completion not recorded), `send-letter`, `launch --round dispatch` and the original launch form (already exists) all refused; only retire previewed.

On lane hcomphost (19:47Z to 20:03Z, across deploy #33) a resume whose helper never came up (`helper-start: .container-hcomphost.sock did not become ready wall_seconds=31.14` with fourteen boxes on the host) left the run in phase `preparing` with every route refusing: `launch --round` (settlement-identity: driver identity is absent), the original launch form (launch-reservation: an earlier container attempt may have started; the run is retained), `retire --dry-run` (retire-uncertain: no recorded identity or absence proof); the run keeps its worker uid and runs-root entry until a route exists.

**Problem (the Claude lane's finished-step state record carries a null driver and monitor)** · `prob:lanes:claude-lane-finished-state-null-driver`

**OPEN.** Lane D deferred it because retaining the finished-step state needs shared lifecycle changes, so a finished Claude step's `state` record still reads `driver: null, monitor: null`; what remains is the driver keeping the finished step's driver and monitor objects (alive false), with the monitor's reader test on the new driver's real output, and the runbook glossary gaining `rate_limit_event`.

`claude-lane` after the runs-root bite (siedelweber, measured on its first Claude step after deploy #33, run 20261021T182519Z-review-foundation round-3, with the unchanged worker-monitor): during the step the run directory is as before, but the capture's `state` record of the finished step reads `driver: null, monitor: null` where the old driver's finished steps carried `driver: {lock, pid, child, alive: false}` and the populated monitor object; the finished record is served, so the duty completes, but a watcher reading liveness from `state.driver.alive` reads nothing for a finished step. Beside it a new Claude event type `rate_limit_event` appeared in a delta, unseen before. Proper fix: the driver keeps the finished step's driver and monitor objects (alive false) in the state record, with the monitor's reader test on the new driver's real output; the runbook glossary gains `rate_limit_event`.

**Problem (a round whose turn ends without its reply of record is not resumed once by the driver)** · `prob:lanes:reply-of-record-one-resume-guard`

**OPEN.** The charter line landed (docs/worker-charter.md:47, lane A); no driver guard found in tools main 3c0f1a13.

Earlier status: DONE — Landed by lane A.

a round ends on an interim status sentence with no reply of record (torrust-index, 2026-10-21T22:44:04Z): run hpinroute2, dispatch round, RUN-REPORT exit=1 wall=14004s budget=7200s verdict=FAILED at 22:41Z; the worker's last agent message was a status note ("the second blend perturbation request has not finished yet; its 600-second worker bound remains in force") with no tool call after it, outbox/dispatch.md absent (read-reply-absent), nine commits on the mirror, the run in the failed phase so retire was the only route (item (`prob:lanes:by-report-recovery-no-next-round`)) and a successor had to re-establish progress from git history; cost a 3.9-hour round with no report. Two fixes, both in the tools repository: a worker-charter line that a turn ends only with the reply of record and that a long request is waited on with commands, never narrated; and a driver guard in codex-lane that, when a round's agent turn ends without the outbox reply and inside its budget, resumes the thread once with a pinned instruction to wait on the running request and write the reply (one resume, recorded in the run report), so no loop pays the round again. Corrected 2026-10-22 by the same loop: the round in question ended mid-tool-call at the identical 14,004 s wall of another run, so the ending was a hard bound outside the worker (item ``entry:box:box-attempt-wall-follows-plan``), not a status sentence; the charter line and the one-resume guard stand on their own merits.

**Problem (a boxed dispatch round failing after its driver started has no exit the refusals name)** · `prob:lanes:dispatch-runtime-failure-recovery-route`

**OPEN.** The hotfix from lane r002f19 is deployed as #36 with two production proofs, and the capacity retry landed as item ``entry:lanes:capacity-retry-sized-to-the-outage`` (f4c60bc4, deploy #44), covering the capacity class. Open: the driver recording helper and driver completion on a runtime failure so `settle` applies directly, the `round-state` and `settle-live-refusal` messages naming the dispatch-round recovery form, and the review's next steps (a) to (c) below.

A vendor capacity stop (`Selected model is at capacity`, a type=error event then turn.failed) makes the driver exit 1 `runtime_failure` (socat broken pipe, `write_stdin failed`, `failed to record rollout items`) without recording helper and driver completion, so `settle --dry-run` refuses `settle-live-refusal`, `launch --round dispatch` refuses `round-state` and `send-letter` refuses `letter-run-state`. The route that applies is the runbook's interrupted-settlement form for a dead dispatch (the original launch form retried after absence is proved, then settle, then a fresh thread by send-letter and launch --round), which none of the three messages names. Before the hotfix it was unreachable: ``launch.rs:1239-1261`` at 6e148e6 admitted an existing run only in phase preparing or in phase failed with a pre_driver_failure string, and ``settle.rs:218-226`` refuses without that absence proof or recorded driver_end/helper_end completion, so a round failing after its driver started had no exit in the tool. The hotfix admits a failed-phase run whose driver ran and whose completion went unrecorded into the same measured-absence recovery, after which settle applies.

Found 2026-10-22T01:03:09Z (naturrhythmuszentrum letter 01792631140393952966, run cas-sorts-s3, 4,765 s into a 10,800 s budget; the answer carries the four-step form); at 2026-10-22T01:10Z (letter 01792631350444750103), with every process absent and settlement_uncertain=false, the original launch form refused `already-exists`. Lane r002f19 was reviewed IMPORTABLE at 2026-10-22T02:25Z (candidate 0f8b9ea6 over 6e148e6: a narrow eligibility predicate with a unit table, the existing interrupted-settlement route, refusals kept by name, six new tests, one runbook sentence) and deployed as #36 at 2026-10-22T03:41:40Z (tools eeed96e, superloop-lane sha16 b761683f1a6ebe20) at the last live clear word.

Production proofs at 2026-10-22T03:43Z: on torrust-index hpinroute3 the original launch form's dry run previewed `interrupted settlement starts no new container`, the act returned `completed-interrupted-settlement` in 1.8 s (attempt 1 status 1, error_free true, ended_by by-report, phase failed), settle finalized to phase settled, and send-letter --step work-2 then launch --round work-2 started a fresh thread in the retained home; on naturrhythmuszentrum cas-sorts-s3 (03:43Z) the same form and settle reached phase settled, then retire (its work had landed through cas-sorts-s3b). Neither met a refusal.

Open review steps: (a) ``launch.rs:2551-2557`` with `:2890-2894` and ``launch/report.rs:157-161``: after a completed recovery the record stays `failed_driver_recovery=true`, so a second original-launch pass re-previews before refusing `launch-report: report already exists` (safe, but the preview promises more than the act delivers); it should refuse by name once this run and round's completed-interrupted-settlement report exists or the recovery proof passes, with a built-binary second-act test; (b) `docs/integration.md:15` says neither end recorded where the code admits either end not completed; (c) ``tests/failed_dispatch_entry.rs:226-320``, the end-to-end built-binary sequence, needs a node granting user namespaces with a running user service manager (item ``entry:door:requests-two-uid-ownership-proof``'s pod route).

Measured again on 2026-10-30 from 06:05Z to 06:12Z, after the host shutdown of 2026-10-29 at 20:36:08Z: both door loops recovered their cut boxed runs by the interrupted-settlement route that deploys #69 and #70 established, with no step of this loop. naturrhythmuszentrum's lattice-live-1 (container exit 143) settled by `launch LOOP RUN --letter FILE --dry-run` and then `--expect-digest` of `interrupted_settlement.preview_sha256`, in 3.2 s with `absence-confirmed-with-missing-evidence` and the container and both helper sockets removed, then by `settle --round dispatch` through dry run and digest, and resumed at 06:12Z. torrust-index's hcadencepub was re-pinned at the loop's #71 door step (`boundary interrupted-settlement`, `container_presence proved-exited`, exit 143, only `stub_sha256` changing, from 2e6261e8 to ce2ba761); `launch --round dispatch` then refused `round-state` and named the dispatch form, the `--letter` launch's apply reported `completed-interrupted-settlement` with the phase `failed` in 3.2 s, settle archived the failed report, and round work2 launched at 06:09:44Z. The route held on both runs, and its one friction is this item's open message: the `round-state` refusal of `--round dispatch` costs the loop a second preview, where a refusal naming the `--letter` recovery form outright would send it there at once.

**Problem (codex-lane's directory diagnostic re-parses argv and can blame the wrong option)** · `prob:lanes:codex-lane-typed-directory-refusal`

**OPEN.** codex-lane's `directory_diagnostic` still re-parses argv, so with two faults it can blame the wrong option; what remains is the driver-family fix in which `parse_arguments` returns a typed directory refusal as `RootRefusal` does and codex-lane prints it.

codex-lane's `directory_diagnostic` re-parses argv instead of carrying the refusal from `parse_arguments` (r002f25 review N7, 2026-10-22): with two faults it can blame the wrong option (`-t 0 -r S -d existing-relative prompt` rejects `-t` but names `-d`, with an "absolute" rule the parser does not enforce). Fix (driver family, `lane-lifecycle`): `parse_arguments` returns a typed directory refusal as `RootRefusal` does, and codex-lane prints it; beside item (`prob:lanes:dispatch-runtime-failure-recovery-route`)'s capacity retry (one retry after a fixed 2 s wait bounded by the remaining round budget, lane-lifecycle ``lib.rs:1916``, constants `:105-106`).

**Problem (a running host Codex step has no route for a session letter or a named stop)** · `prob:lanes:host-step-letter-or-named-stop`

**OPEN.** A running host Codex step still has no route for a session letter, so a correction costs a SIGTERM that the step records as FAILED with no visible cause; what remains is a mid-step letter route for host steps or at least a documented session-initiated stop recorded as `stopped-by-session` with its reason, and the runbook's dispatch section naming the route.

a running host Codex lane step has no route for a letter from the session (blueprint letter 01792665689854755097, lane wall-dual, 2026-10-22T10:41Z): in-round letters exist only for boxed rounds (`superloop-lane send-letter` then `launch --round`), codex-lane exposes no letter or inbox option, so a one-paragraph correction two minutes into a step cost a SIGTERM on the verified pid (the step recorded verdict=FAILED exit=1 with no visible cause, relayed by the watcher as a driver failure), a resumed step with the correction, a second step name and a watcher condition edit. Fix (driver family): a mid-step letter route for host steps (an inbox file the worker reads between tool calls, acknowledged like the boxed STEP-N.md pattern) or, at least, a documented session-initiated stop (`codex-lane stop RUN --reason TEXT`) that records the end as `stopped-by-session` with the reason rather than FAILED, so the watcher and the record name the cause; the runbook's dispatch section names the route.

**Claim (a boxed Codex round re-logged one orphaned custom tool call output at every turn)** · `claim:lanes:codex-orphan-tool-call-output-measurement`

**OPEN.** One boxed Codex round re-logged a single orphaned custom tool call output at every turn without stopping, and the measurement comes first: a fixture showing what the Codex app-server does with an orphaned output and what context it costs, and whether the driver or the box's tool wiring can produce one, before it is bounded or dropped by name.

a boxed Codex round's stderr carried 457 lines of `ERROR codex_core::util: Orphan custom tool call output for call id: call_48fec…` with one call id, in bursts at every turn from 02:49:34Z to 04:01:19Z (naturrhythmuszentrum coordinate-lowering-1, letter 01792728519740361396, 2026-10-23); the sibling run cas-jacobians-1 had none; the round did not stop on it, but one tool output re-sent on every turn may inflate the context. Fix: measure in a fixture what the Codex app-server does with an orphaned custom tool call output (whether it is re-sent, how much context it costs), and whether the driver or the box's tool wiring can produce an orphan (a tool call whose result arrives after the turn that issued it ended); bound or drop it by name. Where: codex-lane driver, box tool wiring; measurement first.

**Problem (two concurrent launches of one loop race for the worker uid)** · `prob:lanes:concurrent-launch-worker-uid-race`

**OPEN.** Two concurrent launches of one loop still race for the worker uid; the second refuses cleanly with `uid-race` and leaves nothing behind, so the loop serialises its launches meanwhile, and what remains is the launcher holding the allocation lock across uid selection and home preparation, or reselecting once by itself, with a fixture of two launches racing for one identity.

two concurrent `superloop-lane launch` calls of one loop race for the worker uid: the second, started 18.6 s after the first (naturrhythmuszentrum, coordinate-lowering-2 at 2026-10-23T04:34:07Z and cas-sign-transport-1 at 04:34:25Z, binary of deploy #43), refused cleanly before creating anything with `prepare-home: … uid-race: worker UID 3079 is no longer the first available identity; dry-run again` (wall 4.1 s), because the first launch took the uid between the second's selection and its home preparation; a relaunch a minute later succeeded and nothing was left behind (letter 01792730134328987327). The refusal is safe and named, so the loop serialises its launches meanwhile. Fix: the launcher holds the allocation lock across uid selection and home preparation, or reselects once by itself on `uid-race` (preview and act agreeing), with a fixture of two launches racing for one identity. Where: packages/superloop-lane launch (uid table and `prepare-home`); with item ``entry:lanes:retire-never-started-preparation``.

**Problem (the settle-route lane leaves an unexercised identity check and an unnamed final-round admission)** · `prob:lanes:settle-route-review-follow-ups`

**OPEN.** The settle-route lane's review left two follow-ups fit for the next step, both open: (a) the report case drives no `rounds::check_identity` over a settled run whose last round failed with a retained thread, and (b) `final_round_ready` admits a dead resumed round settled with a null reply that no test drives, which needs a fixture and a sentence naming it or an admission narrowed to the named shape.

follow-ups of the settle-route lane (r002f51, round 1 of 2026-10-23, fit for the next step): (a) the same-uid fixture in ``launch/settle.rs`` calls the owner-parameterized letter core directly and so skips the binary's `send-letter` transport and its namespace-root gate; the ignored mapped-uid variants in ``tests/failed_dispatch_entry.rs`` cover that route once item ``prob:servers:node-namespace-precondition-for-door-confinement``'s namespace exists, and until then the fixture's launch step drives no `rounds::check_identity(&plan, &saved)` over a settled run whose last round failed with a retained thread, which the work case does; add that call in the report case; (b) ``rounds.rs:141``: `final_round_ready` now also admits a dead resumed round that settled with a null reply, a behaviour outside item ``entry:lanes:settle-failed-round-missing-readback-any-kind``'s named shape that no test in the series drives; either a fixture that drives it and a sentence that names it, or the admission narrowed to the row's shape. Where: packages/superloop-lane ``launch/settle.rs``, ``launch/rounds.rs``, ``tests/failed_dispatch_entry.rs``; with items ``entry:lanes:settle-failed-round-missing-readback-any-kind`` and ``entry:servers:node-namespace-precondition-for-door-confinement``.

**Problem (the Codex driver leaves a run's final step without a report file)** · `prob:lanes:final-step-report-file-written-at-step-end`

**OPEN.** The Codex lane driver still folds a step's `last-message.txt`, events and feed offset into `steps/<name>/` only when the next step starts, so a run's final step has no report file; what remains is the driver writing `steps/<name>/last-message.txt` when the step ends.

(tools, superloop-lane driver) with the per-step run layout the Codex lane driver folds a step's `last-message.txt`, events and feed offset into `steps/<name>/` only when the next step starts, so the final step of a run has no report file (run 20261024T030721Z after its fourth step: `steps/dispatch`, `steps/work` and `steps/fix1` present, the finished `fix2` report only in `events.jsonl`); the driver writes `steps/<name>/last-message.txt` when the step ends, so a report is a file the moment its RUN-REPORT line prints

**Problem (the driver reports a vendor usage-limit stop as an ordinary failure)** · `prob:lanes:driver-usage-limit-outcome-kind`
