**OPEN.** The driver still reports a vendor usage-limit stop, and a turn cut by a host shutdown, as `verdict=FAILED`, indistinguishable from a work failure; what remains is a distinct outcome kind for a usage-limit `turn.failed`, with the vendor's next-window instant parsed into the record and the monitor serving it as its own record, and one for a turn cut without `turn.completed` and without a vendor error (measured on 2026-10-30, below).

(tools, lane driver) wave 12, 2026-10-24T20:05Z: three Codex lanes died mid-step on the vendor's usage-limit refusal and the driver reported `verdict=FAILED` with a progress note as `last_message`, indistinguishable in the run report from a work failure; the driver names a vendor usage-limit `turn.failed` as its own outcome kind (for example `verdict=usage-limit next_window=<instant>`) with the vendor's instant parsed into the record, so the orchestrator and the watcher read a resumable suspension rather than a failure, and the monitor serves it as a distinct record

Measured again on 2026-10-29 at the plan's limit of 18:51Z ``dec:estate:codex-usage-limit-suspension-form``: lane r002g09's work step ended `exit=1 verdict=FAILED` after 1,608 s, and torrust-index's boxed round work3 of hpinwalls ended `exit=1 wall=5248s budget=7200s verdict=FAILED` with no provider text readable inside the box, where the vendor socket read `Connection reset by peer` at 18:50:06Z and Codex logged `failed to record rollout items: thread … not found` at 18:51:33Z; the loop took the cause from the host side, where the vendor unit's two streams, 1,641 s and 5,184 s old, closed at 18:51:33Z, and from the host-side driver log, which alone carries the provider's text. A boxed lane's cut therefore shows inside the box as a socket reset, and the usage-limit outcome kind reaches the box's run report as well as the host driver's, so that a boxed loop reads a suspension where it reads its run.

Measured again on 2026-10-30 at 06:03Z, reading a cut of the evening before: lane r002g10's work step (run 20261029T201406Z-dispatch, launched at 20:16:41Z with a 4,500 s budget) ended at 20:36:08Z after 1,166 s with `verdict=FAILED exit=1`, a progress note as its last message, its second turn without a `turn.completed` event and no vendor text in the event stream. The cause was a host shutdown, not the vendor: at 20:36:08Z the user session stopped every lane unit at once, both door loops' door and vendor units, the launch services and the boxes, cutting naturrhythmuszentrum's lattice-live-1 and torrust-index's hcadencepub in the same second (hcadencepub's box `Exited (143)`), as torrust-index's letter of 06:10Z read from the journal. In the driver's own files a shutdown reads exactly as a vendor cut and as a work failure, and the distinguishing evidence was the journal and a peer's letter. So the outcome kinds this item asks for include one for a turn cut without `turn.completed` and without a vendor error, distinct from a usage-limit stop and from a work failure, so that the orchestrator reads a resumable cut from the run report alone. The worker had pushed its one commit 9b2fd577 at 20:19Z and measured three node rounds on it, so the lane continued from the pin without a resume ``entry:homes:routes-reader-validates-selected-loop-rows-strictly``.

**Problem (A change to the batch partition rule refuses every journal interrupted under the old rule)** · `prob:lanes:conversion-partition-change-resume-path`

**PARKED.** Deferred by lane B to any future lane that changes the partition rule or the bound.

Earlier status: OPEN — Lane B deferred it: Deferred to any future partition-changing lane. This lane changes.

``launch/retire_node_rewrite.rs:396-405`` (f05 round one): the batch partition is recomputed at resume and must equal the journal on disk byte for byte, so a later change to `batches()` or to `MAX_RESERVATION_BYTES` between an interruption and its resume refuses "journal batch differs from whole plan" with no way forward; any commit that changes the partition rule or the bound carries a resume path for journals written under the old rule, named in its message.

**Record (A retirement test once saw the report rewrite change nothing where one change is expected)** · `rec:lanes:retired-report-rewrite-inode-test-red-once`

**OPEN.** Measured once: gate r002f91 red 2 on 2ae3a287 at 2026-10-24T22:42Z, green on the retry at 22:54Z, with no series touching ``launch/report.rs``.

`retirement_after_report_rewrite_keeps_both_inode_generations` (``packages/superloop-lane/tests/census_entry.rs:658``) saw `rewrite-launch-reports` report `changed` 0 where 1 is expected, under node load. The test models allocator inode reuse by renaming the retired report to a name encoding the active file's device and inode, so a cause in the rewrite's selection of a `.N`-suffixed retained report (by mtime, inode or listing order) is to be measured before the test or the tool is changed.

**Problem (The Codex lane driver's report line carries the failure reason of a failed turn)** · `prob:lanes:codex-lane-report-carries-failure-reason`

**OPEN.** Measured by tripod on 2026-10-25; siedelweber met the same classifier on 2026-10-24.

The driver's RUN-REPORT line states exit and verdict but not the cause. When the lane's event stream ends in a `turn.failed` row (a provider content classifier, a usage limit), the report line carries `reason=` filled from that row, so a watcher reading only the report hands back the cause with the failure; the usage-limit outcome kind of (`prob:lanes:driver-usage-limit-outcome-kind`) is one value of that field.

**Problem (The bound-roots check compares the recorded roots as spelled, so a root recorded through a symlink would miss a canonical path)** · `prob:lanes:bound-roots-canonical-recorded-roots`

**OPEN.** Noted by the second review of lane r002g19 on 2026-10-30 (its N1) and measured as unexposed the same day: every root in the 92 bound-roots records under this loop's runs root is spelled in its canonical form, under the real path of the host's home; no change has landed.

`superloop-lane bound-roots` canonicalises the requested PATH and refuses one whose spelling differs from its canonical form (`bound-roots-path`, status 2), but it compares each recorded root as the record spells it (``packages/superloop-lane/src/launch/bound_roots.rs`` lines 144 and 145 at the lane's c02 534ff4db). A `writable_roots` entry written through a symlink, `/home/…` on this host where `/home` links to `/var/home`, would be recorded in that spelling, would match no canonical PATH, and the scan would answer `clear` for a clone that a live sandbox binds, the failure the command exists to prevent ``entry:lanes:driver-records-bound-writable-roots``. The scan canonicalises each recorded root that still resolves and matches on either spelling, with a test of a record spelled through a symlink; until then the dispatch form writes `writable_roots` in canonical spelling, which keeps the gap closed by practice and not by the tool.

The paragraphs of this item's evidence that name an estate's private details are kept in that estate's backlog as ``rec:lanes:bound-roots-canonical-recorded-roots``.

**Claim (Lane harness frictions of the gateway era are re-measured under the current drivers)** · `claim:lanes:gateway-era-harness-frictions-remeasured`

**OPEN.** Recorded inside ``entry:door:permission-triage`` on 2026-09-20 and carried by no other item; not re-measured since the drivers folded into `superloop-lane`.

Three frictions of Claude CLI lanes of the torrust-index loop on falki and tjaldur: twenty zero-byte read-only entries (the driver's home guard: `.bash_profile`, `.bashrc`, `.claude/`, `.gitconfig` and the rest) appeared at a lane worktree root about 27 minutes into a work step and were snapshotted by a gateway push; the lane sandbox in `workspace-write` denied writes under a tracked `.vscode/` directory, so `git rebase` could not detach HEAD across a boundary where `.vscode/settings.json` differs; and the run-result record carried no toolchain field, so a worker could not stamp a gate's toolchain from the record alone. Each is re-measured under `superloop-lane driver` and closed, or fixed.

**Problem (opus lanes run from the Claude CLI like Codex lanes, and both are followed by the sonnet log-eater)** · `prob:lanes:claude-lane-driver`

**OPEN.** The Claude lane driver landed (ADR 027, lanelife import, driver d907b93/63149ca/7f7c587, gwflake e76cc3c, inhibitfix d6ee8f0, runbook fbd57c5); every Opus run is a Claude lane (closed 2026-10-15 with the roster of item ``dec:dispatch:no-producing-tier-below-normal`` and the lane backends record). Still open, carried by no open item: the driver review's findings listed in the last paragraph (no Codex reconstruction integration case; a failed watcher-thread spawn leaving `SIGTERM` and `SIGINT` blocked, with the lifecycle README overstating the fallback; a signal before the child spawns; a natural exit inside the signal race; the live-child refusal naming the child as holder; `Terminated` with no diagnostic; a reaped finish bypassing `RATE-LIMITED`; pid reuse keeping a two-line lock live) and the lane specification's "producing Codex worker" wording, still at generic main 13d05729 `docs/spec/lanes.md:13`.

Opus lanes once ran as Agent-tool subagents inside the orchestrator's harness, with no run directory, no event log for the feeder, no budget, no resume path, derived rather than measured step durations (item ``entry:dispatch:agent-tool-step-duration``) and a report only through the harness's completion notification. The Claude CLI offers the primitives Codex does (print mode with streamed JSON events, a resumable session id, a chosen model and effort), so an Opus lane runs as an external process from `claude-lane` with `codex-lane`'s command line, run directory, `RUN-REPORT` line, budget, stall and runaway constants, read-back gate with `PROCEED` by `--resume`, and follower-offset rules, followed by the same sonnet eater through `lane-feed`, which is schema-agnostic; the harness-side subagent path serves followers only. The lifecycle lives in the workspace library `lane-lifecycle`, generic over a `Backend` trait (`program`, `command`, `thread_id`, `last_message`, `lock_file`, `configure_pure`), and `codex-lane` and `claude-lane` are thin binaries over it. Claude backend facts: `CLAUDE_BIN` else `claude` on PATH; argv `-p --output-format stream-json --verbose` with the prompt on stdin, `--model`, `--effort` and `--resume <session>`; the enclosing session's `CLAUDECODE` and `CLAUDE_CODE_*` variables stripped; events written verbatim to `events.jsonl`; thread identity the `session_id` of the first `system`/`init` event; the last message the `result` event's text (a structural top-level extractor pinned against the key-order trap), else the last nonempty `assistant` text block; the sandbox argument mapped to the permission mode (`read-only` to `plan`, `workspace-write` to `acceptEdits`, `danger-full-access` to `bypassPermissions`), with confinement by Claude Code's bubblewrap sandbox in the lane home's settings, whose workspace-write network rule is denied by default. Lanes run as the main id in a hub clone under `~/.cache/tmp/lane-<name>`, like Codex lanes, since a lane is not loop material and returns its result as data through the hub; the lane home is `~/.claude-<loop>` (mode 700, passed as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`) holding the credential, hook-free `settings.json`, the worker charter as `CLAUDE.md`, the worker-server `ssh_config` and `projects/`, a home of its own because the deployed user hooks fire in any print-mode run under `~/.claude` and inject the orchestrator rulebook, which the sub-harness boundary forbids; its transcripts readable by every loop are the same accepted residual as Codex sessions under `~/.codex-<loop>`.

The end of a step is classified by what the child reached, not by how the monitor noticed it: a region that already ends with the backend's terminal result when the stall or runaway bound crosses is stopped through the same kill-and-wait path and filed as the natural exit would be, `OVER-BUDGET` at exit 0 or `FAILED` at exit 1 on an error result, with evidence `reason=RESULT-SEEN` and a separate `quiet=` interval (`last_growth=` is the offset of the last growth from the step start, and the two sum to the wall); `STALLED` is reserved for a silent event log. The Codex backend reads `turn.completed` and `turn.failed` as its terminal result. `SIGTERM` and `SIGINT` stop the child at the next poll and publish `FAILED`/1 naming the signal, watched by a `sigwait` thread over a blocked set because the workspace denies `unsafe`. The lock file's second line names the child pid, so a resume against a dead driver whose child still runs is refused as a live lock naming that pid, and every lock reader (`lane-watch::read_driver_pid`, `bin/lane-inhibit`) reads the first line as the driver and the second as the child.

Opened 2026-09-12 after the loop-home formalization (items ``entry:homes:subuid-loop-homes``, ``entry:homes:enter-loop-namespace-followups`` and ``prob:homes:orchestration-home-and-bridges``). Designed the same day from measurements on the toolbox CLI (2.1.258, `/usr/bin/claude`, absent on the host): `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` relocates credentials, settings, memory and transcripts (an empty directory reports `loggedIn: false`); the desktop app's login is not the CLI's; a `SessionStart` hook event carried ORCHESTRATION.md into a print-mode run, and `--setting-sources ""` suppresses it. ADR 027 `adr/027-lane-backends.md` landed in lane laneadr (sol xhigh, gate 30 s then work 140 s + 440 s across a stall, import gate 76.8 s, imported `5274f08` and `2a94503`), and the runbook section "Claude CLI lanes", a charter serving both harnesses (9,320 to 9,476 bytes) and an `install.sh` copying it into every `~/.claude-*/` home landed in lane clauderun (gpt-5.5 xhigh, 30 s then 340 s, `fe1add5`).

Frictions measured 2026-09-21 on four lanes of this loop and relayed from the index and tripod loops: `claude-lane` stamped lane ptabsorb `STALLED-KILLED` (exit 1) at wall 2,471 s of a 2,400 s budget with `last_growth` 2,346 s after it had written `READY FOR VERIFY` and shipped its commit, the child idling about 125 s past its result, while `codex-lane` killed the codexrule review at wall 425 s of 420 s with events still growing (`last_growth` 15 s), so both drivers misnamed the end of a step. The index loop found that a `SIGTERM` to `codex-lane` did not reach the `codex exec` child, that the dead-holder path stamped `CRASHED` without checking the child pid, and that the lane-gateway serve has no per-job transport timeout (item (`prob:gateway:lane-gateway-liveness-frictions`)); the tripod loop reported a detached lane dying at a compaction, an `awk` line-range command refused by the sandbox, the eater's stream watchdog ending an eater and a hung `git fetch`; the machete lane could stage no gate script in the Claude sandbox (a file under `$TMPDIR`, under `.git/` and a heredoc each refused); lane briefs needed the per-account `CARGO_HOME` line from `rust-env.sh`. After a host suspend Claude review lanes die with an API `ECONNRESET` (driver `FAILED`, `suspend_gap` recorded) and Codex children hang until the budget kill; a review's analysis lives in its session, so a resume asking for the verdict first is cheap (40 s for codexrule), which is why review briefs ask for the verdict as the first line. For about an hour after a suspend the session-uid clones over the worker aliases disconnected mid-fetch (`fetch-pack: unexpected disconnect while reading sideband packet`, 60 to 120 s on both nodes) while the loop-uid `<node>-orchestration` fetches worked in 10 s; local clones were the workaround and the worker aliases' sshd path is the suspect if it recurs.

Imported 2026-09-21, lane driver (opus xhigh): three signed cherry-picks `d907b93`, `63149ca`, `7f7c587` over `7fc689a`, patch identical, rebuilt package-scoped in 19 s and deployed at `7f7c587` with `lane-watch`, whose lock reader moved with the format. The falki gate's workspace test phase ran 25 m 47.7 s at nice 19 under another loop's load with 2,243 passed and one load-dependent failure in the untouched `runner_group_restart_reaps_roster_run`, which passed three times on rerun in 0.21–0.22 s each; install, root, fmtcheck, kit and public-pack were green in 61.3 s; review 518.5 s, IMPORTABLE. Recomputed under the new rule, ptabsorb (`reason=STALLED wall=2471s budget=2400s event_size=1281728 last_growth=2346s`) is `OVER-BUDGET`/0 with `quiet=125s`, while the morning's three Codex kills (quiet 185 s, 410 s, 141 s after a host suspend) stay stalls because their regions end on item events; the `turn.*` spelling was confirmed against every `events.jsonl` of the day (35 completed, one failed), and an interrupted Codex step reconstructs at `wall=0s`. The lane's own review of its diff caught that the two-line lock broke `lane-watch::read_driver_pid`, a regression the gate alone would not have caught since the watcher's test seeds a one-line lock. Each reaped-finish contract case sleeps one real poll interval (5 s), adding about ten seconds of real time; `await_pid` spins with a 60 s cap. A bundle under `~/.cache/tmp` is read-only for the lane sandbox, so a Claude lane ships its branch as a bundle over the literal ssh command. The runbook moved with the drivers: `docs/integration.md` lines 91 and 237 state the verdict rules and the Codex-home stanza and `--verify` contracts (lane rundocs, sol xhigh, 147 s; review IMPORTABLE WITH AMENDED TEXT, the reviewer's line 237 applied verbatim in a 190 s fix turn; codex main `f5f8fd7` on both hubs).

Flake closed 2026-09-22 in lane gwflake (sol xhigh, 1,826 s; review opus xhigh 296 s; imported as `e76cc3c` over `dd8b2a5`, patch identical): `runner_group_restart_reaps_roster_run` in ``packages/lane-gateway/src/tests.rs`` waited on the fixture's `ready` marker, written by a grandchild the instant it starts, then killed the serve, while the gateway writes `runner-groups/<lane>/<seq>.json` only after the spawn and the `/proc` capture (``packages/lane-gateway/src/run.rs:168-191``), so under load the kill could land before the record, and the panic skipped the reconciliation, which is how `runner-tree.sh` sleep loops leaked on the server for days. The two restart tests now wait for the record to parse, bounded by `FIXTURE_WAIT`, every runner-tree test holds a `RunnerTreeGuard` that reaps on drop, and `clean_runner_tree` tolerates pid files never written; on falki the library suite ran ten times under a concurrent workspace build, 117 passed each time at 11.8 s steady (23.6 s on the first), the package suite 12.2 s, and a deliberately failing run leaked nothing. A gate red in a test the candidate does not touch is answered by a rerun for the import and by a bite on the test for the repository, since the rerun alone leaves the leak.

Tripod-loop frictions recorded 2026-09-22 (relayed at 00:17Z beside its Wave 8 close of seventeen green steps held unpublished, five Codex bites on gpt-6-astra at high with read-backs of 30–195 s and work steps of 1,301–2,580 s): a brief derived from a previous brief kept stale environment sentences four times, so every environment sentence is re-read against the row it now serves; `ssh -n <node> 'cat > file' < local` copies nothing because `-n` closes stdin, so transfers travel by value inside the remote command; a test in a fenced-off module pinned a register figure the bite moved, so a bite that moves a census or register figure names every test that pins it. Its gate-launch ssh had held the session open since about 20:56Z on 2026-09-21, while this loop's `setsid nohup sh -c 'bash <gate> > <log> 2>&1; echo $? > <exit>' > /dev/null 2>&1 < /dev/null &` returned on falki in 1.3–1.4 s at 23:53Z and 00:13Z with the gate running 19 minutes on. Measured 2026-09-22 00:20–00:29Z: under the tripod account a detached `sleep 45` held the session the full 46 s on both pods in every form, while under the orchestration account the same probes returned in 1.29–1.35 s on falki and tjaldur; the holder was the tripod launch line, which backgrounded a whole `cd … && git fetch … && setsid nohup … > LOG 2>&1 < /dev/null` list, so a subshell kept the session's pipes until the gate ended (with a 20 s sleep, 21.3 s against 3.3 s with only the `setsid` command backgrounded). A trailing `&` backgrounds the whole list, so a detached launch is its own command, never the tail of a chain.

Residue still open as recorded: the driver review's findings, none changing a verdict on a path that runs (no Codex reconstruction integration case; a failed watcher-thread spawn after the mask block leaves `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` blocked for the step, and ``packages/lane-lifecycle/README.md`` line 26 overstates the fallback; a signal before the child spawns takes the default disposition; a natural exit inside the signal race publishes no signal evidence; the live-child refusal names the child as the holder; `Terminated` publishes `FAILED` with no stderr diagnostic; a reaped finish bypasses `RATE-LIMITED`; pid reuse keeps a two-line lock live); the lane specification's "Codex worker" wording generalized to either backend.

The paragraphs of this item's evidence that name an estate's private details are kept in that estate's backlog as ``rec:lanes:claude-lane-driver``.

**Problem (A past-budget step with an approved gateway run in flight is killed as stalled)** · `prob:lanes:past-budget-stall-ignores-in-flight-gateway-run`

**OPEN.** 2026-09-25 (lane r002c36 on gpt-6-sol, 2,351 s of 6,000; designed by lane r002c35 on opus; two named commits imported as f8e99da0 onto 6a5f2eeb after an opus review with three amendments applied in place). Still open, carried by no open item: the review follow-ups of the last paragraph, namely `scan_audit` aborting on an unexpected complete audit line instead of skipping it (still early returns through `?` at tools main 3c0f1a13, ``packages/lane-lifecycle/src/lib.rs:2827``), the per-poll re-read of the verdict and proposal files, no test over a realistic multi-record audit or a half-written last line, the runaway timing test that the old arithmetic also passes, and the evidence doc comment's lost sentence on why `last_growth` is not the quiet interval.

The lane monitor of ``packages/lane-lifecycle/src/lib.rs`` returns the stalled verdict when the wall exceeds the budget and the transcript has not grown for `STALL_WINDOW_SECONDS` (120 s), so past budget a step lives only while its transcript grows every two minutes; a step waiting on an approved gateway proposal still executing for it is not silent in the sense the window guards against, since the server is working on its behalf. `BudgetMonitor` therefore takes a second observation: `observe_awaited` raises `last_awaited`, and `probe` reads the quiet interval from the later of the last growth and the last credited poll, the window and multiple unchanged. The driver learns its gateway lane from `--gateway-lane <lane>` and the state root from `LANE_GATEWAY_STATE` (then `XDG_STATE_HOME`, then the home default, the gateway's own order), binds the registration whose `worktree` line equals its workdir, and credits a poll while an approved verdict of that lane has no audited `result` for its sequence and its proposal timeout has not lapsed, scanning the audit incrementally by whole lines; every read failure is no credit, and a step launched without the option writes exactly its former bytes. A credited poll writes `awaiting=<lane>:<seq>` to the sidecar and the kill evidence a `last_await=` reading, both parsed unchanged by `lane-watch`. A brief sizes the budget for the lane's own work, with four times the budget still bounding the work plus the longest run it may wait on, and the runbook's launch lines carry the option and the state root.

Found 2026-09-25 in the torrust-index loop's lane hpins, step work-1: a full study regeneration through the gateway (two such runs measured 1,516 s and 1,337 s on sykora, proposal timeout 3,600 s) outlasted the budget, and the first poll gap beyond the window ended the step `STALLED-KILLED` at wall 6,613 s of budget 5,400 s while the run kept executing and its result landed in the audit afterwards; that loop resumed under a second step with a 7,200 s budget and a pacing rule. Landed the same day: seventeen tests pin the arithmetic, the predicate, the option and the verdicts (an in-flight run with the stall inputs ends `RUNAWAY-KILLED` at the absolute bound; an audited result or an open proposal keeps `STALLED-KILLED`), and the spec's stalled-step scenario, budget-overrun rule and both kill records state the progress source. Roster runs had the same exposure and are ``entry:lanes:past-budget-stall-ignores-in-flight-roster-run``.

Review follow-ups still open, none blocking: `scan_audit` aborts on a complete audit line without a numeric `seq` or a string `operation` instead of skipping it (credit stays off, never on); the in-flight verdict and proposal files are re-read every poll instead of once; no test runs a realistic multi-record audit log or a half-written last line; the runaway timing test passes under the old arithmetic too (a sharper shape: budget 40, observe 150, probe 161); the evidence doc comment lost the sentence explaining why `last_growth` is not the quiet interval.

**Problem (The suspend-resume drivers keep the suspendok and watchsleep review residuals)** · `prob:lanes:suspend-resume-driver-review-residuals`

**OPEN.** Carried from ``entry:lanes:lane-step-idle-inhibit``: the review rounds of lanes suspendok (imported 2026-09-20 as `aa4dcbb`, `dce02c3`, `998c267`) and watchsleep (commits `365402f`, `8c0f1a2`, `1e35a0d`) left these residuals in the lane drivers that ``entry:lanes:suspend-resume-replaces-idle-inhibit`` kept; none is landed.

An unreadable or invalid `/proc/uptime` still fails a whole step, and the poll loop's clock-sample failure returns with the child still running (`boottime_seconds` and the `clock sample failed` exits in ``packages/lane-lifecycle/src/lib.rs``); ``docs/spec/output.md`` states that `wall` plus `suspend_gap` is the elapsed time an outside observer saw, while the measurement is exact only above a two-second tolerance; the Claude driver's suspend case pins `wall=2s` with two probes inside a real one-second stub sleep, and the live-child evidence exists only in the Claude driver's tests; the monitor sidecar goes through `atomic_write`, which syncs the file and its directory on every poll; a resumed step with an old carried-over event log reads as alive early; the reader duplicates the driver's boot-clock read; and the live-record case rests on a 30 s fixture window.

**Problem (A gateway refusal without a sequence leaves a roster run credited until its wall budget)** · `prob:lanes:roster-run-sequenceless-refusal-credit`

**OPEN.** Carried from ``entry:lanes:past-budget-stall-ignores-in-flight-roster-run``, whose lane r002c48 review left two findings, F4 and F6; neither is landed.

The stall predicate's audit scan removes a credited roster run only for a refusal that names its `seq` (the `refusal` arm of `GatewayWatch` in ``packages/lane-lifecycle/src/lib.rs``, line 2880 at tools main 3c0f1a13), so a refusal carrying no sequence (`unknown_request_field`, `malformed_request`) leaves the run credited as progress until its registered wall budget ends, bounded by that budget and the runaway cap; the cure pairs the refusal with its request record or credits only sequences the lane's watermark accepted (F4). The review's fixture rule name (F6) rides with it.

**Problem (The run-directory allocation leaves hand-edited driver test matrices and two low review findings)** · `prob:lanes:run-directory-allocation-follow-ups`

**OPEN.** Carried from ``entry:lanes:run-directory-named-by-the-second``, whose lane r002c79 (main 4bd7aebf) edited the drivers' test matrices by hand and whose review left two low findings; none is landed.

The tools repository's projection policy activates the test-matrix and test-index projections for the gateway and linter owners only (``.linter/policies.toml`` lines 303 to 307 at tools main 3c0f1a13), so the drivers' test matrices, now under `packages/superloop-lane/tests/driver_codex/` and `packages/superloop-lane/tests/driver_claude/`, are maintained by hand; the fresh-run fixture's drop in ``packages/lane-lifecycle/src/lib.rs`` expects its removal (`remove fresh run fixture`, line 5111) where its sibling fixtures ignore the result; and one doc line says the exhausted bound maps to the runtime failure while the test pins only the allocator's error.

**Problem (A host sleep fails a boxed Codex run whose tool process does not survive it)** · `prob:lanes:host-sleep-fails-boxed-codex-run`

**OPEN.** The host slept on 2026-10-25 from about 20:11Z to 20:24Z (about 507 s by siedelweber's measurement): every host process paused and resumed and the host lane drivers survived, but naturrhythmuszentrum's boxed Codex run decision-integer-arms-1 ended FAILED at 2,707 s with `suspend_gap=507s`, `write_stdin failed: Unknown process id`, an orphan tool output and `thread … not found`, because the Codex child's tool process did not survive the sleep; in the same sleep the inbox watcher's 540 s bound overran by 256 s. This item carries that evidence, since the idle inhibit ``entry:lanes:lane-step-idle-inhibit`` is superseded and the suspend-resume runtime that replaced it ``entry:lanes:suspend-resume-replaces-idle-inhibit`` is done: the boxed route treats a step that fails after a measured suspend gap as an external interruption and resumes the same thread (`[ORCHESTRATION-proto:dispatch:external-interruption-resume]`), as the host drivers do for a stream that ended without a result event, and a fixture shows which shape the lost tool process leaves in the transcript; the resume route a boxed round lacks after a budget or suspend kill is (`prob:lanes:killed-first-round-resume-route`).

The orphan tool output is one way a sleep produces the orphan that (`claim:lanes:codex-orphan-tool-call-output-measurement`) measures, and the watcher's overrun is the sleep showing in a wall-clock bound, read as the sleep rather than as a hung call.

**Problem (A Codex lane's worker finds no charter in the Codex home its driver binds)** · `prob:lanes:codex-lane-charter-in-bound-home`

**OPEN.** A Codex lane's worker reported `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` absent in its environment (r002f95 dispatch, 2026-10-26T00:55Z, codex-rt with this loop's Codex home), so the worker charter reached it only through the brief's citation. The charter install for Codex lanes (the deployed `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` named by the runbook) is checked against the Codex home the driver actually binds (`CODEX_HOME=~/.config/loops/<loop>/codex`), where the charter copy may need to live as `AGENTS.md`; the charter copies' earlier staleness and their link design are ``entry:deploy:charter-copies-in-codex-homes``, and the home's move is ``entry:homes:codex-homes-under-loop-directory``.

**Problem (The host lock scan reads a lock-file pid's stat as UTF-8)** · `prob:lanes:host-lock-scan-reads-pid-stat-as-bytes`

**OPEN.** Found on 2026-10-29 at 08:24Z beside the snapshot read that lane r002g02 corrects: `measure_host_lock` in ``packages/superloop-lane/src/launch/retire_proof.rs`` (line 362 at the lane's tip, line 267 at its base) reads `/proc/<pid>/stat` for the one or two pids that a run's lock file names with a UTF-8 read, so a process whose command name is not UTF-8 fails the read and the lock scan records uncertainty instead of a live holder. That still refuses, so nothing retires wrongly, but the refusal does not name its cause. The read becomes a bytes read with a lossy command name, as the process snapshot's is, so the holder is found present; it is an item of its own because it predates the lane and sits on a different path from ``entry:lanes:retire-busy-scan-excludes-own-descendants``.

**Problem (The installer's unmeasured re-pin list names two refusal kinds only and is empty in a blocked preview)** · `prob:lanes:installer-repin-unmeasured-list-scope`

**OPEN.** Found by the third review of lane r002g05 on 2026-10-29, which shipped the list in deploy #68 ``entry:lanes:repin-evidence-read-through-loop-private-step``; runbook line 41 states both limits since the import, and no fix has landed.

The installer's `repin_unmeasured_runs` list names a run pinned to the previous stub whose measurement refused `repin-door-uncertain` or `driver-report-private` only (``install_door.rs:1360`` at the lane's final candidate a126c364). Any other refusal of that measurement, such as the private step's preflight kinds `unsafe-path` and `checkout-record` and the owner-executable checks, propagates (`:1365`, through `measure_repins` at `:1823`) and refuses the whole installation by its own kind without naming the run; and a blocked preview returns before measuring (`:1680`) yet carries the field, so it always reports an empty list. Neither is unsafe, since the blocked preview takes no action and an unexpected kind refuses rather than guesses. The follow-up either omits the field from a blocked preview or measures before blocking, and either lists every refusal kind with its run or names the run in the propagated refusal, with a test for each.

Measured again at deploy #80 on 2026-10-30: lane r002g18 widened the measured set from runs pinned to the previous stub to runs pinned to any stub other than the one being installed ``entry:door:installer-repin-scan-reads-previous-generation-only``, so the propagated refusal that names no run now reaches a run on any older stub, as the review of that lane noted for the first commit's Compatibility paragraph; this item's naming of the run in the propagated refusal covers the wider set unchanged.

**Problem (A started run whose driver died without a report and whose container exited has no route while no interrupted settlement exists)** · `prob:lanes:started-run-dead-driver-exited-container-route`

**OPEN.** A follow-up of lane r002g06 on 2026-10-29, not the shape measured that day, where both door runs the host restart left were in interrupted settlement.

Retirement of a started run requires the labelled container absent (``retire_proof.rs`` and ``retire.rs`` refuse "labelled container is still present"), and the recovery launch, the one route that removes a proved-exited container, applies only to a run in interrupted settlement. A started run whose driver died with no report and whose container exited would therefore have no tool route but a hand removal. Either the retirement's dead-driver route removes a proved-exited container with the recovery cleanup's proof and record, or the runbook names the hand removal, with the container's inspect record and logs saved first, as the route for that shape.

**Problem (The retirement scan's launcher exclusion is tested before the path match and without a uid check)** · `prob:lanes:retire-launcher-exclusion-after-path-match-and-uid`

**OPEN.** Hardening from the review of lane r002g06 (F3), which shipped the exclusion in deploy #69 on 2026-10-29; no fix has landed.

The exclusion of a launcher of the same retire command is tested before `path_match` and without a uid check (``retire_proof.rs`` lines 239 to 244 at the candidate b4bdb3cb), so a process whose argv ends with the scanning argv is excluded even when its earlier words carry the run's driver path or `lane-LOOP-RUN`, whatever its uid. None of these can retire a held run: the apply argv carries `--expect-digest`, a dry run fooled this way is caught by the apply's own scan, and an identical concurrent retire is serialised by the retirement's lease and locks. The argv tail is tested only after `path_match` fails and only for a process whose uid is the effective uid, which keeps the measured case, since the owner-uid `flatpak-spawn` client was refused through the `same_home` branch.

**Problem (No test of the built binary shows the restart deadlock gone end to end)** · `prob:lanes:restart-deadlock-end-to-end-production-binary-test`

**OPEN.** From the review of lane r002g06 (F4) on 2026-10-29; no test has landed.

The re-pin tests of the lane's first commit (``repin_door_tests.rs:97`` and `:180` at the candidate 663cd894) call `repin_door::run` in process rather than the built binary and read its returned value rather than its stdout JSON, and no test runs the sequence the hotfix exists for: a re-pin applied with an exited container present, then the recovery launch removing that container under its cleanup record. The harness exists in ``tests/failed_dispatch_entry.rs`` (the `repin` and `recovery` steps of its report fixture and the ps, inspect and rm fixture runtime), and the end-to-end case is added there, its fixture runtime answering the inspect form the real podman accepts ``entry:lanes:container-exited-proof-reads-plain-inspect``.

**Problem (On a host launch-wall end the attempt's elapsed field holds the whole launch's elapsed time)** · `prob:lanes:launch-wall-end-names-launch-elapsed`

**OPEN.** Noted by the second review of lane r002g20 on 2026-10-30 (its O2); no change has landed.

The host guard of ``entry:lanes:host-driver-launch-bound-follows-plan`` records its end in `driver_end` as `cause: launch-wall` with `bound_seconds` and `attempt_elapsed_seconds` (``packages/superloop-lane/src/launch.rs`` lines 5872 to 5874 at tools main 62e870d5, the lane's c02 81b4828a as imported), the field's name kept from the helper's `attempt-wall` record for symmetry, while its value is the elapsed time of the whole launch, the helper's start and every capacity attempt included. A reader comparing a helper's wall record with a host's reads two quantities under one name, and the guard's span of four attempt walls makes the difference large. The host's record names its own quantity, a `launch_elapsed_seconds` field on a `launch-wall` end with the runbook's budget paragraph (`docs/integration.md:111`) and a reader's test following, or the runbook states that on a `launch-wall` end the field is the launch's elapsed time.

**Problem (superloop-lane launch blocks for the whole round, so the round dies with the session that started it)** · `prob:lanes:launch-detaches-the-round`

**OPEN.** Measured by naturrhythmuszentrum at the host shutdown of 2026-10-31 at about 22:34Z and reported in its letter of 1 November; no change has landed, and the interim form is a launch from the host shell under `setsid nohup`.

naturrhythmuszentrum launched its lane quantity-covariance-1, with a 10,800 s budget, as `flatpak-spawn --host superloop-lane launch …` from a background shell of its session; `launch` stays in the foreground for the whole round, so when the session's process ended at about 22:34Z the shell was killed and the round with it, 5,597 s into its budget, its `run-report.txt` reading `exit=1 … verdict=FAILED` and its stderr ending in `mailbox watcher: Broken pipe`, while the worker's home and uncommitted work survived. This loop's r002g34 driver died the same night at the same shutdown after 749 s ``entry:pack:register-guard-honours-match-kind``. A round's life is thus bound to whichever session started it, and a session restart, a compaction or a desktop update ends every lane in flight; and because `launch` blocks, a shell loop that launches two lanes runs them one after the other. `launch` detaches the round, under a transient systemd user unit or an equivalent, and returns with the run directory as the handle, the blocking form becoming an explicit flag, so that two launches side by side are the ordinary case.

(2026-11-03T20:58Z, step mail-e of lane r002g76) A host session restart killed the codex-lane driver with it, and the step was recorded FAILED exit 1 with no error event; the driver is detached from the launching session (its own session or scope) so that a harness restart suspends the lane's watching, not the lane, and the run record names the kill rather than FAILED, as the outcome-kind item asks for a host shutdown (`prob:lanes:driver-usage-limit-outcome-kind`).

**Problem (A re-pin refused over a plan that predates the entry program's move names retirement as the run's route)** · `prob:lanes:repin-refusal-over-a-pre-move-plan-names-retirement`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index at its door step for #87 and #88 on 2026-10-31 at 16:25Z; no change has landed.

The old run hfastsurface, launched before the entry program moved from the retired tooling path to the deployed one, refuses `launch --repin-door --dry-run` with "protected plan differs from the current pinned plan; differing keys: driver_argv, driver_bin, entry_bin … do not rewrite the protected plan to bypass the mismatch" ``entry:waves:deploy-89-public-set-manifest-and-cut-installed-silently-2026-10-31``. The refusal is right, since a re-pin must not move a run onto programs it was never launched with, but its last sentence tells the reader what not to do and not what to do, so the loop asked whether reconciling the pinned inputs is a door operation. The run's only route is settlement and retirement (`prob:lanes:retire-unrecoverable-settlement-with-retired-programs`); the refusal names it, and the door's install dry run, which lists such a run under `repin_unmeasured_runs` at every step, says the same, so that the step record carries the reason the run never re-pins.

**Problem (A run whose pinned driver and entry program are retired cannot be retired, because its recovery needs a re-pin that refuses)** · `prob:lanes:retire-unrecoverable-settlement-with-retired-programs`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index on 2026-10-31 at about 16:28Z for hfastsurface, whose plan dates from 2026-10-22 with an interrupted settlement, and the same for hcomphost; a tools lane after the publication lanes, with its own review and deploy, carries it, and until then both runs stay untouched and off every re-pin.

`retire --dry-run` refuses `interrupted-settlement: started run awaits launch recovery and reply readback`; the recovery is the original launch form of the interrupted round, which refuses `plan-read: protected plan differs from the current pinned plan; differing keys: door, driver_argv, driver_bin, entry_bin; first reconcile the named non-door pinned inputs`; and `launch --repin-door` refuses the non-door drift (`prob:lanes:repin-refusal-over-a-pre-move-plan-names-retirement`). Each refusal is right and together they close a loop with no exit, so a run launched before the entry program and the driver moved can never be retired; the earlier retirement routes reconcile a run with no recorded identity or a dead driver ``entry:lanes:retire-without-recorded-settlement`` ``entry:lanes:dead-driver-started-run-retire-route``, not one whose pinned programs no longer exist. The tools need a retirement form for an unrecoverable settlement: when the pinned driver or entry program no longer exists at its pinned digest, and the container's state is exited or unknown with the launcher gone, `retire` takes the run's launch, container and readback records as the evidence that nothing of it runs and writes the retirement record with that reason instead of a recovered reply, refusing while any of those records shows a live process.

**Problem (A relative run directory is reported as a malformed mailbox line)** · `prob:lanes:relative-run-directory-reported-as-a-malformed-mailbox-line`

**OPEN.** (wabe letter 2026-11-01 16:19Z). `codex-lane … -d runs/<run>` (a run directory relative to the launch directory) exits 1 within a second with `runtime_failure: mailbox watcher: letter-record: malformed durable line`, while the absolute `-d` runs normally and the run holds no letters.jsonl: `open_absolute_directory` in ``packages/lane-lifecycle/src/mailbox.rs`` returns the same `invalid_record()` error for a non-absolute directory as for a malformed journal line, so a path-form mistake reads as mailbox corruption and sends the reader to repair a record. Remedy: refuse a relative run directory at the CLI boundary with a message naming `-d` and the path (or canonicalize it against the current directory at parsing), and give the mailbox opener its own error text; a test through the built binary for the relative form. Evidence: wabe run 20261101T145305Z-graph-impl-3, step-reflow.txt 16:10:25Z. Patch class under the cadence.

**Problem (The Codex lane sandbox masks the host's credential directories)** · `prob:lanes:codex-sandbox-masks-host-credential-directories`

**OPEN.** (same finding). The codex-lane sandbox binds the whole host filesystem read-only as the host uid, so every host-uid credential directory (`~/.config/gh-loops`, `~/.config/loops/*/codex` auth, the Claude config dirs of other loops) is readable by a worker. Remedy: the lane driver masks the known credential directories inside the sandbox (`--tmpfs` over `~/.config/gh-loops` and over every `~/.config/loops/<other loop>` and the Claude config dirs the lane does not own), with a test through the built binary that a worker cannot read a file placed there; patch class under the cadence; defence in depth beside the custody migration, not a substitute.

**Problem (The runtime-proxy acceptance compares the proxy's digest with a reference from an independent authority)** · `prob:lanes:runtime-proxy-acceptance-reference-moves-to-an-independent-authority`

**OPEN.** TOOLS digests (r002g59 review N2): D069, the runtime-proxy acceptance (``packages/superloop-lane/src/install.rs:256-266``) compares the proxy's digest with a 16-digit prefix from a sibling provenance file controlled by the same owner; lengthening the digest would not help, the reference itself must move to an independent authority.

**Problem (The launch checks the vendor proxy's readiness before the first round after a boot)** · `prob:lanes:launch-checks-vendor-proxy-readiness-after-a-boot`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-02) Finding from torrust-index (15:50Z): the first boxed round launched 6 min after the host boot (vendor and door units active since 15:41:44 local) failed after 13 s with `codex_models_manager::manager: failed to refresh available models: request timed out` and a socat broken pipe; a settle and a fresh round ran normally. Fix: the launch checks the vendor proxy's readiness (a bounded models-list or health request through the proxy) before starting the first round after a boot, refusing with a named readiness cause instead of spending a round; pairs with the resume-after-boot row. The 13:23:17Z stops across the estate (torrust-index hreuse exit 143, tripod's work turn, this loop's three dispatches) were the host user session's shutdown.target, not letters or vendors.

**Open Question (Whether the new launcher argv bumps the boxed-runtime contract)** · `open:lanes:launcher-argv-bump-of-the-boxed-runtime-contract`

**OPEN.** (r002g66 review 1, 2026-11-02) F4 (tools contract): the new launcher argv is not caught by the `boxed-runtime 1` contract check; launcher and driver are one binary on this estate, so a bump is a backlog decision.

**Problem (The lane forms of the tools provision a worker from the bare repositories in its workspace)** · `prob:lanes:launch-provisions-workers-from-bare-workspaces`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-03) Every worker of every loop receives clones of the bare repositories it needs in its workspace and makes its own checkouts; the rule is stated in the generic specification as ``rule:dispatch:workers-clone-from-bare-repositories``. The tools' forms that still assume a clone made for the worker follow it: the Codex home's writable roots, whose canonical form lists no lane clone (`prob:homes:check-signin-home-admits-lane-clone-git-roots`), and every launch or review form that names a worker's clone instead of its workspace; the loop's own lane scripts follow in the estate's backlog.

(2026-11-03, lane r002g76's c11 draft observation) Node worktrees of one repository share .git, so preparing or fetching a branch while a workspace gate runs in a sibling worktree adds a shared ref and reds the repository-integrity tests; the lane form uses separate clones for preparation and gates, and the charter or the runbook states it.

**Problem (The settle report's wall survives its JSON round-trip, so the interrupted-completion test compares what was written)** · `prob:lanes:settle-report-wall-survives-json-round-trip`

**OPEN.** r002g74 run 3 settle red is a FLOAT ROUND-TRIP, not load (2026-11-03): `interrupted_completion_report_validates_socket_evidence` compares a report object whose `wall_seconds` read back as 1.3276944880000001 against the computed 1.327694488; the test (or the report writer) compares an f64 after a JSON round-trip, so it reds whenever the measured wall is not exactly representable after serialization. Fix: compare serialized text, or an epsilon, or round the written figure to the millisecond at the writer (tools lane; untouched by r002g74).

**Problem (A reviewed route retires or restores old runs that have no owner route)** · `prob:lanes:historical-retirement-route`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, deploy #106) The cutover's closing letter reported `retire-uncertain`, `interrupted-settlement`, `missing-identity` and pending-request refusals for old boxed runs that have no owner route, and a reservation that the census refused because it predates the import records cannot be harvested or retired through the normal path: one loop's two lanes reach theirs only through the loop's own quarantine refs and fresh runs (its letter of 2026-11-05). A reviewed historical-retirement route retires such a run with its evidence recorded, and a reviewed restore route returns a refused reservation to the normal path so that harvest and retirement run as for any run; the migration's census classifies the same history (`prob:mail:migration-reconciles-pre-record-run-history`).

**Problem (The support for non-boxed workers is removed from the tools)** · `prob:lanes:non-boxed-worker-support-removed`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, the lead of the second group, taken once the Claude boxed workers work, (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) The tools' support for workers outside a box is removed: the host-form drivers (``codex-lane``, ``claude-lane`` and the host form of the lane driver) with the settings source a host driver would need, the host lane-home scans (`prob:homes:host-lane-home-scans-removed`), and the code that serves only them, each removal closing the items that exist only because of it. A host form is a second code path beside the box for every lifecycle concern, launch, resume, settlement, retirement, letters and credentials, each with its own defects, reviews and items, and it leaves a worker's credential and transcripts readable at the host uid; one confined form reviewed once is the smaller and the safer estate.

A loop that really needs a worker outside a box uses an in-harness subagent of its own session and reports the friction as a finding; work that needs the desktop, visual work and the browser tool, is the standing case of that exception, and the generic specification states the rule (``prob:dispatch:non-boxed-worker-exception-is-an-in-harness-subagent`` in the generic backlog). The estate's half is the runbook's host forms and the workspace form's dispatch scripts that launch a host driver (``prob:dispatch:workspace-dispatch-launches-only-boxed-workers`` in its backlog).

Folded from ``prob:lanes:host-driver-reads-an-owner-held-settings-source``, whose remedy the removal supersedes: `superloop-lane driver start` and `driver resume` read their driver and backend vectors only from the three variables the entry exports, the entry runs as the loop uid, which holds no vendor home, and the runbook's host form names no settings source, so a loop's two lanes stopped on `missing driver_argv` (its letter of 2026-11-05). The host driver gains no settings source; until the removal the installed ``codex-lane`` stays the host route, and the runbook writes the interim form down (``prob:docs:runbook-host-launch-forms-owner-uid`` in the estate's backlog).

### The lane box · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-box`

**Problem (A healthy box round's stderr carries a socat connection-reset line every minute)** · `prob:box:box-control-heartbeat-reset-noise`

**OPEN.** Lane B deferred it to an investigation of the relay; the healthy round's stderr still carries the reset lines. The lane's note on the only `socat` invocation it found ends mid-word in the source table ("found i"), so its rest is lost.

the box driver's stderr carries one `socat … E read(6, …): Connection reset by peer` line per minute for the life of the round (naturrhythmuszentrum, 2026-10-15: 42 lines on solving-t from 14:31:02Z, 37 on matrix-core-1 from 14:30:59Z to 15:06:15Z, a run that finished cleanly with its reply): the per-minute control heartbeat over `.control-RUN.sock` resets after each exchange, so the lines are routine noise that reads as a fault when a run is examined. Item (tools): the heartbeat closes its side cleanly or the driver filters the expected reset; the stderr of a healthy round carries no error lines.

**Problem (the box install dry run refuses a private report over its byte bound)** · `prob:box:install-private-report-bound`

**OPEN.** The box install dry run for torrust-index still refuses with `install-private: private report exceeds 65536 bytes`, so the first production measurement of the sign-in root's group convergence ``entry:homes:prepare-signin-group-remedy-scope`` cannot be taken; what remains is the private report paging or summarising its unchanged entries, or the bound raised with its reason stated, and a fixture pinning a private tier above the bound through the production command.

(tools, superloop-lane install) deploy #55 host step, torrust-index, 2026-10-24T20:05Z: `superloop-lane install torrust-index --dry-run` refuses `install-private: private report exceeds 65536 bytes` (5.5 s) on a loop whose private tier has grown with its runs, so the box install's dry run, and with it the first production measurement of the sign-in root's group convergence (item ``entry:homes:prepare-signin-group-remedy-scope``), cannot be taken for the loop that most needs it; the private report pages or summarises its unchanged entries, or the bound is raised with its reason stated, and a fixture pins a private tier above the bound through the production command

**Problem (The exercise's usage text and scope refusals name the forms that would measure)** · `prob:box:exercise-usage-and-scope-name-their-forms`

**OPEN.** Measured at deploy #58 by naturrhythmuszentrum.

`superloop-lane exercise` accepts `exercise LOOP --run RUN --case CASE` (``main.rs:915-930``; cases door-shape, door-attribution, door-extensions, network, harvest, home-layout, mounts, rotation and reply-receipts), but its usage refusal prints only `exercise LOOP [--dry-run]`, so a caller who adds `--dry-run` or drops the loop name is told the case form does not exist; the usage text names both forms, and the lane-box-build conformance row that documents the case form matches the parser's words. `exercise … --case door-attribution` on a review-scoped box ends `unmeasured` with `door-review-compute-refusal`; the report names the launch flag (`--door-scope work`) that would make the measurement possible, so a loop does not read the scope as a finding.

**Problem (The lane box build-out's review residuals and trial frictions)** · `prob:box:lane-box-build-out-residuals`

**OPEN.** Carried from ``entry:box:lane-box-build-out``, closed 2026-10-15 at the first bite (tools main 5edd79f), whose early reviews and trial runs left these residuals; none is landed.

A settings-only build hashes the template supplied now while a launch from an older report reopens the path that report records. The ownership report lives only on standard output, so a launch verifies every inode after assignment and records a ready state, and resource values need one authoritative source. The entry's check of the driver follows a link and proves only an execute bit (`fs::metadata` and the `0o111` test in ``packages/loop-home/src/driver_launch.rs``, lines 258 to 264 at tools main 3c0f1a13), and the checked run directory is compared by name against the runs root but is not bound to the driver's arguments or working directory, so its components can change between check and launch. One test named for the child's status proves a stub's. Frictions from the trial and other loops: a refusal line carries no wall; a lane's pushes reach one hub, so the mirror lags until the next fetch; the gateway's run cooldown and queue wait stack to about 140 s per approved proposal; and a loop's first lane on its own sign-in needs pure mode to honour an explicit Codex home.

**Problem (A boxed lane's letter-inbox callback cannot initialise inotify and polls once a second)** · `prob:box:box-inotify-init-denied-letter-poll`

**OPEN.** Measured by torrust-index on 2026-10-26: inside a box, the lane's letter-inbox callback reported a negative descriptor from inotify initialisation and fell back to polling the inbox directory once a second. Nothing failed, but the box's inotify limits or its seccomp profile deny `inotify_init`, so a boxed worker that waits for letters polls for as long as it waits. The next step measures the box's `max_user_instances` and the seccomp verdict on the call, then either allows the call or states the poll interval as the callback's contract; the host's own inotify limit, a different cause of the same fallback, is ``prob:estate:host-inotify-instance-limit``.

**Problem (The codex name is bound where the vendor's installer never writes)** · `prob:box:codex-name-bound-where-the-installer-never-writes`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-04T08:40Z) `codex update`, the standalone installer, ran from 0.157.0 to 0.166.0 in 12 s and replaced ~/.local/bin/codex, the script copy of bin/codex-rt that routes every Codex run into the codex-rt container, with a symlink to the bare host binary; the wrapper was restored from the deployed codex-rt copy once the running lane step had ended.

An installer that owns a path the estate also binds clobbers the binding at every update, so the install map binds the codex name where the installer never writes (the lane driver and the loops call codex-rt by name, or the wrapper lives at a path the installer never writes), and the update procedure re-checks the wrapper afterwards. Also open: whether the standalone auto-update (auto-update-version in ~/.codex/packages/standalone) can run unattended and clobber it again.

**Problem (Claude lanes run in boxes, launched and driven as the boxed Codex runs are)** · `prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, the lead of the Claude boxed workers, the estate's next large task) Claude lanes run in boxes as the boxed Codex runs do: launched under the box launcher's subordinate identity, with the worker's own mail folder as its only read-write mail mount, reaching the compute node through the door route with the registered worker key, driven by the same lane driver, and reporting through the box's run report, with the watcher duties unchanged. The launcher and the driver gain the Claude backend's boxed form, and the parts below land in order: the pinned program (`prob:box:claude-template-pins-the-program`), the sign-in and its endpoints (`prob:box:claude-sign-in-and-endpoints-in-the-box`), rounds and resume in one box home (`prob:box:claude-rounds-resume-in-one-box-home`), the door's first modes for Claude requests (`prob:door:claude-requests-take-the-floor-compliant-first-modes`), resources and harvest (`prob:box:claude-resources-and-harvest-measured`), the confinement cutover (`prob:box:claude-confinement-cutover-disables-the-inner-sandbox`) and the lane route's move off the runtime wrapper (`prob:box:claude-lane-route-leaves-the-runtime-wrapper`).

A boxed worker is the confined and reviewable form of a producing worker: its filesystem is its box home, its outward routes are the door's, every effect it has outside the box is a door request with a record, and its sign-in never sits where another session of the host uid can read it. Every Claude lane today runs as the main id in a host lane home under Claude Code's own sandbox, so its credential and transcripts are readable at the host uid, its node calls depend on a settings exclusion that a `time` prefix defeats, and its state lies outside the box's harvest boundary; and the loop reviews two code paths for every lifecycle concern, the host form and the box. The design names this work as the box build-out's Claude parity bite (its eighth, in the estate's lane box build document), after the Codex full box; the estate's half is the trial and the per-loop cutover (``prob:dispatch:claude-boxed-workers-trial-and-cutover`` in its backlog) and the runbook's boxed Claude form (``prob:docs:runbook-states-the-boxed-claude-form`` there). The measured Claude lane and box frictions stand in this group's slice: the driver's review residue and its finished-step record, the box's own residuals, and the launch's boot and detachment rows.

(2026-11-05) The group's first consumer is the backlog manager, a persistent Claude worker that runs today as a host lane fed by brief files and moves to a boxed run steered by letters in its own mail folder (``prob:dispatch:backlog-manager-is-the-first-boxed-claude-worker`` in the estate's backlog).

**Problem (The Claude program is pinned in a template of its own, measured in the box)** · `prob:box:claude-template-pins-the-program`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) The box's Claude program comes from a template of its own, pinned by digest and copied into the launcher-owned read-only ``.local/bin`` of the box home as the Codex program is, with its start, refresh and resume measured in the box. The program runs without the toolbox that supplies it today, so the image's runtime dependencies are checked against it, and the SELinux label choice for its binds is measured: labelled binds where they work, or the recorded acceptance of the disabled-label shape. The toolbox's package channel stays the source the template is built from (``prob:estate:claude-loops-toolbox-records-the-rpm-channel`` in the estate's backlog).

**Problem (The box reaches the loop's Claude sign-in through the vendor proxy, on pinned endpoints)** · `prob:box:claude-sign-in-and-endpoints-in-the-box`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) A boxed Claude run reaches the loop's Claude sign-in as a boxed Codex run reaches its own: the real credential replaced at the vendor proxy, never present in the box home, with a per-run state split where measurements show two runs of one loop overlapping on the sign-in's state. The exact hosts the Claude program reaches during start, refresh and resume are recorded and pinned, and every other host, including one newly reached after an update, fails until its name is added. The sign-in itself is the loop's, made once by an owner-run login (``prob:homes:owner-claude-sign-in-command-in-runbook-and-birth-brief`` in the estate's backlog).

**Problem (A boxed Claude run resumes its rounds in one box home, with its letters over mail and its pushes through the door)** · `prob:box:claude-rounds-resume-in-one-box-home`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) A boxed Claude run keeps one box home across its rounds, so a resume reaches the session's transcript where the first round left it; its steering arrives as mail in the worker's own folder and is retained as step evidence as for Codex rounds; and its lane branch reaches the workspace bare through the door's bare push. The driver's finished-step record (`prob:lanes:claude-lane-finished-state-null-driver`) and the review residue of the Claude driver (`prob:lanes:claude-lane-driver`) are settled in the same code, since the boxed form drives the same lifecycle.

**Problem (A boxed Claude run's door requests take the floor-compliant first modes)** · `prob:door:claude-requests-take-the-floor-compliant-first-modes`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) A boxed Claude run's door requests enter each class at the floor-compliant audit or allow first mode that the loop's row records, with enforcement optional once agreeing adjudicated labels accumulate for Claude requests, as the Codex full box proved for its own; the door's records and the triage agent read a Claude run's requests without a backend-specific path.

**Problem (A boxed Claude run's resources and harvest are measured)** · `prob:box:claude-resources-and-harvest-measured`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) The box's recorded memory, process-count and CPU limits are measured against a Claude run, whose program and node runtime differ from Codex in size, and the harvest of ``.local`` (sources, outbox and inbox) is proved for a Claude run, with scratch, sessions and credentials excluded as for Codex.

**Problem (The confinement cutover repeats for Claude and disables its inner sandbox)** · `prob:box:claude-confinement-cutover-disables-the-inner-sandbox`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) The confinement exercise case is repeated for Claude, showing the mode, an in-home destructive canary and refused outward and mount escapes, and the Claude program's inner sandbox and command approvals are then disabled for new boxed dispatches, because the box's confinement admits no inner sandbox and replaces it.

Folded from ``open:homes:claude-lane-home-sandbox-redirects``, whose question the boxed form answers: a host Claude lane's sandbox ran without network any ssh call whose command text carried a stdin redirect or a command substitution, and any lane script that called ssh inside it, because the sandbox exclusion and the allow rule match a command's first words, so a `time`-prefixed ssh call and a pipeline whose first word is `git` ran inside the sandbox (`Network is unreachable` within 15 ms, six calls in one loop's work step; lane scripts such as ``gate.sh`` and ``render.sh`` could not reach the node either). In a box the node is reached through the door route rather than an excluded ssh, so no exclusion list remains to decide; until the cutover a host Claude lane calls ssh directly with a literal command, as the loops did.

(2026-11-05, lane r002g79's review c1) A review lane under the host Claude sandbox had `git grep` refused by the permission gate while plain `grep` passed; the boxed form replaces the inner sandbox and its permission rules, so the finding is evidence for this cutover rather than an allow-list edit.

**Problem (The Claude lane route leaves the runtime wrapper)** · `prob:box:claude-lane-route-leaves-the-runtime-wrapper`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:box:claude-boxed-workers`)) The Claude lane route stops launching through the runtime wrapper's Claude entry as the Codex route did, and the wrapper's global disposition, kept only where an owner's interactive use still calls it, is checked by the remaining-caller and live-install test that the box design names.

### Tests under load · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-gates`

**Problem (Timing-bound tests of the lane gateway fail under load in gates of candidates that do not touch it)** · `prob:gates:gate-timing-flakes`

**OPEN.** 2026-09-29, five occurrences recorded, and a defect of the gate form with them. The fifth: the gate of lane r002d21's candidate `c3d5a0e`, which touches only the chat export's package and one runbook passage, failed on sykora in 692.4 s with 2,758 tests passed and 4 failed, all four in the permission-triage package's topology tests (`model_prose_naming_a_prompt_is_not_prompt_evidence`, `real_hook_tool_failure_is_invalid_and_blocking`, `real_hook_trials_retain_raw_output_exit_and_separate_walls`, `every_trial_requests_and_retains_one_json_result_document`); the re-run on the same node, by then the lighter one, passed in 599.0 s with 2,762 tests. These tests start real processes and keep their walls, so they belong to the same family as the gateway's. The defect: the gate's log names a failed test and the line that panicked, and the message of the panic stays in a temporary file that is gone when the gate ends, so a failure that does not repeat cannot be diagnosed from what is kept. The gate form is to copy the failing tests' output into its log. What follows is the earlier status, kept as written. **OPEN 2026-09-29, four occurrences recorded.** A gate that fails on a test of a package the candidate does not touch costs a re-run of ten minutes and teaches nothing about the candidate. Occurrences: `approve_executes_exact_parked_argv` on orel at 88af917 and again on orel at the merged main b0d297e (gate total 637.8 s, load average near 20); `heartbeat_stays_fresh_during_long_runner` and `serve_loop_shuts_down_promptly` on sykora at 1c9d5f2 (gate total 709.0 s); and `every_trial_requests_and_retains_one_json_result_document` of the permission triage on sykora at 532e3a3. Each test asserts a bound in wall time on a node that runs other loops' work. The fix is of one kind: a test of ordering asserts the order through the program's own events and not through elapsed time, and a bound that must be in time is scaled by a factor the gate measures on the node before the run. Until then a red gate whose only failures are these tests is re-run on the other node and recorded here.

**Problem (Runtime-backed resume fixtures cannot run from a node's group-writable target directory)** · `prob:gates:node-target-dir-owner-program-refusal`

**OPEN.** Lane B deferred it to a later lane that places node fixtures; which ancestor fails is unmeasured. The lane's note on the prescribed target directory ends mid-word in the source table ("Prescribed target directory reta"), so its rest is lost.

TOOLS (r002d83 work, measured on the node): a production resume preview from the node's build directory refuses `owner-program` because ``rounds.rs:877-879`` checks the current executable's every ancestor with `pinned::owner_executable` (``pinned.rs:139-161``: owner not the caller, root or nobody, or group- or other-writable without the sticky bit), present at base; the test binary sits under a group-writable ancestor on the node, so runtime-backed resume fixtures cannot run from the node's target directory; which ancestor fails is unmeasured. A later lane that needs such fixtures on a node gives the gate a target directory under an owner-only ancestor or names the refusal as the expected outcome.

**Problem (The lane package's suite is wall-bound, so one gate costs over twenty minutes and a wait can hang it)** · `prob:gates:lane-suite-bounded-waits-and-controlled-clock`

**OPEN.** Lane E landed bounded named route waits, immediate-client failure handling and the separation of slow transfers at 9daa3817, after which the complete lane package took 79.00 seconds; the broader clock and suite policy remains for the design lane: deadline fixtures on a controlled clock, the large and slow fixtures in a separate slow suite, and the default suite under a few minutes.

(2026-10-18) TEST DESIGN (measured across the gates of 2026-10-17/18, HIGH, design lane): a code gate costs 1,250 to 1,580 s of which the cold workspace build is about 40 s; the rest is the lane package's integration-style suite, wall-bound rather than CPU-bound (real git, stubs and services over sockets, fixtures waiting out real 100 ms, 5 s and 120 s deadlines and retry pauses, single fixtures of 10 to 21 s, a 206 MiB route fixture of about 90 s in the default suite; the test binary idles at a quarter of a core), so a profile change gives nothing (e02) and one unbounded wait hangs the whole gate (item ``entry:gates:route-fixture-bounded-waits``). The lane: every fixture wait bounded with a named failure; deadline fixtures on a controlled clock; the large and slow fixtures moved to a separate slow suite run at deploy and nightly; the default suite under a few minutes; gates on warm per-slot targets (item ``prob:gates:gate-targets-per-slot``)

Folded in, three more sleep and deadline tests of the lane package go red under node load: partly landed, Lane E, 9daa3817, mitigated: the retained-reader observation bound is 20 seconds; the receive-deadline fixture has a 120-second observation bound. Existing timing seams remain; controlled-clock coverage is deferred.

three more sleep/deadline tests of the lane package go red under node load (load 100 on 256 threads at 2026-10-19 10:08Z, index's door measurements running): `container_helper::tests::helper_bounds_reader_after_child_exit` (200 ms seams, 2 s bound), `door::transport::tests::door_receive_deadline_before_packet_records_not_reached` and `door::transport::tests::door_status_deadline_review_and_disconnect_have_typed_outcomes` (wall-clock deadlines); all green alone ×3 (0.58 s) and in their binary (802/802, 94.6 s) at load 79→56 minutes later. Into the test-design lane (items ``entry:gates:route-fixture-bounded-waits``, ``entry:door:route-registration-fixture-asserts-value``, ``entry:door:door-ssh-diagnostic-fixture-production-reply``, ``entry:gates:large-route-fixture-slow-suite``, (`prob:door:request-upload-bound-and-count-together`), ``entry:gates:lane-suite-bounded-waits-and-controlled-clock``, ``entry:gates:route-regression-case-and-poll-accept`` and ``entry:gates:fixture-exec-text-file-busy``): barriers and controlled clocks, not wall seams.

**Problem (two release harness cases fail under node load and when two gates share the ssh-stub harness)** · `prob:gates:release-harness-cases-red-under-load`

**OPEN.** Lane r002f65 landed at c01 4bef755 (tools main 6b49047a, shipped in deploy #52) the harness cases preparing and observing their transport replies before timed polling, both steward stubs serving a snapshot byte range with `exec dd`, the exporter replayed untimed after each client run, and the correspondence stub recording a timeout kill as `EXIT 143`. The two clauses stay open, because in-window work still scales with chunk count and node load (one `timeout`, bash stub and `dd` chain per read under the 800 and 300 ms bounds) and the shared-resource collision is unidentified; since the steward `read status=124` at about 900 ms recurred under three in-window implementations, the next step is the bound policy of ``entry:gates:workspace-move-review-follow-ups`` (e) and running the shell harness only as its standalone Meson test or serialising the cargo wrapper. The feeder clause recurred as the quiet-success case of `release-harness-tests-run-sh` at 2026-10-26T01:04Z, and both clauses recurred on the gate of deploy #63's candidate at 05:52Z and 06:00Z (below).

(tools, release harnesses) two release-suite harness cases fail under a saturated node (five concurrent gates, the shared cgroup at 59 cpu-seconds per wall-second, 320 s of cpu stall over a 440 s wall, 2026-10-23 19:16Z): ``tests/steward-run.sh`` watch content (`STEWARD-TRANSCRIPT-FAILED read\|list server=alpha status=124`, the transport sample's 600/300 ms deadline) in gates r002f56 and r002f59, and ``tests/run.sh`` successful cursor poll (`host=alpha SSH-FAILED`, `FEED-DURATION-MS 397`, the feeder's ssh stub against its deadline) in gate r002f60, and three superloop-correspondence subprocess tests (`failure_isolation_admin_publication_and_deadlines`, `release_harness_keeps_all_correspondence_pins`, `bare_reads_and_second_caller_preserve_and_resume_shared_cursor`, `SSH-FAILED` fast at light load) in a r002f60 gate that ran beside another gate and passed when the gate ran alone, so two gates running the same ssh-stub harness at once collide on something they share; the steward case also failed at 17 cpu-seconds per wall-second with 1.8 s of stall on a solo r002f59 gate at 20:11Z, so it is not only load; both are deadline-sweep members in the sense of item ``entry:gates:workspace-move-review-follow-ups``(e): the fixture observes the event it needs before the deadline is armed, no bound widened, and the sample it measures is the transport, not the node's scheduler; r002f60 c04 prepares the steward watch responses, the feeder case is unhandled. Source: gate outputs gate-r002f56.out, gate-r002f59.out, gate-r002f60.out.

Measured again 2026-10-26T01:04Z on the r002f95 gate of an untouched candidate (GATE TOTAL 199.8 s): `release-harness-tests-run-sh` failed `FAIL quiet success exit was 1` with `captured: host=alpha SSH-FAILED` and `FEED-FAILED all ssh polls failed: ssh_fails=1 polls=1`, while the all-fail and partial-fail census cases passed. The quiet-success case depends on an ssh poll to a fixture host succeeding under load, which makes it a load-shaped red to retry (that gate went green on its third run); the fixture does not reach ssh at all, or the case asserts the verdict it can control.

Measured again 2026-10-26 on the gate of lane r002f97's candidate on sojka, which touched only the Claude sign-in home, with the shared cgroup at 34 to 47 cpu-seconds per wall-second: run 1 (05:52Z) failed the steward transcript case with `status=124` and an ssh-poll case of `release-harness-tests-run-sh`, beside three Meson release timeouts at 33 to 63 s; run 2 (06:00Z) failed the partial-census ssh-poll case of the same harness, which had passed at 01:04Z; the fourth run went green at 06:21Z. Both clauses recur at that load, and the partial-census case joins the quiet-success case among the ssh polls whose success the fixture does not control; the load itself is recorded in ``prob:gates:gate-cpu-reservation-on-shared-node``.

Measured again on 2026-10-29 at deploy #67: run 1 of the deploy's build went red on the steward harness's `status=124` shape in an untouched tree, and run 2 was green in 503 s.

open (r002g48 gate run 1, 2026-11-01 18:34Z). The meson release test `release-harness-tests-run-sh` failed its "partial census" case once: both fake hosts reported SSH-FAILED although the transport log shows the second CALL exiting 0, under node load 34.5 cpu-seconds per wall-second with ConnectTimeout=5; the lane touched only superloop-pack. Retry launched; if it repeats, the fixture's transport exit capture under load is the place to look (the test reads the exit after a poll window).

**Problem (untouched package tests go red on a shared node under other loops load)** · `prob:gates:untouched-tests-red-under-node-load`

**OPEN.** Lane r002f64 (f4dfa28 and 1ace365) armed the registry bounds after fixture readiness and established acceptance before row tampering, deployed as #51 on 2026-10-24, yet at 2026-10-24T01:40Z `registry_capture_waits_for_child_and_both_streams_under_load` and `door_agent_case_uses_production_handler_and_names_unmeasured_vendor` went red again in an untouched tree at node load 103 to 144. Tools package tests in files no series touches go red on a shared node under other loops' load, because their wall-clock bounds assume a quiet node: deadline-sweep members in the sense of item (e), in the load family of items (`prob:gates:release-harness-cases-red-under-load`) and ``prob:gates:gate-cpu-reservation-on-shared-node``.
