The four r002f59 gates of 20:08Z–20:43Z each failed a different untouched test while other loops' cargo tests shared the node, and the topology probes read `elapsed_s=0.000 start` in the r002f57 gate of 19:20Z. The float comparison was red in lane r002f59's FIX2 round and green on retry.

r002f60's FIX3 repeats measured four more intermittent timing reds (1 in 15 or 25): base `failed_group_kill_bounds_helper_joins` (`.child-pid` unreadable after 10 s), base `letters_context_deadline_fits_the_eight_second_harness_timeout` (elapsed under 5 s), `blocking_model_stdin_is_governed_by_the_model_deadline` (expected Timeout), and `letters_deadline_kills_the_entry_group_and_keeps_the_heartbeat_prompt` (``letters_contract.rs:569``, elapsed under 5 s, 1 in 5 on the folded candidate). Measured again 2026-10-26T06:05Z on the third run of the same r002f97 gate: one cargo test went red, the readiness wait of the `superloop` subprocess contract with `private ready: Timeout`, while 4,437 tests passed, and the fourth run at 06:21Z was green with 4,438 at 24.5 cpu-seconds per wall-second.

That loop takes the fix as its next lane, so the measurement stands here as evidence of the shape rather than as work of this item.

Measured again on 2026-10-30 at 12:21Z on run 2 of lane r002g13's gate (592.5 s), whose series touched neither test, with 1,253 passed and 2 failed in the lane library battery. `launch::settle::tests::dead_resumed_round_entry_settles_by_report` failed at ``settle.rs`` line 2418 on the load-independent defect this item names, the two report objects differing only in `wall_seconds`, 1.811064678 against 1.8110646780000001 after the JSON round trip, so its recurrence is the float comparison and not a load measurement, and the decimal-text or tolerance comparison stays its fix. `launch::checkout::tests::checkout_clones_only_verified_preserved_objects` failed at ``checkout.rs`` line 1411 with `checkout-git` `preserved pack transfer failed`, a red seen once before and not recorded by name until now, at 31.58 CPU-seconds per wall-second on the node over the test battery with 36.5 s of CPU stall. Run 3 on the same tip was green ``prob:gates:gate-cpu-reservation-on-shared-node``.

Measured again on 2026-10-30 at 14:12Z on run 2 of lane r002g14's gate, on the fix tip c03 (639 s), launched at a one-minute load of about 146 with other loops' gates and boxed lanes running: 4,490 passed and 1 failed, `door::tests::door_unprovisioned_estate_referral_has_no_effect` reading `door-request-deadline` at ``door.rs`` line 4083 where `door-adjudicated-refusal` was expected, at 22.16 CPU-seconds per wall-second on the node over the test battery and 24.79 over the gate, with 144.0 s of CPU stall over the gate and 15.4 s of it within the test battery. The same name had gone red under load in four earlier gates (`prob:gates:fixture-deadlines-independent-of-node-load`), but ``door.rs`` was a file the series touched, on the answer-wait path, so the red was not retried until the confirmation read had judged whether the change could produce the deadline outcome where the base adjudicated. Its judgement (review 3, CONFIRMED IMPORTABLE): the test gives its whole request a one-second deadline (`deadline_ms: 1000`), its adjudication is written by the `AFTER_WAIT_READ` hook on the same thread after the first read, and when setup, filing, the first read and the hook's synced write exceed one second, `wait_answer`'s deadline check (lines 740 to 760) fires before the second read sees the adjudication, a load shape on a path without lock contention that the series could not reach. Run 3 on the tip c04 was green in 608 s, and lane r002g15 then raised both door probes' deadline to 30,000 ms, shipped in deploy #77, so the family loses that member; a red in a touched file is judged against the change before it is retried, as here.

GATE FLAKES under load (r002g74 run 3, 2026-11-03 06:26Z, node at 31 cpu-s per wall-s): permission-triage `model_timeout_terminates_the_entire_child_process_group` (fixture child pid file not readable after 10.0 s) and superloop-lane `launch::settle::tests::interrupted_completion_report_validates_socket_evidence` (report object assertion), both untouched by the lane; the first is a timed harness (the 10 s bound), the second's differing field is recorded in the log line below. Retry taken (run 4).

**Problem (Door fixtures go red on the request store's two-second reader lock bound while the gate's cgroup reads quiet)** · `prob:gates:door-fixtures-request-lock-reader-bound`

**OPEN.** Measured on run 4 of the gate of lane r002f99's candidate on sojka (2026-10-26 at 10:09Z): four door tests, `door::tests::door_unprovisioned_estate_referral_has_no_effect` and three `launch::exercise::door_modes` cases, failed on `requests-record-refusal: request lock: Resource temporarily unavailable (os error 11)` while the candidate touched only `signin_home`, Meson and the Codex home harness. The reader side of the request store's lock is a bounded `flock` wait of 2 s (15 s for a writer, ``requests/store.rs`` near line 1030), so a writer holding the record over 2 s turns into `door-request-deadline` or `failed_stage: fixture-agent-call`; the same EAGAIN from the two-second shared-lock reader was fixed for one enforcement exercise test by lane r002f40 ``entry:gates:door-enforce-exercise-request-lock-under-load``, and the four tests here are outside that fix. The cgroup figures did not explain it: 10.24 cpu-seconds per wall-second, throttled +0 and a cpu `some` stall of 4.9 s over 644 s, while the node's fifteen-minute load was 66 with two other loops compiling, and the gate's single-test rerun of the vendor case failed again alone in 9 s. Three changes follow: the door fixtures report the reader bound as a measurement rather than a verdict, since 2 s is tight on a shared node and is the production bound of (`prob:door:requests-list-waits-for-request-lock`) met in a fixture; the gate's resource line prints the node's load averages beside the cgroup deltas ``prob:gates:gate-report-carries-node-load-sample``; and the single-test rerun, the right diagnostic step, is a line of the gate report with its outcome.

Run 5 of the same gate (10:22Z) went red on one of the four with the cgroup at 42.28 cpu-seconds per wall-second and a 260 s CPU stall. Both runs fell inside torrust-index's first capped battery, whose contention is recorded in ``prob:servers:per-account-gate-cgroups-on-shared-node``, and run 6 went green at 11:39Z after a load-watch.

Measured again in the gates of the r002g00 to r002g04 series on 2026-10-29: the untouched door fixtures went red at one-minute loads of 150 to 200, beside the topology-ceiling case of (`prob:gates:topology-ceiling-case-red-on-quiet-node`), and the reruns went green once the one-minute load fell under 60. The series' record keeps the loads rather than each red's refusal text, so these reds count toward the load family and not yet toward the two-second bound as such.

Diagnosed on 2026-10-30 by lane r002g13 for the two door_modes tests of ``entry:gates:door-modes-nested-fixture-root-lifetime``: the request lock is per fixture home and its writers hold it through the syncs of the record's leaf and directory while a reader waits two seconds, so on the node's overlay-backed `/tmp` a loaded disk's sync latency consumed the reader's deadline, which the cgroup's CPU figures above could not show. The lane moved those two tests' fixture custody onto tmpfs under `/dev/shm`, shipped in deploy #75; its review read every other test of the door_modes module as building its store with `Fixture::new()` under the caller's TMPDIR, none of them on the timed agent path, and `door::tests::door_unprovisioned_estate_referral_has_no_effect` was outside the lane's scope. What remains here is the same custody for the door fixtures still timed against the reader deadline on a disk-backed root, and the production deadline is ``entry:door:request-lock-reader-deadline-against-sync-under-lock``.

**Problem (The pipe-and-file document test compares a scan wall that the node's load moves)** · `prob:gates:pipe-and-file-documents-differ-in-scan-wall`

**OPEN.** Red on gates of three lanes (r002f88 red 1 at 2026-10-24T21:49Z, r002f91 red 1 at 22:30Z with 1 against 0 and its resume round with 0 against 50, and an earlier red on r002f68) on code the series did not touch.

`stdout_pipe_and_regular_file_receive_same_document_except_clock` (``packages/superloop-lane/tests/driver_follow/subprocess_contract.rs:677`` at tools main 3c0f1a13) compares the pipe and file documents whole, so `cost.scan_wall_ms` (2 against 0 under node load) fails a test whose name excepts only the clock. The fix masks the wall field as the clock is masked, or asserts it separately as a bound.

Measured again on 2026-10-30 in lane r002g17's second fix step: the test failed once in the lane's first full run on sojka because `cost.scan_wall_ms` differed between the pipe and file receives, and passed alone and in the full rerun with no source change, the series touching no driver-follow code. A measured field compared by equality in a test that excepts only the clock is a defect of the test that load exposes, the same shape as the `wall_seconds` comparison of `dead_resumed_round_entry_settles_by_report` in the untouched family (`prob:gates:untouched-tests-red-under-node-load`), so a red of this test is retried as untouched until the fix stated above lands.

**Problem (The topology trials report a ceiling under heavy load as unmeasured rather than as a mismatch)** · `prob:gates:topology-trials-report-ceiling-as-unmeasured`

**OPEN.** Measured 2026-10-25 from 04:38Z to 04:54Z: 19 failures at node load near 170 on code identical to a green gate at load about 45; retried as an untouched load-shaped red.

``packages/permission-triage/tests/topology.rs`` trials assert an outcome but receive `ceiling` when the node is saturated. The trials report the ceiling as `unmeasured` with the node's load sample beside it, so a loaded node reads as a loaded node; the earlier work on this family is (`prob:gates:topology-suite-load-sensitive`), ``entry:gates:topology-censoring-and-tmp-root-fallbacks``, ``entry:gates:topology-guard-measured-not-skipped`` and ``entry:gates:real-hook-ceiling-waiver``.

**Problem (Fixture deadlines in the correspondence and door tests are bounded independently of the node's load)** · `prob:gates:fixture-deadlines-independent-of-node-load`

**OPEN.** Part landed: the door half, lane r002g15's commit 9f595104, imported with its review's amended message as f30aa56 (tools main 445367fa) and shipped in deploy #77 at 2026-10-30T15:12:10Z, raised the referral probe's and the merge-failure probe's `deadline_ms` from 1,000 to 30,000 ms (``door.rs`` lines 4072 and 4134), a bound that outlasts the 15.4 s test-battery stall measured on lane r002g14's gate run 2 (`prob:gates:untouched-tests-red-under-node-load`) and adds no wait on a quiet node, since a passing iteration ends about 10 ms after the hook's adjudication; the review read the merge-failure probe's change as uniformity only, its deadline reaching only the fixture judge, which ignores it. No finite deadline covers an unbounded stall, so the door test's red under a longer stall stays a load measurement. What remains: the bounds of the correspondence pins test and the correspondence watch-deadline test.

Earlier status: OPEN — Two tests measured red on untouched code under load on 2026-10-25.

`release_harness_keeps_all_correspondence_pins` (``packages/superloop-correspondence/tests/subprocess_contract.rs:308``) failed once on a candidate that does not touch the crate, in a gate whose cgroup ran at about 25 cpu-seconds per wall-second (13:34Z), and a correspondence watch-deadline test failed once under load and passed on unchanged code. `door::tests::door_unprovisioned_estate_referral_has_no_effect` fixes `deadline_ms` 1000 on its request and reds as `door-request-deadline` when the loaded node (cgroup 28 cpu-seconds per wall-second, load above 100 on 61 cores) stretches the loop-review path past one second (red gate at 14:36Z on r002f93's tip 1ddb4342). These belong on the list of untouched load-shaped reds that are retried rather than fixed, and their deadlines take a load-independent bound or a clock the test controls, under the policy of (`open:gates:timing-bound-test-policy`).

A unit test whose own steps take nine seconds of durable I/O holds a library battery thread that long and widens the window in which a loaded disk reddens it, so the test moves to the integration battery, or its fixture does less durable I/O per case, one store across a mode's cases or the fixture's custody on tmpfs as for the door_modes pair of ``entry:gates:door-modes-nested-fixture-root-lifetime``, measured on the node before and after. Its deadline is (`prob:gates:fixture-deadlines-independent-of-node-load`).

**Open Problem (A timing-bound test takes its bound from a stated policy)** · `open:gates:timing-bound-test-policy`

**OPEN.** The decision is named by several items and carried by none.

The review of the workspace-move series asked for one policy for deadline fixtures (item ``entry:gates:workspace-move-review-follow-ups``, part (e)): a deadline fixture observes the event it claims or derives its bound from the measured latency. Its sweep landed, but the bound policy itself, how a timing bound is chosen and when a red under load is retried rather than fixed, is cited as open by (`prob:gates:release-harness-cases-red-under-load`) and (`prob:gates:untouched-tests-red-under-node-load`) and has no item of its own. This item states it: either every timing bound is derived from a measured latency with its margin, or the test controls its clock; and an untouched test that reds only above a named node load is retried once the node is lighter, with the load sample recorded.

**Problem (the topology probe suite is load-sensitive on the shared servers)** · `prob:gates:topology-suite-load-sensitive`

**OPEN.** The topology probe suite's ceiling is trial-sized and follows the load of the run it meets, last by the per-run resample imported as `774658f` over `8d306a5` (lane topology, 2026-09-22), and the load-sensitive families found beside it (the gateway's fixture waits, the feeder's fork-to-exec read, the broken-pipe payload write) are fixed across the workspace through `dfedade` (2026-09-21). Still open, carried as a whole by no open item: the resample's window, which narrows the red-gate failure without closing it and is measured by the next loaded red such as the one (`prob:gates:topology-trials-report-ceiling-as-unmeasured`) records, the cached failed resample, the resample on every run from the always-censoring timeout modes, the recording helper's doc-comment overstatement, the corpse preflight that proves the fractional `sleep` operand accepted rather than honoured, the 1 s corpse verdict bound, the hook contract case's undeclared dependence on debug assertions, and the shared cargo registry snapshot that cannot serve the lock until refreshed.

The permission-triage topology suite drives the real probe through fake harnesses with per-trial walls of seconds and progress pipes read under timeouts, so under CPU contention a slow trial reads as a missing progress line, a stale temporary or a crossed ceiling, and gates on untouched packages go red. Every timing bound in a test is one of three kinds and says so in code: a wait on a fixture's side effect (a pid file, a restored lock path, a completing run) carries a deadline of 10 to 20 s that costs nothing when the event arrives; a fixture delay that production must outlast or interrupt keeps its idle value as a floor raised by one measured run under load (`interrupting_deadline`); and a bound assertion keeps its idle value as a floor scaled by a measured sample and capped (`deadline_return_bound`, `ceiling_cut_wait_bound`), with the ordering stated in prose where the ordering is the property. A measured bound is meaningful only when the measurement happens outside the window it judges, a rule stated in the sampler's doc. The ceiling is twenty times a trial-sized baseline sample, raised during the run to four times the largest uncensored observed wall and resampled under load once a trial censors, always clamped to the 2,000 ms floor and the 120,000 ms cap; a censored wall never feeds the maximum, since it equals the ceiling by construction and would ratchet it. Beside it, a test helper that writes a payload into a child's piped stdin accepts exactly `ErrorKind::BrokenPipe` and panics with its context on any other write error, so the child's exit status, streams and log stay the verdict; and a running-process observation waits for the process name in the poll shape instead of sampling `/proc/<pid>/comm` inside the fork-to-exec window.

On 2026-09-19 four gates of record failed timing-bound permission-triage tests in candidates that did not touch the package (a gateway change on tjaldur at load 25–39 while the node compiled a C++ toolchain, six topology trials timing out at `invocation_wall_ms` 2,006 against the 2,000 ms floor; a driver change on falki at load 25–29; a feeder test change twice on tjaldur under a lane's synthetic load, 3 and then 11 failures), each green on re-run on the lighter node, because the baseline sensor read a bare version spawn, 3 ms idle and 3 ms at a one-minute load of 40 alike, and the ceiling was the floor in every recorded run. Lane loadsense (claude-opus-5 xhigh through the Claude CLI lane driver; read-back 105 s, work 7,151 s over a 5,400 s budget with the overrun stated, a fix round of 1,970 s; tests only; first review NOT IMPORTABLE in 580.8 s, re-review 288.2 s IMPORTABLE WITH AMENDED MESSAGE, two body clauses broader than the code replaced with the reviewer's text at cherry-pick; informative gate green on tjaldur in 912.9 s, gate of record on falki 738.6 s with 2,180 workspace tests; imported as `a4821a2` and `ea66945`, patch identical) made the sensor trial-sized: one probe run over the `baseline` roster in the executed mode at the default ceiling, its three graded trials each paying the spawn, the hook round trip and the result read, the maximum wall standing, the ceiling twenty times that; the stderr line gains `samples=` and `max_sample_ms=` and the pure function is pinned at seven points. Under 48 busy loops at nice 19 on tjaldur (the pod's cgroup quota saturated, `/proc/loadavg` showing the host) the previous tree's sensor read 2 ms for a 2,000 ms ceiling and three topology tests failed in a 2,240 s package run; the new tree's read 1,189 ms for a 23,780 ms ceiling and the package passed in 2,333 s; idle it reads 124 ms for 2,480 ms. The lane also classified every other timing bound in the package's tests into the three kinds (a 40 ms deadline under load had killed a fixture before it recorded its child), with the pure scaler beside the reader's unit tests and the two samplers beside their fixtures, because the crate's bite-one guard forbids process and clock imports in the shared test module. The first review found the flake class reintroduced: three bound assertions took their stamp before the first sampler call, so the test initialising the sampler paid three fixture runs inside its own bound's window (about four samples plus teardown against a four-sample bound, invisible idle); the hook bound was uncapped (vacuous above a 2 s sample against a 4 s lock wait) and the delay cap above the 10 s hang it interrupts. The fix round binds the deadline before the stamp at every bound site and closes the window into an elapsed value before the bound is asked for, caps the hook bound and the interrupting delay at 4 s, memoises a failing probe so a censored sample is reported once, and turns the lock fixture's 5 ms budget, a wait for callers expecting a stale lock to be stolen, into a 10 s recovery wait beside the 5 ms refusal delay. Follow-ups it named: `runner_group_recovery_requires_the_recorded_leader` in lane-gateway on a 2 s `wait_for_text` deadline, red on two loaded gates that day; `exits_without_stdin` in the hook tests racing a `sleep 2` inside its fixture script; and a lane running synthetic load makes its node unusable for gates for its whole budget while the one-minute average between batches tells `node-load` otherwise, so a gate goes to the other node meanwhile.

Also 2026-09-19, lane waitres (claude-opus-5 xhigh; read-back 85 s, work 3,235 s over a 2,400 s budget, the overrun in the workspace test section; tests only; gate of record on tjaldur 713.5 s with 2,203 workspace tests; review read-only 665.0 s IMPORTABLE, every wait site checked against the file's nineteen clock stamps and the probe fixture's bytes derived at 4,290 unread in a 12,482-byte file (the brief's figures 38 too large); the review's one scratch redirect, self-reported and removed, a process finding against a read-only lane; imported as `200137b` and `bc7c566`, patch identical) gave the gateway's seven wait-loop deadlines (2 to 5 s on a published result, a sequence's result, a verdict, a runner's marker text, an accepted heartbeat, a serve thread's return, an unfinished-request record) one twenty-second constant, each panic naming the path or the predicate's purpose and the elapsed wait, the polls unchanged; the runner stub's `.stat` file, read straight after its `.ready` marker although the stub's redirect creates it before `cat` fills it (a race the lane's verifying run caught), is now waited on for the field it parses. Delays that are a test's subject (settle delays before an absence assertion, a promptness bound, a configured termination grace) stay. A three-second bound asserted under a message naming a two-second deadline stood for the record until resid3 derived it.

Also 2026-09-19, lane sensrev (read-back 130 s, work 4,285 s over a 4,200 s budget, the overrun the loaded proof at 2,611 s against an estimate of 1,800; one commit, tests only; gate of record on tjaldur 726.4 s with 2,207 workspace tests; review read-only 601.5 s IMPORTABLE, the shared maximum shown monotone and race-free, the observed term binding only above five times the sample, which under matched load it never reached (3.4× loaded, 4.5× idle), so it serves a sample taken idle before load rises or an unlucky-fast one (idle samples on record 124–289 ms); imported as `6e6a926`, patch identical). The sample had been taken once per test binary while every other ceiling-needing thread waited on the cell, so it saw the binary's quietest moment, and a workspace run beside the suite failed three trials naming the ceiling with a sample well below the cap; the sampled ceiling is now the run's floor and a shared maximum of uncensored observed walls raises it at four times rather than twenty, because an observed wall already paid the hook round trip and the capture, with one progress line per rise. Under 48 busy loops on tjaldur: sample 914 ms and ceiling 18,280 ms, observed maxima 2,808 to 3,109 ms opening ceilings of 11,232 to 12,436 ms, 75 topology tests green in 2,566 s. The sampler's four structural failures (scratch home, probe spawn, verdict parse, trials array) are stored in the cell like the censored and non-zero-exit ones, so each is reported once and never re-runs the probe; a `timed_out` that is not a boolean is named apart from a censored trial; the poll-clock settle has its own 5 s cap below the 20 s deadline it precedes; the zero-deadline pin carries its class marker. An uncensored wall just under the ceiling can raise it fourfold per round, bounded by the cap in three rounds, the designed escalation. The review's non-blocking residuals (the sampler doc's claim, a non-boolean `timed_out` named as absent, two threads printing their rises out of order, one pin exercising an input a run cannot emit, only the main probe path feeding the maximum) went to resid3.

On 2026-09-20 lane resid3 (work 2,955 s of a 3,000 s budget; three commits, tests only, three files +146/−35; gate of record on tjaldur 694.8 s green on the lighter node, load 5.6 against 16.3, no timing failures; review read-only 609 s IMPORTABLE WITH AMENDED MESSAGE, the permission-triage commit's third paragraph replaced; imported as `efd5493`, `9166756` and `a291eb7`, patch identical) bounded the sampler doc's claim to the faults it reaches on its own (five fixture-helper raises before the spawn, a retry after any costing a directory and a copy, never a probe run), named a `timed_out` present as a string or a number apart from an absent one (pinned on three shapes), and made every verdict-parsing site record its walls into the run's maximum, seven sites where there was one. The review found two of the eleven non-feeding sites, the confined run and the isolated-binary retry, grading trials while asserting on exit codes alone, so their walls stay discarded until a case there needs the verdict; the message was amended, and the recording helper's doc comment keeps the same overstatement as a residual with the review's replacement sentence on file. Feeding more sites cannot defuse a censorship expectation, since every censoring case runs the ceiling mode with a literal ceiling; a hung trial in another mode only waits longer, bounded by the cap and announced. In the gateway the runner start-time wait is the parse itself, so a torn `/proc` stat line waits instead of panicking or parsing a truncated integer, and the approval bound is derived from the proposal deadline plus its margin and prints all three values, numerically the old three seconds.

Also 2026-09-20, lane feedflake (read-back 80 s, work 1,515 s of 2,400; imported as 5fbba5d; gate of record on tjaldur green in 679.4 s, 2,227 passed and 0 failed, the workspace test section 622 s; review by a read-only Opus lane in 595 s, IMPORTABLE as written) fixed a second family: three gates of record on trees not touching `lane-feed` failed `a_dropped_guard_kills_and_reaps_the_feeder_it_held` ("the held feeder is running before its guard is dropped") under one-minute loads of 20 to 56, because the case sampled `/proc/<pid>/comm` the instant the spawn returned and read any other name as a departed process, while a forked child carries the spawning program's name until its own image is installed. An exiting feeder cannot produce it: the guard holds the only child handle and never waits, so an early exit leaves a zombie still reading `lane-feed`, and with `--bounce=30`, a fresh mtime and a non-heartbeat last line no release branch of `delta_is_ready` is reachable. The running observation is `feeder_runs_within(pid, RELEASE_DEADLINE)`, ended early by the entry rather than the name: `process_is_absent_or_dead` reads the state after the command field's closing parenthesis in `/proc/<pid>/stat`, so no entry or one reported `Z` or `X` answers no at once, and a guard that killed without reaping still fails. A new case delays its own exec (`sh -c 'sleep 0.3; exec "$0" "$@"'` through a new `Feeder::spawn_command`): red on the old predicate at ``subprocess_contract.rs:751`` ("a feeder still reaching its exec was read as one that had ended", 25 passed and 1 failed), green 26 of 26; package counts 84 lib and 25 contract to 84 and 26, doc 0; `src/` untouched, so no host build. The contract suite run 20 times beside 24 idle-priority hogs on the 128-thread falki: 20 of 20 passed at 4.3 to 4.5 s each, load 8.4 to 26.5, a repetition check rather than a reproduction (the gates failed at 20 to 56 on a 13.6-thread node, and idle-priority hogs cede the processor); a discriminating round needs a red-first arm against `623e4b0`, hogs at default niceness at two to four times the thread count or a `taskset` onto two cores, and repetitions sized to the rate bounded. The same day's other load failures were two topology ceiling cases on tjaldur under a five-minute load of 26 (the earliest trials of a burst running against the floor before any observed wall raised it) and one `orchestrator-hook` pipe case. Process finding: the gate follower's NUL stripping, an escaped `tr` inside a doubly quoted launch string, stripped every zero digit from the mirrored log, so the gate of record was read from the node's raw log and the follower now calls a helper script with no escaping layer.

Also 2026-09-20, lane feedres2 (read-back 75 s, work 2,071 s of 1,800, the workspace test section alone 1,227 s under a peer's load, 1,079 s of it permission-triage; imported as 8237095; gate of record on GATENODE green in 720.9 s, 2,228 passed and 0 failed, the section 663 s; review 495 s, IMPORTABLE as written) closed feedflake's three residuals in ``packages/lane-feed/tests/subprocess_contract.rs``. `an_unreaped_corpse_is_not_a_feeder_still_reaching_its_exec` spawns `sh -c 'exit 0'` through `Feeder::spawn_command`, waits for `Z` through an independent parse (`entry_is_a_corpse`, deliberately not the reading under test), and asserts `feeder_runs_within` answers no under `CORPSE_VERDICT_BOUND = WAIT_PAUSE × 20` (1 s, a tenth of the release deadline, about 19,000× the measured 51.6 µs verdict); mutation-proved, removing the `Z`/`X` match failing only the new case ("the reading took 10.03 s over an entry that was already a corpse, past the 1 s deciding a present and dead entry may cost") and inverting it failing the new case and the two live-feeder cases. The delayed-exec case gains a preflight, `sh -c 'sleep 0.3'`, failing by name where the runner's `sleep` rejects the fractional operand, and a comment before its drop states that the ordering of its two readings is its substance, since `process_ends_within` alone answers yes for a live shell not yet at its exec. Counts 84 lib + 26 → 27 contract; six sections green (fmt 1.5, clippy 12.8, doc 2.4, workspace test 1,227.4, linter check 46.8 clean, fmt --check 0.8 s) plus `linter project` with 0 rewrites. Lane findings: falki and tjaldur carry separate hub repositories, so a lane whose compute node is not the hub's reaches its checkout by bundle over that node's alias; a detached-launch ssh returned in 1 s to over 120 s for an identical command, so a launcher whose return can exceed the tool cap takes a background-safe shape.

Also 2026-09-20, lane hookpipe (read-back 75 s, work 2,426 s of 1,800, the workspace test section alone 1,646 s under a peer's load and run once on a tree differing from the shipped bytes only by formatter whitespace in the support module, the other five sections and the package suite re-run on the shipped bytes; imported as bb41f50; gate of record on tjaldur green in 707.0 s, 2,228 passed and 0 failed, the section 650 s; review 470 s, IMPORTABLE as written) closed the pipe case: `run_hook_bytes` in ``packages/orchestrator-hook/tests/support/mod.rs`` wrote the payload under an `expect`, and the induced-panic case (`ORCHESTRATOR_HOOK_TEST_PANIC`, the one site among 51 hook-helper calls that arms an early exit) leaves the hook before its stdin read, so a parent still writing met a closed read end and failed on the write instead of the log and status it asserts; `run_python`'s three sites waited on the same `BrokenPipe`. Both helpers now hand the payload to a shared `write_payload`. `payload_write_survives_a_child_that_exits_before_reading_stdin` pairs the injected panic with a 4 MiB payload, above any pipe capacity an unprivileged process can arrange (the default 64 KiB under a 1 MiB cap), so the write meets the closed end deterministically: red on the old helper at ``support/mod.rs:203`` ("write hook payload: Os { code: 32, kind: BrokenPipe }"), green on the new; counts 10 → 11 in the contract file and 59 → 60 in the package. The review noted five cases satisfied by a child that exited silently before reading (``subprocess_contract.rs:40``, ``canonical_scenarios.rs:80``, ``project_root_contract.rs:42``, `:169`, `:184`), blind to an undelivered payload before the change too, assertion gaps rather than a weakening; the new case depends on debug assertions and would fail under a release test run, which its sibling's name advertises and its own does not; and the remedy was owed elsewhere: `permission-triage` carried it at ``tests/hook.rs:399-406`` while six sibling helpers wrote under a bare expect (`tests/wrapper.rs:166`, ``tests/journal.rs:269``, `:291`, `:337`, ``tests/hook.rs:1601``, `:1847`), `heartbeat-guard` was the exact twin (its test-panic assert ahead of the stdin read at ``src/lib.rs:43``, induced at ``tests/subprocess_contract.rs:500``, written under `expect("write hook input")` at `:27-31`), and `codex-lane`, `claude-lane` and the linter corpus test had bare writes.

Also 2026-09-20, lane pipesweep (read-back 85 s, work 1,936 s of 3,000; six commits imported as `6f6b38c` (heartbeat-guard), `ae9230b` (permission-triage, message amended at import), `55ab16b` (codex-lane), `047eef0` (claude-lane), `80f7909` (linter) and `a5c8924` (orchestrator-hook); gate of record on tjaldur 711.4 s green, 2,230 passed and 0 failed, the section 651 s, the first full workspace run on these bytes; review by a read-only Opus lane 711.5 s, importable with one message amended) swept the rule across the workspace. `heartbeat-guard` gains a shared `write_payload` for its hook and Python-guard helpers and `injected_panic_leaves_an_oversized_payload_write_without_a_reader`, red on the old helper at ``subprocess_contract.rs:38`` ("write hook input: Os { code: 32, kind: BrokenPipe }", 39 passed and 1 failed) and green on the new, keeping the `Stdio::null()` panic case for a hook invoked with no stdin; `permission-triage` routes its wrapper, journal and hook sites through one helper in a new ``tests/support/mod.rs`` (the wrapper denies a missing instance file or binary before exec, a non-hook argv never reads, the 1 MiB input ceiling leaves bytes queued), with ``journal.rs:337``, `:903` and `:933` file appends left as they are; `codex-lane`, `claude-lane` and the linter corpus test take the smallest local form, each child's early exit derived at main; the `orchestrator-hook` helper's comment names the drain boundary (a child emitting more than the pipe buffer while a large payload is in flight would deadlock). Counts: `heartbeat-guard` 39 → 40 in the contract file and 107 package tests; `permission-triage` 534 green in 852 s; `codex-lane` 39, `claude-lane` 39, `linter` 804, `orchestrator-hook` 60. The review found six `heartbeat-guard` and five `permission-triage` cases asserting only status 0 and empty streams, pre-existing and not weakened. The `ae9230b` message was amended because its subject claimed every child payload write routed through the helper while ``tests/hook.rs:403-410`` kept a deliberate inline absorb, and its body counted four helpers for five converted sites; its review also asked where the review binary's only read, the mark branch at `src/bin/permission-triage-review.rs:87`, belonged. Lane findings: the shared cargo registry under the root-owned toolchain tree lacks crates newer than its snapshot (`block-buffer 0.12.1` absent, `0.10.4` present) and a loop account cannot write into it, so the linter round died with a permission error until the lane copied the readable parts into a lane-local `CARGO_HOME`; ``Cargo.lock`` pins `block-buffer 0.12.1` through `sha2 0.11`, so a read-only shared snapshot is a cache, never a source of truth for a lock newer than itself, and the fix is a root-run registry refresh per pod life or a per-loop `CARGO_HOME` by default; and the piped bundle ship (`git bundle create - … | ssh …`) is refused by the lane sandbox's network filter, so the bundle is written to a file and fed to the top-level ssh by stdin redirect, which the runbook's ship line should say.

On 2026-09-21 lane ptabsorb (read-back 75 s, work 2,355 s of 2,400; imported as `dfedade`; gate of record on falki 783.0 s green, 2,231 passed and 0 failed, the kit self-check converged, read from the node's raw log because the local follower's ssh hung through a host suspend; review by a read-only Opus lane, about 278 s of work inside a 2,822 s span that included the same 30-minute suspend, importable with one sentence amended to name the fixture carrying the comment and the one open-coded mark repeating its override) closed the sweep's two residuals. The `run` helper in ``tests/hook.rs``, the one whose input-ceiling cases write more than a pipe holds, now hands its payload to `write_payload` with a context naming the helper and the payload; no existing case drove it against a child exiting before reading, so `hook_runner_accepts_the_closed_read_end_of_a_non_hook_argv` was added (the file's cases 67 → 68, the harness's hook target 70 because a macro mints cases a textual count misses). The ``journal.rs`` sites do reach `permission-triage-review`, since the two mark fixtures re-pipe stdin over `review_command`'s `Stdio::null()` and both carry `--mark` and reach the read at `src/bin/permission-triage-review.rs:86-91`, so the fact is a one-line comment where the mark cases pipe their stdin. Lane findings: dropping the inline write orphaned an `io::Write` import (one gate bounce, rc=101, 7 s); the driver stamped the step `STALLED-KILLED` at 2,471 s although the report was written and the branch shipped at about 2,346 s, because the child idled after its result, a driver bite recorded under the watcher residuals of ``entry:lanes:lane-step-idle-inhibit``; and helper scripts live inside the working directory because a `$TMPDIR` write is sandbox-denied.

On 2026-09-22 lane topology (gpt-6-astra xhigh after a Claude lane lost three turns to server-side API errors without a commit; work 5,728 s, stopped by the driver as `STALLED-KILLED` at 125 s of quiet past a 5,400 s budget while its third acceptance run still ran on the server, then a 900 s report turn stamped `OVER-BUDGET`, the deployed drivers' new vocabulary in its first live use; review opus xhigh 446 s, IMPORTABLE with five non-blocking findings; the full gate at the pre-amendment tip green on every test, 2,248 passed with the topology binary, and red on the linter root check because four test labels spelled `re-sample` where their function names derive `resample`, fixed in a 230 s turn, the remaining sections green on the amended tip in 60.8 s; imported as `774658f` over `8d306a5`, patch identical) answered the two red workspace gates of 2026-09-21/22: under another loop's load the ceiling stood at about 2.5 s from an idle sample while trials took longer, every such trial censored and censored trials cannot raise the ceiling, so four cases failed with `invocation_wall_ms` of 2,494–2,599. The suite now resamples the baseline once per run when a trial censors, under the load that censored it, with the sample headroom and the cap unchanged and the bounds pinned by assertions. On tjaldur from the shipped commit three consecutive loaded runs at eight threads passed 79 of 79 each (the second in 1,095 s at load 45, the third in 1,116 s at load 54), then the package suite was green with the binary at 79 of 79 in 508.78 s. The lane's own reproduction did not censor (sampled under load, a ceiling of 11.9 s against a slowest trial of 1.1 s), confirming that the failure needs a quiet sample followed by load, so the green loaded runs are a non-regression result, not a demonstration of rescue. Lessons for briefs: a lane whose acceptance round outlasts its budget is stopped by design, so the budget is sized to the acceptance wall or the acceptance is its own step; and test executables on this toolchain live under `debug/build/<pkg>/<hash>/out/`, so a brief takes the path from cargo's own `Executable` line.

Open residue, none blocking: the resample's window stays open by construction, since it is requested only when a verdict is read while a probe's ceiling is fixed at spawn, so the first probe to meet a too-low ceiling and every probe in flight when load rises still complete at the old ceiling; a failed resample is cached and fails every later censored-verdict reader, which needs a baseline trial over two minutes; the deliberate-timeout modes always censor, so the resample runs once on every run, quiet included, at no cost measured. Not closed in this record: `exits_without_stdin` racing its `sleep 2`; the recording helper's doc-comment overstatement (replacement sentence on file); the corpse preflight proving the fractional `sleep` operand accepted rather than honoured (a minimum-elapsed assertion on the preflight would close it); the 1 s corpse verdict bound, the file's only wall-clock upper bound, to relax to two or three seconds if a gate bounces on it; the hook contract case's undeclared dependence on debug assertions; and the shared cargo registry snapshot, which cannot serve this lock until refreshed.

The paragraphs of this item's evidence that name an estate's private details are kept in that estate's backlog as ``rec:gates:topology-suite-load-sensitive``.

**Problem (The permission-triage topology integration tests leave their fixture roots under the temporary root)** · `prob:gates:topology-integration-fixtures-left-under-tmpdir`

**OPEN.** The fixtures `permission-triage-topology-integration-{topology-auto-executed,filesystem-confinement,pre-fixture-refusals,stale-captures,real-hook-tool-failure,infra-model-executes}-<pid>-28` are left under `TMPDIR` after the run: 3,034 directories, about 13 GB under `/tmp/loop-orchestration` on sojka at 2026-10-25T21:05Z, up to 201 MB each, swept by name on 2026-10-25. The tests remove their fixture roots on exit, or the gate form clears the gate's temporary root at its end; the suite's load sensitivity is (`prob:gates:topology-suite-load-sensitive`).

**Problem (The permission-triage topology-ceiling case goes red on an untouched tree at a quiet node)** · `prob:gates:topology-ceiling-case-red-on-quiet-node`

**OPEN.** Measured on 2026-10-29: ``packages/permission-triage/tests/topology.rs`` line 3427, the case `bite_two_generated_counts_and_censored_latency_survive_failure` (an assertion `left == right` with `mode=topology-ceiling`), failed in three gates whose series never touched the package: run 3 of r002f97, run 2 of r002g00 at node load 200 with a cpu stall of +272 s, and run 2 of r002g02 at 08:56Z at a quiet node, with the cgroup at 22.97 cpu-seconds per wall-second, a cpu stall of +17 s and the case alone taking 120.05 s of its battery; it passed on run 3 of r002g00 four minutes earlier at the same quiet. A test that fails on an untouched tree without a load signature has a wall-clock assumption or an ordering dependence of its own, which the load reading of (`prob:gates:topology-trials-report-ceiling-as-unmeasured`) does not explain, and the gate classifies such a red as a retry at the cost of a 590 s gate each time. The case's timing assumptions are measured, since its 120 s reads as a deadline the case waits out; the ceiling it checks is made independent of the wall; and until then its failure shape is recorded in the gate's list of known shapes.

**Problem (A fixture runtime that answers a formatted query is checked once against the real program's grammar)** · `prob:gates:fixture-runtime-checked-against-real-grammar`

**OPEN.** The lesson of 2026-10-29, from ``entry:lanes:container-exited-proof-reads-plain-inspect``; no form has landed.

The container exited-state proof's `podman container inspect --format` template passed every test for a month because the fixture runtime printed the JSON object the test wanted, while the real podman (5.8.9) refuses the template's `{{json .Id}}` (it accepts `{{json .Config.Labels}}`, `{{json .Name}}` and `{{json .State}}`), and the recovery launch's container cleanup never ran against a real podman before the first restart-leftover run reached it. A fixture that stands in for an external program is derived from one recorded answer of the real program to the exact argv the code sends (a probe captured once and kept as a fixture file with the program's version), and a test asserts that the fixture's accepted argv grammar equals the code's; the first recorded probe, for podman 5.8.9, is in ~/.cache/tmp/record-inspect-template.txt.

**Problem (The tests' shell-script fixtures are written while sibling tests spawn, and the lane binary executes them without a retry)** · `prob:gates:script-fixtures-written-under-the-spawn-lock`

**OPEN.** Found by the review of lane r002g08 on 2026-10-29 as a pre-existing observation outside the lane's scope; no run has failed on it so far, and no fix has landed.

The fork-inherited-writer race that the shared `retry_busy_entry` covers for the copied lane binary ``entry:gates:fixture-exec-text-file-busy`` applies as well to the shell-script fixtures that the lane package's tests write and rewrite: ``packages/superloop-lane/tests/failed_dispatch_entry.rs`` line 432 at tools main 51304ac3, where `fixture.runtime(...)` rewrites the podman script in the middle of a test, and the `executable()` scripts of the census and failed-dispatch fixtures. The lane binary, not the test, executes those scripts, so a text-file-busy failure there would surface as a production error that no test-side retry covers and would read as a defect of the code under test. The fixtures write their scripts under the one lock that the test binary's spawns also hold, the idiom of ``entry:gates:stub-executables-written-and-spawned-under-one-lock``, so that no child of a sibling test can inherit a script still open for writing.

**Problem (Two registry refusal assertions outside the refusal fixture table still match substrings)** · `prob:gates:routes-contract-registry-substring-assertions`

**OPEN.** Noted by the review of lane r002g16 on 2026-10-30 as its N2, with no action required at the import since neither text changed; no change has landed.

Lane r002g16 made every expectation of `registry_refusal_fixtures` an exact match of the report's whole `reason` field, so trailing text such as the dropped count suffix can no longer pass ``entry:gates:routes-registry-field-count-refusal-fixture``. Two registry refusal assertions outside that table still match substrings (``packages/superloop-lane/tests/routes_contract.rs`` at tools main fcae5c2e): line 170, the `session_identity` block, which joins stdout and stderr across four callers including the Codex backend's wrapped `superloop-lane signin-home:` text, where an exact match of the joined output is not natural, and lines 1000 and 1001, the heading check `registry-table: missing ## Node routes`. Each asserts the exact refusal line it expects, the wrapped caller's line taken whole from its own stream, so a change to either text fails the test that names it.

**Problem (Four tests go red in the gate of a series that does not touch them, two on a float comparison, one topology count and one broken pipe)** · `prob:gates:load-sensitive-tests-made-deterministic`

**OPEN.** Measured on 2026-10-31 in gate runs 2 and 3 of lane r002g25, the broken pipe again in gate run 2 of lane r002g30, and a fifth test in r002g30's worker rounds; no change has landed.

Earlier status: OPEN — Measured on 2026-10-31 in gate runs 2 and 3 of lane r002g25; no change has landed.

Gate runs 2 and 3 of lane r002g25, on c02 8e156807, a `trim_start()` and a doc comment in the sign-in check with a contract paragraph, went red on tests in files the series does not touch, while run 1 on c01 had been green and run 4 on the same tip was green ``entry:waves:deploy-87-signin-check-varied-lines-installed-silently-2026-10-31``. Run 2 (702.8 s, at 49.1 CPU-seconds per wall-second on the node with 288.4 s of CPU stall) failed permission-triage's `bite_two_generated_counts_and_censored_latency_survive_failure` (``tests/topology.rs:3427``, `mode=topology-ceiling`, a count of 1 where 3 was expected) and superloop-lane's `launch::settle::tests::dead_resumed_round_entry_settles_by_report` (``settle.rs:2418``) and `launch::settle::tests::interrupted_completion_report_validates_socket_evidence` (``settle.rs:2255``); run 3 (549.0 s, at 16.5 on the node) failed `door::compute_tests::door_compute_queue_deadline_and_disconnect_have_no_effect` with `BrokenPipe` at ``compute_tests.rs:1055``. Read against the gate log, the two settle reds are not load: each pair of report objects differs only in `wall_seconds` after a JSON round trip (1.929450451 against 1.9294504510000001, and 1.554553655 against 1.5545536549999999), the load-independent float comparison that (`prob:gates:untouched-tests-red-under-node-load`) already names for these same two tests (its ``settle.rs:2250`` assertion is `interrupted_completion_report_validates_socket_evidence` before the file moved), still unfixed. The topology count is the case of (`prob:gates:topology-ceiling-case-red-on-quiet-node`), and the broken pipe reads as a write meeting a peer that has already closed, in a test whose subject is a disconnect. Each red cost a whole gate rerun of 550 to 700 s. One tools lane makes the four deterministic: the settle reports compared by the decimal text of `wall_seconds` or a stated tolerance, the topology ceiling's count bounded below rather than exact, or reported as unmeasured under load (`prob:gates:topology-trials-report-ceiling-as-unmeasured`), and the compute test's write tolerating the peer's early close, so that a red on these names reads as a defect again.

Measured again on 2026-10-31: gate run 2 of lane r002g30 on c03 went red in 628.3 s with 4,523 passed on the same compute test's `BrokenPipe` at ``compute_tests.rs:1055``, in superloop-lane, which the lane did not touch, with the node at 40.49 CPU-seconds per wall-second and r002g29's gate beside it, and run 3 was green (`prob:estate:tools-repository-publication-cleanup`). A fifth test joined the family in r002g30's worker rounds: ``packages/superloop-lane/src/door/agent.rs:1133`` expects a constant 137,075 bytes of stderr from its script and once observed 137,106, in a package the lane did not touch, passing in isolation and in the final run; the 31 stray bytes came from outside the fixture, so the assertion prints the stray bytes when it fails, and the lane that makes the four deterministic takes it as the fifth.

**Problem (The outbox's owner-private reservation test is red in lane workspaces and in 12-thread worker runs while the gate passes it)** · `prob:gates:outbox-owner-private-reservation-red-in-lane-workspaces`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-03 15:45Z, from lane r002g76's B completion report) The cause of the red in worker checkouts is found: the node directory that holds the loop's lane checkouts is group-writable under a parent that is not, and the production executable-custody validation refuses a group-writable ancestor, so the test is red there and green in the loop gates, whose workspaces sit under a directory that is not group-writable. The fix of the directory is the estate's provisioning of a worker lane workspace (``prob:gates:node-lane-checkouts-created-without-group-write`` in the estate backlog); the fixture's dependence on its environment's modes, below, stays this repository's. The earlier status follows.

Earlier status: OPEN — Measured in the worker runs of lanes r002g27 to r002g32 on 2026-10-31; no change has landed, and the red is accepted in worker runs meanwhile.

`driver::follow::outbox::tests::loop_watcher_uses_durable_rounds_with_owner_private_reservation` (``tests.rs:476``, left 1 right 2) failed in every one of r002g32's 12-thread worker runs, in both trees and all three rounds, while the gate's battery passed it, run 1 on c21 green, and the lane touched no superloop-lane file ``entry:estate:cut-tree-acceptance-before-first-publication``. r002g27 and r002g29 reported the outbox's owner-private red in every node round as the accepted red, and r002g29's reviewer traced it to the lane workspace's ownership: ``outbox.rs:413-419`` falls back to the real owner under the injected caller, and the test fails identically with only that lane's first commit (`prob:estate:tools-repository-publication-cleanup`). A lane finds the shared state or the timing that the worker's run exposes and makes the test hold there, so that a red on its name reads as a defect again.

open (r002g46 review 1, N1, 2026-11-01 16:5xZ). `loop_watcher_uses_durable_rounds_with_owner_private_reservation` (``packages/superloop-lane/src/driver/follow/outbox/tests.rs:476``) injects identities for three directories only; the others the fixture creates keep their real owner, group and mode, and `Identity::search` (``outbox.rs:374-408``) judges them for the injected uid by the real group or other search bits, so the expected two lines depend on what the environment gives new directories under TMPDIR (umask, a default ACL, a setgid group). It failed at the unchanged base under the lane's TMPDIR (also with a umask-022 probe) and passes in the gates' temp roots on the same main. Remedy: the fixture sets explicit modes or injects identities for every directory it creates; confirm the cause once with getfacl/stat on a lane TMPDIR. Patch class.

TOOLS test (r002g59 deviation): `loop_watcher_uses_durable_rounds_with_owner_private_reservation` expects two notice lines and gets one on the untouched base 64466187 under the lane's node environment (exit 101, 43.7 s); the gate on the same base passes it (r002g58 run 2: 4,596 passed). Environment-dependent: compare the lane's TMPDIR/PATH/cgroup with the gate's; if the gate on r002g59 passes, record as a lane-environment finding, else a fix row.

LANE ENVIRONMENT (r002g59 item 4): the watcher test's single-line failure under the lane env points to an OUTBOX REFUSAL line; it depends on the canonical $TMPDIR (fixture directly under it; driver_dir equality), (st_dev, st_ino) agreement between fs::metadata and fstat on O_PATH descriptors, and the real euid/groups; the gate passes the same base. Captured: ``packages/superloop-lane/src/driver/follow/outbox/tests.rs:476:5: assertion left == right failed, left: 1, right: 2`` on base 30d7697a under the lane env (TMPDIR under lanes/, lane uid); the gate on the same base passes. Lane-environment row; the node dir is swept.

LANE ENVIRONMENT (r002g64 review N2): the failing watcher test's logs record "shared-ancestor 0775 safety refusals": the one output line is an outbox refusal on the first scan, tied to the lane's TMPDIR under lanes/ (group-writable ancestor); the gate's TMPDIR differs. Lead for the lane-env row: give lanes a TMPDIR whose ancestors are not group-writable, or relax the test's ancestor check for the lane environment.
