**Problem (A gate names a candidate's new dependency edge at setup instead of reporting a battery red)** · `prob:gates:gate-setup-names-a-lock-change`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-03 09:00Z, lane r002g76) The first dependency-edge change since the public-history switch: a candidate that adds a dependency edge fails the gate at setup under `--locked`, because ``Cargo.lock`` lives only on the private build-inputs branch. The gate form could detect a lock change at setup and name it as such, not as a battery red. The loop's own step, regenerating the lock offline in the merged gate workspace and committing it signed on a lock branch of the slice worker's own, gated as that worker's overlay, while the mainline private branch takes the lock at the landing merge, and the slice brief's report of every new dependency edge are the estate's runbook rows for the slice form (``prob:docs:runbook-rows-for-the-slice-paradigm`` in the estate's backlog).

**Problem (A fixture finds the POSIX shell instead of assuming Debian's)** · `prob:gates:fixtures-find-the-posix-shell-instead-of-assuming-dash`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, lane r002g79's full workspace run on the root-full node as the orchestration account, 462 s, five targets red and none touched by its change) A superloop-lane fixture executes ``/usr/bin/dash``, which Fedora CoreOS does not ship, so the test goes red on the node while the code under test is correct: a fixture that assumes Debian's shell is a fixture defect. The fixture derives its shell from the real program it stands in for, or uses the POSIX shell by its path, so that it passes wherever the program it tests runs; it is one of the root-full node's acceptance debts (`claim:gates:confined-login-reds-are-namespace-and-terminal-differences`).

**Claim (The confined login's untouched reds are namespace and terminal differences, to be measured before they are called defects)** · `claim:gates:confined-login-reds-are-namespace-and-terminal-differences`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, lane r002g79's full workspace run on the root-full node as the orchestration account, 462 s, five targets red and none touched by its change) The tools workspace test on a Fedora CoreOS node under a confined loop account has untouched reds that the loop's own gate never reached, because its Meson slow suite failed first. Read against the node, they fall into three classes. A provisioning gap: loop-home's five publication copy tests refuse with `publication copy fixture requires a second mapped supplementary group`, the account carrying only its own group, which is the estate's kit row and operations step (``prob:gates:mail-copy-path-tests-need-a-second-supplementary-group`` and ``prob:servers:orchestration-account-gains-a-second-supplementary-group`` in its backlog). A Debian assumption: a superloop-lane fixture executes a shell the node does not ship (`prob:gates:fixtures-find-the-posix-shell-instead-of-assuming-dash`). And the rest, which this claim holds: superloop-lane's three overflow-ancestor assertions, the namespace uid map's item (`prob:homes:ancestor-trust-resolves-the-namespace-uid-map`), and its empty-reply and writable-observation assertions; lane-gateway's `replacement skipped TERM forwarding`; permission-triage's journal test, `scope validation failed: loop scope contradicts a protected target or unresolved identity/effect`; and superloop's subprocess-contract terminal tests, with `Permission denied (os error 13)` and `entry stop observation: ECHILD`.

The claim is that the rest are namespace and terminal differences of the confined login rather than defects of the code under test. Each is measured on the node before it is called either, and becomes a fixture row of this backlog or a kit row of the estate's by what the measurement shows, because a red read as a defect without its measurement sends a worker to repair code that is right.

Until the debt lands, a slice worker's bar on the root-full node is the touched packages' tests plus the Meson lint and release suites, with the workspace run reported and its untouched reds named, as the priorities register states; the loop's tools gate form states the same.

### Loop entry and sign-in homes · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-homes`

**Problem (The entry provisions every loop's standard roots from one TOML registry and checks them on each call)** · `prob:homes:entry-provisions-standard-roots`

**OPEN.** The design (lane r002c66, 1dca0ac9) and bites 1 to 4 landed 2026-09-26/27 (r002c68 e7131933, r002c70 d26552ff, r002c73 56a597f2, r002c76 e4c54068): the TOML registry, the ensure step on every entry, and `verify` checking the account registry and the env file. Open ``dec:loopregistry:migration-order``: the permission-triage hook and assembler, the Codex home builder and the other env-file readers move to the registry, the loops.d env files retire after the last reader, and the owner-run chains lose their directory steps; at tools main 3c0f1a13 loops.d is still read by permission-triage, superloop-chat-export, superloop-lane signin_home and loop-codex-home (``packages/loop-home/README.md`` line 76).

The loop registry was seven `loops.d/<name>.env` files read by the entry, the permission-triage parser, `run-worker` and the Codex home builder, while the uid of record lived in a second, owner-run registry (`~/.claude/superloop/accounts/<name>/uid`), and each tier of a loop's roots (`~/.var/loops/<loop>/` with `private/`, `shared/` and `src/`, `~/.config/loops/<loop>/` with `private/` and `lanes/`, `~/.cache/loops/<loop>/`) was made by a hand-written chain under `podman unshare` while `provision` made a flat home only, leaving the estate uneven. The decision record `adr/029-loop-registry-and-standard-roots.md` fixes one registry of record, `~/.config/loops/registry.toml` under the namespace `org.superloop-tools.loophome.registry`, named to the entry by `ENTER_LOOP_REGISTRY` (`ENTER_LOOP_ROOT` keeping its meaning until `run-worker`'s bite), owner-owned inside the custody root and shaped like the repository's `.linter/` files (a namespace and version header, then one row per loop with name, uid, launch directory, session, an optional worker uid run and a transitional `legacy` field for a home not yet at its standard place), from which every standard root derives by name and uid alone. The estate layout's loop roots table is the specification the tests pin: the three partitions, the loop root and its tiers (the root and `runs/` owner-owned at 0711 until the lane box moves the drivers' writes through the entry), the XDG directories beneath `private/`, the custody root and its per-loop directories, and the cache partition with `tmp/` the one path the entry makes in it (the loop's `TMPDIR`, while `XDG_CACHE_HOME` is the partition), each with owner, mode and whether the entry creates or only checks it; the custody tier is checked and never created, because an empty tier would switch a loop onto an empty keyring. The ensure step runs at namespace root on every form before the identity switch with the custody bind's descriptor discipline: everything opened and checked before anything is made, a wrong mode on the right owner corrected and reported, a wrong owner or a non-directory refused by name with no change, so a mismatch surfaces at the first entry rather than at the first failing signature; `provision` is that step run once. The record rejects, each with its reason, a hand-written chain per tier, the env files as the registry of record, a projection rendered at every entry, ownership correction of existing trees, the entry laying out the cache partition, and a deploy that waits for every home move.

Measured 2026-09-26: the env-file and account registries disagreed for one loop (2005 in the env file, 2003 in the account) until its home move; eight `.var` roots existed, none of the `.config/loops` custody trees, no `.cache/loops` partition, and one root lacked a tier its design names.

The design landed 2026-09-26 as 1dca0ac9 (lane r002c66, opus xhigh through the Claude CLI lane driver: read-back 70 s, work 1,811 s of 3,600; review round 1 NOT IMPORTABLE in 680 s on the loop root and `runs/` being loop-owned against the owner-run drivers, with eight further findings; fix rounds of 655 s and 480 s; gate green on orel at the first two tips in 646 s and 645 s, red at the amended tip in 571 s on a wall-clock comparison in the untouched worker-monitor package (item ``entry:gates:monitor-contract-test-compares-clock-fields``) and green on sykora in 615 s; review round 2 IMPORTABLE WITH AMENDED TEXT in 485 s, two lines applied by script). Each bite ran gpt-6-sol xhigh through the Codex lane driver, was reviewed by opus IMPORTABLE WITH AMENDED TEXT, amended by the superloop and re-gated.

Bite 1 landed 2026-09-27 as e7131933, three signed commits (lane r002c68: read-back 20 s, work 3,026 s of 4,800, fix 465 s of 1,200; gate green on sykora in 614.3 s and after the amendment in 600.7 s; review amendment one ADR 029 line on the loop-home premise, 600 s of 1,800): the entry and `run-worker` read the one TOML registry, validated whole at every read and refused by name with status 2 on each case of the README's refusals row; `enter-loop registry NAME` prints the validated row as compact JSON; the `loops.d` reader and its scan are gone from both binaries, with no compatibility field and no second reader (item ``entry:dispatch:compatibility-paths-are-a-desk-question``). The lockfile gained the one line a package's new dependency needs, which the brief had wrongly ruled out; the lane stopped and asked rather than break either rule.

Bite 2 landed 2026-09-27 as d26552ff, two signed commits (lane r002c70: read-back 30 s, work 2,186 s of 4,800; gate green on sykora in 621.8 s and after the amendment in 617.0 s; amendment two lines of the class-root check, 660 s of 1,800): the entry binary's private step `ensure-roots UID NAME HOME REPORT` in a new `loop-home` module, with the standard roots as a data table (23 rows without the custody tier, 27 with it, `HOME/.config` opened only as the parent of its partition), the two-pass discipline (`openat2` beneath the parent's descriptor under `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` and `RESOLVE_BENEATH`, every existing row opened before any is checked, `mkdirat` at 0700 then `fchownat` and `fchmodat2` under `AT_EMPTY_PATH` on the pinned descriptor, corrections last, the loop root widened to 0711 only when `private/` exists), and a core taking the two expected uids as parameters so the tests run unprivileged; `fchownat` comes from the `nix` wrapper (its `user` feature) and `fchmodat2` from the raw system call through `libc`, one lock line.

Bite 3 landed 2026-09-27 as 56a597f2, three signed commits (lane r002c73: read-back 20 s, work 2,612 s of 5,400; gate green on sykora in 641.4 s and after the amendment in 617.1 s; amendment four lines and two commit bodies, 680 s of 1,800): the ensure step runs in every entry ``dec:loopregistry:ensure-every-entry`` as the outer script's first command after `set -e` and the `h=` assignment, before the `private/` test and any mount, so a refusal ends the entry with its status; the entry creates the report file at mode 0600 in the caller's temporary directory before podman, reads and removes it after, and `ENTER-REPORT` and its JSON form carry `created` and `corrected`, the step closing its report with `wall MICROS`. `provision` is the ensure step alone in one `podman unshare sh -c` and checks an existing home ``dec:loopregistry:provision``; `verify` carries `ensure` with the created and corrected paths in report order and the step's wall in microseconds ``dec:loopregistry:ensure-report``. Ownership is checked as uid and gid pairs and the class roots from the `fstat` of the descriptor that opened them; a counting test pins one open and one `fstat` per row plus one of each for the caller's `.config`, 28 with a custody tier and 24 without.

Bite 4 landed 2026-09-27 as e4c54068, three signed commits (lane r002c76: read-back 20 s, work 1,991 s of 4,800; first gate red on sykora in 560.6 s on a timing bound in an untouched package, then green on orel in 672.4 s; amendment one README line, 525 s of 1,800, re-gated in 666.0 s): `verify` reports `account_uid`, the uid the owner-run account registry holds for the loop (null when the file is absent; a uid other than the row's ends `verify` with status 1 naming both), and `registry_drift`, every difference between the env file and the row over the schema's five keys (null when absent, no status change) ``dec:loopregistry:account-check`` ``dec:loopregistry:consumer-migration``; bite 3's three unpinned behaviours (a wall-only report's JSON, the entry-level error naming the report path, the report file under `TMPDIR`) are pinned, and the documents state that the step opens the three class roots by path and each partition beneath its class root's descriptor.

**Goal (A lane reads the forge through a read-only wrapper of the entry, never through a network exclusion)** · `goal:homes:read-only-forge-wrapper`

**OPEN.** 2026-09-28; raised by a peer loop's design question. A study or scouting lane that reads a forge (issues, pull requests, actions, contents) today receives everything as placed evidence, because a lane home runs sandboxed with no network and no forge client, and the loop's forge sign-in is reachable only through a toolbox the lane may not spawn. The shape that keeps one door: a second, read-only forge grant lives in the loop's custody tier beside the signing one (`~/.config/loops/<loop>/private/gh-ro/`, loop uid, 0700; the pre-custody sibling of the present `gh-loops` directory until the custody chain of item ``prob:homes:signing-keys-per-loop`` lands), a fine-grained token on the loop's own account scoped to named repositories with read-only permissions, per-loop as every forge credential is (item ``entry:homes:per-repo-git-identity``); a wrapper `loop-gh-ro` enters the loop and execs the deployed forge client with that directory as its configuration, so the lane names the wrapper in its sandbox exclusions as it names its ssh aliases, and neither the client nor the network is excluded; the forge client is a deployment in the loop home's tool directory with a provenance file, as the key-custody document requires for any forge command run as the loop. Review lanes keep placed evidence, because a verdict recomputed from inputs the import checks must not widen its inputs mid-review; the route is for study and scouting lanes. The wrapper is generic (the loop name is its argument), lands in the tools repository beside the existing per-loop forge wrapper, and each loop's runbook cites this entry rather than restating the shape.

**Problem (A fresh loop's first entry: its temporary and cache paths do not exist, and the report of its roots is hard to read)** · `prob:homes:fresh-loop-entry-frictions`

**OPEN.** **OPEN 2026-09-29, found by the trial loop in its first three commands after it was provisioned; the first is repaired by hand for that loop and owed as a fix in the entry.** The trial loop was registered (uid 2009, 64 worker identities from 3000) and provisioned by the entry, which created 18 roots; `verify` then reported nothing to create and nothing to correct in 0.4 s. One: inside an entry `TMPDIR` names `private/.cache/tmp` and `XDG_CACHE_HOME` names `private/.cache`, and neither exists in a freshly provisioned home, so anything that makes a temporary file or uses a cache fails; `verify` does not list them among the roots, so the check and the environment disagree. The older loops have the directories because they were made by hand when their homes were reshaped. The entry's environment and its table of roots are one statement in two places and must agree: either the two directories join the provisioned roots, or the variables name the cache partition the entry already provisions (`~/.cache/loops/NAME/tmp`), which the record on the registry and standard roots gives as their place. Repaired for the trial loop by creating the two directories at mode 0700 through its entry. Two: the report of `verify` gives the owner of session-owned paths as uid 0, which is the user namespace's view of the session's uid, while its separate field for the runs root gives the host's uid; a reader takes the first for root. The report gives one view throughout and says which. Three: the custody tier's directories, which appear only with the key move that fills them, are reported as missing with the action `nothing`, which reads the same as present and in order; an action of their own says that their absence is expected. Four: `registry` prints no report line with its wall, while `verify` and the entry do; measured at 0.003 s.

**Problem (The sign-in scratch fixtures bypass the binary entry and its namespace-root switch)** · `prob:homes:sign-in-fixtures-through-binary-entry`

**OPEN.** A check of the tree found the sign-in scratch fixtures still calling `sign_in::converge` directly, so convergence through the namespace-root switch and the binary entry is unexercised; what remains is a binary-entry fixture, which needs a namespace root available to the tests or a test-only route into `_install-private`.

TOOLS (r002d89 round one, next step; with items ``entry:door:door-sign-in-placement-route`` and ``entry:door:sign-in-policy-distinct-uid-table``): the sign-in scratch fixtures in ``tests/private_entry.rs`` call `sign_in::converge` directly; `_install-private` refuses `rootless namespace root is required` (``main.rs:1446-1451``) before descriptor receipt, and the door's fixture entry registers the caller's own uid, so convergence through the namespace-root switch and the binary entry is unexercised; a binary-entry fixture needs a namespace root available to the tests (desk: unshare/podman environment) or a test-only route into `_install-private`.

**Problem (The worker key's placement into the custody tier has no tool, so it lands on the owner)** · `prob:homes:worker-key-placement-tool`

**OPEN.** loop-home's ``ensure.rs`` names the `lanes/keys` destination, but no tool is verified to place the worker key there, so the step still lands on the owner at every birth; what remains is `loop-keygen` writing the worker key's copy into the custody tier itself with the owner uid and mode the box expects, or `loop-codex-home` linking it from the private tier, with the custody table naming which.

the worker key's placement into `~/.config/loops/<loop>/lanes/keys/` has no tool: a loop's copy through its entry is refused by its session's classifier as a credential placement (rosylina, "Unauthorized Persistence"), and the superloop never handles key bytes, so the step lands on the owner at every birth. Item: `loop-keygen` (which mints the worker key inside the entry) writes the worker key's copy into the custody tier itself, with the owner uid and mode the box expects, or `loop-codex-home` links it from the private tier; the custody table names which.

**Problem (The any-order hosts test of loop-codex-home checks only that some HostName line exists)** · `prob:homes:codex-home-hosts-test-pins-stanza`

**OPEN.** A check of the tree left it unclear whether the any-order `--hosts` test now pins node-a's stanza (the test file names `node-a-worker` three times); what remains, unless that check settles it, is pinning the HostName that follows `Host node-a-worker` or comparing the whole reversed-order file with cmp.

r002e06 reviewer remark: the "any order" `--hosts` test (``tests/loop-codex-home-run.sh:372``) only checks that a HostName line exists somewhere, so a positional mapping would pass; pin node-a's stanza (the HostName following `Host node-a-worker`) or cmp the whole reversed-order file.

**Problem (The loop-codex-home refusal for disagreeing node values has no test)** · `prob:homes:codex-home-disagreeing-values-test`

**OPEN.** A check of the tree found the refusal for account or key values that disagree across selected nodes still without a test, and no single-node `--hosts` refusal case; what remains is a disagreeing third stanza asserting `cannot resolve user`, and the single-node case.

r002e06 reviewer remark: the refusal when account or key values disagree across selected nodes (``bin/loop-codex-home:281-304``) has no test at base or tip, nor a single-node `--hosts` refusal case; add a disagreeing third stanza asserting `cannot resolve user`.

**Problem (The per-loop vendor lane homes sit at the top of the host home instead of the loop's tier)** · `prob:homes:vendor-lane-homes-move`

**OPEN.** A check of the tree found the per-loop vendor lane homes still at the `~/.codex-<loop>` and `~/.claude-<loop>` form; what remains is the one lane that moves them beside the custody root and retargets every consumer in the same step, then each existing home moved per loop with its settings carried and the layout page's row closed.

LAYOUT: the per-loop vendor lane homes live at `~/.codex-<loop>` and `~/.claude-<loop>` (layout.md open item: "stay at ~ until lane box bites 8 and 9") while the loop's custody root `~/.config/loops/<loop>/` already holds `lanes/keys/` and the box sign-in `lanes/codex/`; the homes belong beside them as `~/.config/loops/<loop>/codex/` and `~/.config/loops/<loop>/claude/`. The move is one lane that retargets every consumer in the same step: `bin/loop-codex-home` (destination and the Claude-home source), `codex-lane` and `claude-lane` (the `CODEX_HOME`/`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the launch forms pass), the runbook's launch forms and the permission allow rule text (`Bash(ssh -F ~/.claude-<loop>/ssh_config …)` matched as written), `lane-inhibit`, the worker charter, the fixtures naming the paths (lane-watch, worker-monitor, rulebook-install, permission-triage), and the dispatch scripts of the loops; then each existing home moves per loop with its settings carried (orchestration's own home holds the edited writable_roots) and the layout page's row closes. Rosylina's homes were made at the `~` form so its lanes run today.

**Problem (the entry's stop-report channel between relay and owner does not meet the pin rule)** · `prob:homes:entry-stop-report-channel-pin-rule`

**OPEN.** The entry-signal follow-up lane landed as `f39db892` with the stop-report channel's reach bounded, and its follow-ups remain: (a) the channel between relay and owner moved onto held descriptors with device and inode compared and the `.signals` name chosen inside the report's retry, and (b) a fixture for the new `relay-stop` refusal; (c), `run-worker` linking the same `ProcessRunner`, was named in deploy #45's letter.

follow-ups of the entry-signal follow-up lane (r002f48, round 2 of 2026-10-23, landed as `f39db892`, fit for the next step): (a) the stop-report channel between the relay and the owner (``packages/loop-home/src/runner/signals.rs:31-44``, `:169`, `:188`, `:231-236`) does not meet the pin rule: the owner holds no descriptor, creates the `<report>.signals/` directory and the file by path, reads and truncates by path (`std::fs::write` follows a symlink at the last component and creates the file if absent), the relay reopens by path with `O_NOFOLLOW` on the last component only, no identity is recorded or compared, and the `.signals` name is chosen outside the report's exclusive retry loop; the reach is bounded (the directory is created exclusively at mode 0700 under the caller's `TMPDIR` or sticky `/tmp`, so only the caller's uid or root can substitute it, and a foreign pre-created name is refused as `relay-stop`), which is why it landed; fix: create the directory and file through a held directory descriptor (`mkdirat`, then `openat` with `O_EXCL\|O_NOFOLLOW`), read and truncate through the held file descriptor, pass device and inode to the relay as literals and compare them after the relay's `O_NOFOLLOW` open, and pick the `.signals` name inside the report's retry; (b) the new `relay-stop` refusal (``src/app.rs:1014-1021``, a missing or relative stop path) has no fixture, since `relay_signals_refusal` fails on the set first; add a valid set with a missing or relative path to the refusal fixture; (c) `packages/run-worker/src/bin/run-worker.rs:12` links the same `ProcessRunner`, so its inherited-io children take the changed dispositions, terminal ownership and stop signals without a relay or stop report: named in deploy #45's letter and rebuilt with enter-loop. Where: packages/loop-home, packages/run-worker; with items ``entry:homes:entry-signal-forwarding-job-control`` and ``entry:homes:entry-forwards-termination-signal``.

**Problem (A moved Codex home keeps absolute rollout paths, so a pre-move thread cannot resume)** · `prob:homes:codex-rollout-paths-survive-home-move`

**OPEN.** The estate's homes were rewritten once by hand; the tools fix is open.

`state_5.sqlite`'s `threads.rollout_path` names the old home after a move, so `codex exec resume` of a pre-move thread refuses `no rollout found` (-32600). Measured on all six homes moved at deploy #56: 665 of 668 threads (blueprint 64 of 65, siedelweber 10 of 11, orchestration 515 of 516). A one-time host rewrite by `S/deploy56-rollout-paths.sh` (a backup through the sqlite backup interface, then a prefix rewrite in every text column) ran at 2026-10-25T00:44:47Z, with blueprint deferred to its idle and tripod rewritten with its move; a resume probe of a pre-move thread answered PROBE-OK in 7.5 s afterwards. The blueprint and siedelweber letters of 00:39Z and 00:42Z are the evidence.

The fix: the move step (`prepare-signin-home --reconcile` or the migration form) rewrites `rollout_path` as part of the move, or the driver resolves rollouts beneath `CODEX_HOME`; and `check-signin-home` reports a `rollout_path` outside the home as a defect.

**Open Problem (The Claude home move's live-driver scan cannot read a desktop host's process environments)** · `open:homes:claude-home-scan-process-evidence`

**OPEN.** The hotfix landed and the design question stays open. The Claude home move's live-driver scan (``claude_scan.rs``) read the environment of every owner-uid process and refused `claude-home-driver-unreadable` when any was withheld, which on a desktop host is always the case (22 such processes at 2026-10-26T00:51Z: gpg-agent, bwrap, editor renderers, zypak sandboxes, the user systemd, ssh-agent, a messenger, sd-pam), so deploy #60's moves could not run; hotfix lane r002f95 (8d5698df) judges an unreadable environment by its command line and reports the count. Open: what process evidence a desktop host can give (a driver-written pid file under the loop's runs root, the run record's driver pid, or the home's own lock) would make the scan evidence rather than a heuristic, and whether a running Claude CLI under the moved home can be detected by its open files rather than its environment.

The move this scan guards is ``prob:homes:claude-sign-in-homes-move-to-loop-config``; the retirement scan's reading of a foreign uid's unreadable environment as absent is ``entry:lanes:foreign-uid-environ-eacces-absent``.

**Problem (The Claude home move leaves links into the previous home dangling)** · `prob:homes:claude-home-move-inbound-links`

**OPEN.** The Claude home move rewrites spellings only inside the moved home, so a symlink elsewhere that resolves into the previous home dangles after the move with no error until its first use: blueprint's Codex worker IdentityFile `~/.config/loops/blueprint/codex/keys/superloop-blueprint_ed25519` -> `~/.claude-blueprint/keys/…`, the only such link across the loops' Codex homes at 2026-10-26T01:06Z. The move preview names the inbound links found under the owner's `~/.config/loops/<loop>/` (a bounded scan for links whose target starts with any spelling of the source), and until then the loop re-points its own link after its move, as its completion letter says; the same shape at the Codex charter's retirement is ``prob:deploy:retired-charter-copy-repoints-old-home-links``.

**Problem (The Codex route render targets a codex child that the door-only loops' Codex homes do not use)** · `prob:homes:render-routes-codex-home-from-custody`

**OPEN.** The Codex render writes a door-only loop's key and known-hosts paths under `~/.config/loops/<loop>/codex/`, while torrust-index's and naturrhythmuszentrum's Codex homes live at `~/.config/loops/<loop>/lanes/codex/` (observed 2026-10-26T02:45Z on the first table render). The renderer takes the Codex home from the loop's registered custody rather than assuming the `codex/` child, or the record says which home a door-only loop's render targets; the render form landed with ``entry:homes:loop-home-ssh-repoint``, and the table it reads is ``prob:servers:node-routes-table-published``.

**Problem (No test pins that a Claude home move applied under a held lock leaves no pending move record)** · `prob:homes:claude-home-move-apply-under-lock-untested`

**OPEN.** Found in the review of lane r002f98 (2026-10-26). Once the move preview holds the destination parent ``entry:homes:claude-home-move-preview-takes-lock``, an apply under a live hold is reached only out of order, by an apply whose preview ran before the hold began, and its outcome, a refusal before any `move-record.json` is written, is stated but not tested. A fixture holds the loop's configuration directory between preview and apply and asserts the refusal and the absence of a pending record, because the recovery of a pending record is the runbook's route, and a record left by a refused apply would send a loop down it for nothing.

**Problem (The Codex home repair's apply is not atomic and leaves staged siblings that no later run detects)** · `prob:homes:codex-home-repair-apply-atomicity`

**OPEN.** Found in the review of lane r002f99 (2026-10-26). The repair apply runs the key rename, then the links, then the files, so a failure between the key rename and its link leaves the home's key path missing (recoverable with `--key`), and a killed process leaves `.repair-*` staged siblings that no later run detects, while the reconcile detects its own `.ssh_config.` leftovers. The apply orders the key link before the rename's effect is visible, and the next preview sweeps or refuses on `.repair-*` leftovers, so an interrupted repair is found by the form that left it ``entry:homes:codex-home-repair-pre-tool-shape``.

**Problem (The Codex home repair's option rule is untested and its runbook paragraph drops the program prefix)** · `prob:homes:codex-home-repair-option-rule-tests`

**OPEN.** Found in the review of lane r002f99 (2026-10-26). No test exercises `--repair` with `--reconcile`, with `check-signin-home` or without a mode; the pre-existing error text for `--dry-run` without any form still reads "reconcile requires…" although two forms now take the option; the runbook's repair paragraph writes `prepare-signin-home` without the `superloop-lane` prefix its neighbour uses; and the release test's no-write snapshots cover the two homes but not `lanes/`, where the apply creates the key's directory and moves the key. Each refusal of the option rule gets its case, the error text names the forms it applies to, the paragraph carries the prefix, and the snapshots cover `lanes/` ``entry:homes:codex-home-repair-pre-tool-shape``.

**Problem (The Codex reconcile resolves its nodes only from --nodes while the other sign-in forms read the routes row)** · `prob:homes:codex-reconcile-resolves-nodes-from-routes-row`

**OPEN.** Measured by blueprint on 2026-10-29 at 07:47Z, with its Node routes row published: `prepare-signin-home --backend codex blueprint --reconcile --dry-run` refuses `cannot resolve nodes; supply --nodes`, while the Claude backend and `render-routes` read the loop's nodes from the registry's table ``sec:servers:node-routes``. The Codex backend resolves its nodes from the loop's rows as the other forms do, with `--nodes` kept as an override, so that one registry row serves every sign-in form; the table's record is ``prob:servers:node-routes-table-published``.

**Problem (The sign-in home command's usage line omits the repair form and the case that requires --key)** · `prob:homes:signin-home-usage-names-repair-and-key`

**OPEN.** Measured at torrust-tracker's repair on 2026-10-29 at 09:01Z: the `superloop-lane` usage line lists `--reconcile` but not `--repair`, and nothing in the help says when `--key` is required, although the repair refuses `cannot resolve key; supply --key` when the worker key is not where the pool expects it; the runbook's repair sentence carries the flag, so a loop reading the help alone cannot find the route. The usage line names both modes and states the `--key` requirement in the words of the refusal, and the surface pins check the help text against the runbook sentence; the option rule's own tests are (`prob:homes:codex-home-repair-option-rule-tests`).

**Problem (The registry-node refusal of a differing --user value is tested on the creation path only)** · `prob:homes:registry-node-user-refusal-check-mode-test`

**OPEN.** Noted by the second review of lane r002g09 on 2026-10-29; no test has landed.

The `registry-node` refusal that names `--user` when the value differs from the loop's routes row is tested on the creation path only; the check mode reaches it through the same shared branch, so it is covered by construction and not by a test of its own. A sibling check-mode test asserts the refusal by name, so that a later split of the branch cannot drop it from one mode unseen.

**Problem (Every Codex lane runs at the vendor's default service tier, and no check reads the tier)** · `prob:homes:codex-service-tier-default-checked`

**OPEN.** Measured on 2026-10-29 at 19:52Z on the seven host Codex homes and in the tools' writers; the setting of record holds everywhere, and what remains is a check that reads it.

The paragraphs of this item's evidence that name an estate's private details are kept in that estate's backlog as ``rec:homes:codex-service-tier-default-checked``.

**Problem (The routes reader's worker-identity form repeats the path rule's dot-component test instead of sharing it)** · `prob:homes:routes-worker-form-shares-path-component-predicate`

**OPEN.** Noted by the review of lane r002g16 on 2026-10-30 as its N1, with no action required at the import; no change has landed.

Lane r002g16 gave an absolute `worker_identity` that fails its rule a refusal form that names the fault, `an absolute path with a dot component` or `an absolute path with an unsafe character` ``entry:homes:routes-reader-refusal-text-names-the-fault``. The classifier chooses between the two by its own dot-component test (``packages/superloop-lane/src/routes.rs`` line 113 at tools main fcae5c2e, `matches!(part, "." | "..")`), a restatement of `path_valid`'s component test (lines 54 and 55), and reports every other failure as an unsafe character in its `else` branch. Both forms are right today because `path_valid` rejects a component for exactly those two reasons; a third reason, such as an empty component, would be reported as an unsafe character. The classifier and `path_valid` share one component predicate, so a new rejection reason either reaches the form or fails the classifier's test.

**Problem (The reconcile keeps a pre-tool home's hand-written header lines as the owner's leading comments)** · `prob:homes:pre-tool-header-lines-kept-under-generated-header`

**OPEN.** Measured at the per-loop reconciles of deploy #72 on 2026-10-30; the lines are removable by hand, and no loop has removed them yet.

The Codex reconcile keeps the leading comment lines of a home's `ssh_config` as the owner's comments and counts them in its plan as retained leading comment lines, so a pre-tool home's hand-written header stays under the generated header after the reconcile: this loop's home keeps 5 lines naming both earlier estate servers and the old registry path (06:24Z) and tripod's keeps 2 naming both earlier compute pods (06:30Z), while blueprint's and siedelweber's keep none. Keeping the owner's comments is the reconcile's rule and is right for a comment a loop wrote on purpose; these lines describe a layout that is gone, and a reader of the file meets them before the generated stanzas. The tool's rule therefore stays, and each loop with such lines removes them by hand at an idle boundary, this loop's five first, with a reconcile preview of `changed=0` and no retained line as the evidence.

**Problem (A loop's Claude lane home carries no keys directory where its face describes one, so its session-alias stanza names the worker key under lanes/keys)** · `prob:homes:claude-home-keys-directory-matches-the-face`

**OPEN.** Measured by wabe on 2026-10-31 and reported in its letter of 12:14Z, at the close of its rename ``entry:homes:rename-attestation-to-tripod``; no change has landed.

wabe's Claude lane home has no `keys/` directory while the loop's face file described one, so after the reconcile under its new name its session-alias stanza names the worker key in the configuration root's `lanes/keys/`. Either the face's description is wrong or the reconcile leaves out a key link the home is meant to carry: a reader who follows the face to the home finds no key, and a stanza that reaches outside the home it configures ties the home to a sibling directory's layout. A tools lane reads the Claude reconcile's writer to decide which holds: if the home is meant to carry its own key link, the reconcile renders it and the stanza names it; otherwise the face template and the faces copied from it describe the stanza's key where it is.

**Problem (The sign-in home check admits the lane clones' .git roots as an expected variance)** · `prob:homes:check-signin-home-admits-lane-clone-git-roots`

**OPEN.** TOOLS (small): `superloop-lane check-signin-home` reports a Codex home as varied on config-roots when `writable_roots` lists lane-clone `.git` directories (the canonical form expects `[]`), although a lane commits in its clone and needs that root writable; feldlerche and siedelweber both met it after the instance-1 reconcile (feldlerche also sees `config-roots-comment missing`). Admit lane-clone `.git` roots under the loop's lanes root as an expected variance, or report them apart from a real variance. From the instance-1 acknowledgements, 2026-11-01.

**Problem (The sign-in home check resolves the loop's active registry rows when no nodes are named)** · `prob:homes:check-signin-home-resolves-active-registry-rows`

**OPEN.** TOOLS (small): `check-signin-home` without `--nodes` reports `missing: nodes, hosts, user, key` for a home whose loop has active registry rows; it could resolve the loop's active rows the way the reconcile does (torrust-tracker, 2026-11-01).

**Problem (The reconciled Codex ssh_config carries its header comment once)** · `prob:homes:reconciled-ssh-config-header-written-once`

**OPEN.** TOOLS (cosmetic): the reconciled `codex/ssh_config` repeats its five header comment lines (torrust-tracker's plan 856c76ab).

**Problem (The reconcile drops project-trust entries for lane directories that no longer exist)** · `prob:homes:reconcile-drops-absent-lane-project-trust-entries`

**OPEN.** TOOLS (small): the reconcile leaves `config.toml` project-trust entries for lane directories that no longer exist; the reconcile (or a named route) should drop entries whose directory is absent (torrust-tracker; also the writable_roots variance row above).

**Problem (The Codex and Claude lane homes render their node routes from the registry's routes table)** · `prob:homes:lane-homes-render-node-routes-from-the-routes-table`

**OPEN.** (measured 2026-11-05 at the handover of the estate's root-full node) ``loop-codex-home … --reconcile`` refuses a hand-kept home, first with `cannot resolve hosts/user/existing-key` and then with `reconcile requires the generated Codex node header`, so the new node's worker stanza and its pinned known-hosts file were written by hand into the lane homes of seven loops, one loop rendered its own from the registry rows, and one home of a session-alias form was left to its loop. Either the home preparation adopts a hand-kept file once, writing the generated header and reconciling from the table, or the registry rows are rendered by one command per loop that the orchestrating loop runs after a node change; the estate's runbook then names that command and its registry paragraph names the renderer.

The parts already recorded follow this item in its slice: the Codex backend resolving its nodes from the loop's rows (`prob:homes:codex-reconcile-resolves-nodes-from-routes-row`), the check command doing the same (`prob:homes:check-signin-home-resolves-active-registry-rows`), the render's custody root (`prob:homes:render-routes-codex-home-from-custody`), and the reconcile's handling of hand-written header lines (`prob:homes:pre-tool-header-lines-kept-under-generated-header`) and of its own header (`prob:homes:reconciled-ssh-config-header-written-once`).

**Problem (A loop with no active route prepares its home with an empty node section)** · `prob:homes:home-preparation-admits-a-loop-with-no-active-route`

**OPEN.** (observed 2026-11-05 at the collection of the compute pod) The node routes reader refuses a loop with no active row (`no active routes for selected loop` in ``packages/superloop-lane/src/routes.rs``, propagated by `prepare-signin-home` and the Claude home preparation), so between a node's collection and its replacement's handover every loop's home preparation would refuse; the collected pod's rows therefore stayed `active` in the registry for the interval and rendered stanzas to a dead address. A loop with no active route prepares its home with the node section empty and a recorded observation, so that the rows of a collected node move to `retired` at the collection, as the status vocabulary intends; that registry step is the estate's (``prob:servers:node-routes-table-published`` in its backlog).

**Problem (Every exact-mode custody reader compares modes through the ACL mask that the mail provisioning leaves)** · `prob:homes:exact-mode-readers-reason-about-the-acl-mask`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, deploy #106, the mail cutover) The mail provisioning leaves an ACL mask on the registry, the allocations file, the mail service configuration and the loops' configuration, mail, private and workers directories, and every reader that compares a whole mode refused them: the lane's private reader (``pinned.rs``, `read_private`), the door's store open (``store.rs``, `open_inner`) and its observed loop identity (``snapshot.rs``, `observed_loop_identity`), the last two leaving the door running but publishing no direct policy. Three hotfixes mask the group triplet in those readers (089a37f3, 59d5acd2 and cbf06ae8), and the door's publication was confirmed after the last.

The proper fix is one ACL-aware comparison for every exact-mode reader, the one the ledger reader already uses (``mail::acl::preserved_mode`` of the loop-home package), so that a reader reasons about the mask instead of ignoring the group bits, with a test per reader over a file that carries a mask. The hotfixes' changelog lines stand in the 0.4.1 patch release (`prob:docs:patch-release-0-4-1-stamps-the-cutover-fixes`), and this fix carries its own entry when it lands.

**Problem (The ancestor-trust rules resolve the user namespace's uid map instead of trusting the overflow uid)** · `prob:homes:ancestor-trust-resolves-the-namespace-uid-map`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, deploy #106) The ancestor-trust rules (the allocations reader's `read_source` and the driver launch's workers-settings and run-path checks in the loop-home package, and the lane home's checks) accept only uid 0 or the caller as an ancestor's owner, and inside a user namespace the real root appears as the overflow uid 65534; the hotfix fa62466d trusts 65534 outright. The proper fix resolves the namespace's uid map, so that an ancestor is trusted when its owner maps to root or to the caller and an unmapped owner is named in the refusal, with tests inside a namespace and outside one.

**Problem (The host lane-home scans are removed with the host forms)** · `prob:homes:host-lane-home-scans-removed`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a part of (`prob:lanes:non-boxed-worker-support-removed`)) The scans that guard a host lane home, the Claude home move's live-driver scan over the owner uid's processes and the Codex home's scans for a live host driver, exist because a host driver may hold a home that a move or a repair rewrites; once no host driver runs, they are removed with the commands that call them, and the open question of the Claude scan's process evidence (`open:homes:claude-home-scan-process-evidence`) closes with them. The loop's sign-in homes stay, as the source the box's vendor proxy reads.

### Server-side packages · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-servers`

**Problem (staff-provision cannot admit a key on a pod account without SELinux tooling)** · `prob:servers:staff-provision-pod-profile`

**OPEN.** A check of the tree found the owner guard still reading the SELinux login mapping with `semanage`, which the compute pods lack, so no key can be admitted to a pod account through `staff-provision`; what remains is a pod profile of the guard (uid, group and home checks without SELinux, home under `/home`) or a documented statement that pod accounts are provisioned outside the tool.

TOOLS (torrust-index door, measured 2026-10-16 05:35Z): `staff-provision --server krkavec --ssh-alias krkavec-root check index` and `admit-key index --key … --dry-run` both refuse after the three read-only probes with `sudo_refused: probe-index-selinux-login through exit 1: sudo: semanage: command not found` (5.7 s each): the owner guard requires the account's explicit SELinux login mapping (``staff_u``) and reads it with `semanage login -l`, and the compute pods carry no SELinux management tooling (the pod accounts also live under `/home/ACCOUNT`, not `/var/home/ACCOUNT`, which the managed-account guard requires), so no key can be admitted to a pod account through the tool. The door's premise (the loop's worker key is already authorized on its pod account) holds for nrz and fails for torrust-index, whose pod account authorizes only its loop key. Options for a lane: a pod profile of the guard (uid, group and home checks without SELinux, home under `/home`), or a documented statement that pod accounts are provisioned outside the tool; until then an admission on a pod is a hand step outside the tool.

**Problem (node-status labels a code-less status as an ssh signal and leaves 137 ambiguous)** · `prob:servers:node-status-signal-labels`

**OPEN.** superloop-node-status still labels a status with no exit code `ssh_signal=N` and leaves 137 ambiguous between the wrapper's kill grace and an external kill; what remains is `wrapper_signal=N` for the code-less case and timed probe reads so that 137 reports `deadline_ms=N expired` only when the wall reached the limit, else `ssh_exit=137`, with fixtures for both.

superloop-node-status labels a status with no exit code `ssh_signal=N` (r002f25 review R1, ``src/main.rs:98-101``, ``tests/subprocess_contract.rs:45-66``): under the real `timeout` an ssh that dies by signal comes back as 128+N, so a missing code can only mean the wrapper itself was signalled, and status 137 is ambiguous between the wrapper's kill grace and an external kill within the deadline. Fix: `wrapper_signal=N` for the code-less case, and each probe read timed so that 137 reports `deadline_ms=N expired` only when the wall reached the limit, else `ssh_exit=137`; fixtures for both.

**Problem (The node-side programs are verified on a Fedora CoreOS node before it carries lanes)** · `prob:servers:node-programs-verified-on-a-coreos-node`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a slice of the root-full platform set, whose lead is the generic repository's ``dec:servers:root-full-platform-baseline-is-fcos-stable``) The node-side programs are verified on a Fedora CoreOS node before it carries lanes: ``superloop-node-status``, ``staff-provision``, the steward kit and its charter template, the run wrapper, and every node build step of the deployment contracts (`spec:deployment:tool-build-and-install-contracts`). Anything that assumes a python3 or a distribution package on the node is listed with its layering or its replacement, and the server toolchains (rustup, cargo, PostgreSQL, Elements) are placed as a root-full node carries them, a system toolchain installed once and each account's own under its home, rather than in a pod's shared workspace tree.

The first root-full node's acceptance gates measured two such dependencies, each an item of its own: the pack verifier's undeclared ``unzip`` (`prob:pack:stage-verify-declares-its-extraction-dependency`) and the cut acceptance test's git date string (`prob:pack:cut-acceptance-compares-epoch-seconds`). The run wrapper was built on the node and installed root-owned with its provenance, and the node carries the SELinux management utilities by its layer, which the pods lacked (`prob:servers:staff-provision-pod-profile`); the evidence is the estate's deploy notes register of its first root-full deploy.

### The published tree · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-estate`

**Problem (The tools repository is cleaned for publication: records leave, estate names leave the code, every tool carries its own version, and the published history begins at 0.1.0)** · `prob:estate:tools-repository-publication-cleanup`

**OPEN.** Parts (A), (B) and (E) have landed, and part (C) has landed but for its lint: re-shaped by the audit of 2026-10-31, its generalization was made by lanes r002g27, r002g29, r002g30, r002g34 and r002g35 between 31 October and 1 November, as stated in the paragraphs before the last, and part (E) is the public repository's root commit tagged v0.1.0 on 2026-11-01 ``entry:estate:tools-public-cut-after-private-reference-audit``; the lint that refuses estate names in tracked files so that they cannot return waits with the tree's burn-register matches, which the publication by the cut did not measure (`prob:pack:tools-tree-burn-register-matches`); part (D)'s per-package versions were superseded for the published set by one workspace version, 0.1.0, which every first-party package inherits ``entry:pack:lockless-public-cut-version-and-public-tag``. Open: part (C)'s lint and the two findings in the last paragraph.

Earlier status: OPEN — Parts (A) c4db350 and (B) tools 4f00b78 landed; part (D)'s binary half landed as r002d77's version handler (item ``entry:waves:six-lanes-after-the-budget-rule-and-deploys-19-and-20``) and per-package versions with bite 4 of lane H (item ``entry:build:dependency-bite-version-line-and-ledger``, imported 978e1ed9); estate values left the library contracts with bite 3 (item ``entry:build:dependency-bite-estate-values-to-configuration``). The audit of 2026-10-31 measured part (C)'s scope, 341 major values in 125 tip files with no critical reference anywhere in the history, and re-shaped it: the operations documents are excluded from a tracked public set instead of generalized in place, so part (C) generalizes only what that set keeps ``entry:estate:tools-public-cut-after-private-reference-audit``. Open: part (C) so re-shaped, with the tracked tree still matching the burn register (item (`prob:pack:tools-tree-burn-register-matches`)) and the lint that refuses those matches; part (E), the published history from a 0.1.0 root commit after decision record 033's closing gate, the form this loop proposes after the audit; and the two findings in the last paragraph.

Earlier status: OPEN — Parts (A) c4db350 and (B) tools 4f00b78 landed; part (D)'s binary half landed as r002d77's version handler (item ``entry:waves:six-lanes-after-the-budget-rule-and-deploys-19-and-20``) and per-package versions with bite 4 of lane H (item ``entry:build:dependency-bite-version-line-and-ledger``, imported 978e1ed9); estate values left the library contracts with bite 3 (item ``entry:build:dependency-bite-estate-values-to-configuration``). Open: part (C), the tracked tree still matching the burn register (item (`prob:pack:tools-tree-burn-register-matches`)) and the lint that refuses those matches, part (E), the published history from a 0.1.0 root commit after decision record 033's closing gate, and the two findings in the last paragraph.

A tools repository holds tools and their usage documents; a record of what one estate did with them belongs to that estate, and a published tree that names a home, a node or a peer loop tells a reader about the estate and nothing about the tool. The shape has five parts: (A) the records, with the runbook's history rows as one document, move to one archive folder of the orchestration repository, outside the label and formatter surfaces because they are kept as written; (B) the tools tip drops them with their declarations, the runbook keeping protocols, templates, timings, the roster and the tooling paths; (C) the live code is generalised in place, every tool staying, with node names, the home prefix and loop names from the registry or fixtures and a lint refusing them in tracked files so they cannot return; (D) every tool is individually versioned: each workspace package states its own version and its binaries answer the version flag with it, each shell tool carries and answers its version, and the release suite's smoke test covers the whole list, which closes item ``entry:build:every-synced-binary-answers-version``; (E) the published history begins at the cleaned tree, one root commit tagged 0.1.0 with no earlier history, the earlier history kept in a private bare as an archive, and provenance records from that tag on naming the new commits. (C) and (D) are the third and fourth bites of decision record 033, and (E) comes after its closing gate: the first installs run from estate-only milestone tags, the rename bites rebuild and install from successive milestone tags, and the root commit is cut once the names are final, so a reader of the published history only ever sees the final names and the milestone tags never reach a published ref (item (`prob:build:binary-names-and-commands`)).

Measured 2026-09-29 at tools main feecdc4: of 784 tracked files about 160 were records of this estate (two brief folders with 75 files and 405 KB; three proposals, one design, one audit and one review, 121 KB; the retired tooling backlog, 30 KB; four live gateway records of one lane; and in the runbook 173 history rows, 236 KB of its 389 KB), the home prefix occurred 216 times in 70 files, node names sat in three scripts and one harness, all twenty workspace packages but the vendored one shared one workspace version, and two of twenty-five shell tools answered a version flag. (A) ran beside the chat export's rebuild, since it touches another repository, and (B) to (D) after that rebuild's import, because they touch the same build lists and declarations.

Part (A) landed 2026-09-29 as c4db350 (lane r002d05, gpt-6-sol high through the Codex lane driver: read-back 35 s, work 760 s of 3,000; gate green on orel in 7.7 s; the archive compared with the export by sha256, 86 of 86 equal): `archive/superloop-tools/` holds the 86 records as written and `runbook-rows.md` with the runbook's one history table (193 rows, 231,657 bytes), outside the label surface by a shape row and outside the census by its pattern; tracked files 52 to 140.

Part (B) landed 2026-09-29 as tools main 4f00b78 (lane r002d11, gpt-6-sol high: read-back 25 s, work 1,615 s of 5,400; its independent reader, gpt-6-sol xhigh, 945 s, importable with one replacement line applied as written; gates green on orel in 630.1 s and, at the amended tip, 610.7 s with 2,732 tests): the 86 archived files left the tools tree, each byte-identical to the commit the archive was taken from, with the runbook's 188 dated history rows (five operating rows stay; the runbook went from 389,909 to 158,784 bytes) and the declarations naming them (four exclusion rows, two owner patterns, four ceilings, the Meson lists and the share map's targets); tracked files 870 to 784. No history row had been added since the archive, so it stands as taken; the published share folder holds the runbook, the worker charter and none of the records.

Part (C)'s first lane, r002g27, generalized every major value of the first audit's lists in 76 files of `packages/`, `tests/` and `infra/`. It ran on Codex, dispatched at 12:55:37Z on 31 October with a 5,400 s budget; its read-back (156 s) found the values list's leading-space defect and took six rulings, its work step (2,069 s) met the golden plan fixture, which the lists had missed, and its fix rounds took 563 s for that fixture and 353 s for review 1's F1. Its eight commits by package covered the identity, permission-triage, command, lane and lint-policy fixtures, the scripts with their payload digests, the golden plan fixture's synthetic uid range, and the fixture registry's worker block moved off the loop uids: nodes became node-a and node-b, loops and projects fictional names, account roots fixture roots, the owner path a fixture home, the uids 2001 to 2004 became 41001 to 41004 with every equality and inequality kept, and persons a fixture author; production code changed only in two comments, the operations documents not at all, and the Apache text and the SPDX notices stayed intact. Measured: 0 private references in the changed files beside 188 false-positive lines (22, trial, wise and the documentation number 2001); gate run 1 on c07 green in 692.4 s and run 2 on c08 green in 551.8 s, each with 4,524 passed; review 1 IMPORTABLE WITH AMENDMENTS with F1 and four observations for the rerun ``entry:estate:tools-audit-rerun-after-first-cleanup``, review 2 IMPORTABLE. It was imported without amendment at 14:12Z as f8b5faf to 217b45b (tools main 217b45b1) and shipped silently in deploy #88.

Lanes r002g29 and r002g30 took the rerun's two lists, both dispatched over tools main 217b45b1, at 14:47:36Z and 14:47:38Z. r002g29 (5,400 s budget; read-back 73 s, which found the steward exporter's root at ``infra/steward/transcript-export:23`` to be production and kept it; work 4,060 s) generalized the 25 files outside the linter as six commits by package: the lane-gateway examples, permission-triage's captured identity and roots, the retained heartbeat reference's attribution, superloop-lane's estate paths and captured identities in 13 files, superloop's door account root and superloop-pack's policy owner example, each stand-in keeping the path depth and the uid shape and the payload ledger rows refreshed where bytes moved, with the steward exporter's production root and the linter's prefix rows reported and untouched. Measured: 4 raw hits in the changed files, all identifier false positives; gate run 1 on c06 green in 723.3 s with 4,524 passed. Review 1 was IMPORTABLE WITH AMENDMENTS: the heartbeat reference is not installed, its Meson variable feeding only the declared-files and census inputs, so c03's message claiming changed installed bytes was rewritten; ``route_tests.rs:616`` got back a root equal to the loop name beside one that differs, the case the rename had lost; and a synthetic agent id got back its captured 17-byte shape. The reviewer settled the outbox owner-private red of the node rounds as the lane workspace's ownership red and found ``packages/superloop/tests/command_door.py`` to be a manual acceptance that no gate runs. It was imported at 16:12Z as 456e656 to 540a4f3 with the two code amendments (lint 11.1 s, wall 32.1 s; tools main 540a4f34). r002g30 (7,200 s budget; read-back 91 s; work 2,710 s; fix rounds 342 s and 152 s) generalized the linter's fixture vocabulary in 52 files: the owners INDEX to SENTINEL became PROJECTA to PROJECTE, the crates torrust-demo and its siblings example-demo and example-project-a to -e, and the prefix example-, with the production prefix row, the `INDEX_OWNER` fallback and the live assembly identifier reported rather than changed, and the README and crate description naming the tool as the estate's label linter with one lineage acknowledgement. The brief's own mapping sent torrust-demo and torrust-index both to example-project-a, so one stand-in meant the root in some fixtures and a member in others, which cost review 1's fix round; review 2's fix round restored a package-ordering test whose name order and directory order had come to agree after the rename, so that it no longer proved that the name decides (`orders_discovered_packages_by_crate_name`, ``workspace.rs:435-436``: the two orders must disagree). Measured: 759 raw lines in the changed files and 0 private references; gate run 1 on c02 green in 701.7 s, run 2 on c03 red in 628.3 s on the door compute test's broken pipe in an untouched package with the node at 40.49 CPU-seconds per wall-second (`prob:gates:load-sensitive-tests-made-deterministic`), and run 3 on c04 green in 553.7 s. Its reviews were FIX ROUND, FIX ROUND and IMPORTABLE, and it was imported without amendment at 16:14Z as 951eb8c to 6bdf293 over r002g29's main (lint 10.3 s, wall 21.0 s; tools main 6bdf2939).
