### Build and install · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-build`

**Problem (The install primitive's release refusal names its reason, resolves the home only for home paths, refuses a staging root equal to the live one, and its graph and README follow)** · `prob:build:install-primitive-follow-ups`

**OPEN.** 2026-09-28; the review follow-ups of the install targets and maps bite (lane r002c90), none blocking. (1) When the tag or commit moves after the last target, the refusal report carries no class or reason line, only the refused count, the last completed step and exit 4, because the target count is set to the completed count before the map-level reason prints: the reason line prints, and the path gets a test. (2) The invoking home is required for every map, even one with an absolute root or a substituted root, and its absence is refused as a generic invalid root: the home is resolved only for `~/` paths, with a reason of its own. (3) A staging directory that normalizes to the filesystem root makes the stage the live tree with staging's relaxations still applied: a staged root equal to the resolved root is refused. (4) The install scripts run the synced primitive through the environment program because Meson refuses a build output as an install-script program, and configuration runs a directory-creating program for the release-test roots; both are recorded here, and a later graph passes the output directly if a Meson form allows it. (5) The registry environment fallback also applies to a live install, so the flag is not the only override; informational. (6) The tools README's layout section does not list the maps directory and its options entry names only the domain. (7) Staging is tested only through the flag and not the environment variable, the `~/` registry-pattern expansion is untested, and the step-8 rejection is untested: each gets its fixture.

**Problem (Four small findings of the workspace move: harness syntax only, install scripts in every domain, the runbook's old code class, and a stale path in the forge wrapper)** · `prob:build:workspace-move-small-findings`

**OPEN.** 2026-09-29, none blocking. (1) The Meson graph registers the shell harnesses for a syntax check only, so a gate that runs the suites never runs them; the gate form runs them by a glob of its own, and the graph should own that list. (2) The install scripts are registered in every domain, so a server configuration carries install steps it never runs; they belong to the trusted-host domain. (3) The runbook's import-gate section names the code class's earlier path list, which lacks the maps, the linter declarations and the Meson files that the gate form now includes. (4) The forge wrapper names a configuration path from before the loop homes moved. Items (3) and (4) fall to the publication cleanup of item (`prob:estate:tools-repository-publication-cleanup`) if it reaches them first.

**Problem (Every shipped executable takes a family name, tools that release together become commands of one binary, and helpers leave the search path)** · `prob:build:binary-names-and-commands`

**OPEN.** The bites this item listed as open are met: bites 10, 11, 12, 14 and 15 are DONE in the estate's closed record (``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-10-lane-merge``, ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-11-subject-roots``, ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-12-server-kits``, ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-14-gateway-commands`` and ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-15-runtime-merge``), landed with lane r002f28 in tools main 978e1ed9 and deploy #47. What remains is bite 2's half beyond the entry (the entry rebuilt and deployed, the registry fields written for every row, and the drivers' lane that reads them) and the residue in the last paragraph; the earlier status follows.

Earlier status: OPEN — Decision record 033 (generic 8401bedc) decided the family names; the entry halves of bites 1 and 2 (db7364c, c27e10d) landed; the renames proceed through the dependency concept's bites, of which bites 6, 7, 9 and 13 are done (items ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-6-build-and-release-tools``, ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-7-gate-submitter-and-run-report``, ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-9-claude-adapter-and-session-core`` and ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-13-chat-export-rename``) and 2 to 5 and 8 landed with lane H at 978e1ed9. Open: the lane merge (item ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-10-lane-merge``), the two subject roots (item ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-11-subject-roots``), the server kits (item ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-12-server-kits``), the gateway (item ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-14-gateway-commands``) and the runtime (item ``entry:build:toolsdeps-bite-15-runtime-merge``); for bite 2, the entry rebuilt and deployed, the registry fields written for all rows by one owner-run script, and the drivers' lane that reads them; and the residue in the last paragraph.

The search path and the unit directories are flat namespaces shared with every other package, so every executable a person runs carries the family prefix `superloop-`, the bare word is the family's dispatcher in the style of `cargo`, and an executable run only by a hook, a unit or another tool lives in the package's private directory where no name can clash; a name carries no file extension, no vendor's product it only wraps, no estate's or loop's name and no bare generic word (a provider of another trade using the word `superloop` is in another namespace). Tools that share a caller, a trust level and a contract become commands of one binary with one version (the lane drivers with the lane's status and follow tools; the loop entry with its bridges; the account tools; the triage owner tools; the runtime adapters; the steward clients; the correspondence clients), while a hook that runs on every tool call, the privileged worker launcher, the gateway and each single-purpose tool stay alone; every tool stays in the published set. Order: the four preparatory bites at any time, the rename bites after the first live installs from milestone tags, the gateway and runtime bites last, each with a test at its turn (another accepted record's gate has moved the callers and a live install has reported the destinations retired, or the bite runs), and the published root commit after this record's gate; the individual versions of item (`prob:estate:tools-repository-publication-cleanup`) part (D) follow the merges, since a merged binary has one version.

Rules the reviews taught: the brief of a record that carries a table and an order crosses the table against the bites row by row; a structure that other packages construct is part of what a change to it touches, so a brief that extends a public structure names its constructors and the lane's bar runs the workspace's compile and clippy; and a report about a loop is made where the loop's programs are reachable and with programs the loop cannot replace, naming its shell and helpers by fixed system paths and tested against planted copies of them.

Design lane r002d06 (2026-09-29, opus xhigh through the Claude lane driver, read-only: read-back 50 s, work 965 s of 3,600) inventoried the fifty executables the tools repository ships and proposed their names; its report, kept beside the run, states what it could not verify without a network (the packaging policy's section numbers, clashes in a distribution's archive and in the crate registry). It found five binaries built and installed by no map, one server script installed on the workstation without a caller, the deployed name `codex` being the path the vendor's own installer rewrites, and twelve shipped scripts and units holding forty lines of one estate's names. Its six open questions (the bare command while the heartbeat guard holds it, whether the desktop helpers and the forge and host wrappers stay in the published set, the linter's name, aliases during the move (none by default, sequencing instead), one source package per tool, and the server account's name) were decided as it recommended.

Record 033 landed 2026-09-29 in the generic repository as 8401bedc, the sixteen bites open (lane r002d07, opus xhigh through the Claude lane driver: read-back 55 s, work 1,040 s of 3,600, four amendment steps of 500 s, 195 s, 200 s and 200 s; its independent reader, gpt-6-sol xhigh through the Codex lane driver: 1,010 s, 570 s, 355 s, 350 s and 605 s over five rounds, the fifth importable with two replacement lines applied as written; gate green on orel in 3.7 s at the amended tip). The rounds found what a gate cannot: a bite that removed defaults with no step supplying the settings, a helper moved off the search path while a Meson layer found it by bare name, an account rename without its state's migration, a proof no lane may run, a closing gate impossible as worded, a timer a migration would have enabled, and two executables with a decided name and no bite; the paragraph on the lane box took three rounds because it predicted what the box would retire, and now claims nothing about it.

Bite 1's entry half landed 2026-09-29 as tools main db7364c (lane r002d12, gpt-6-sol high through the Codex lane driver: read-back 65 s, work 1,956 s of 5,400, three amendment steps of 2,371 s, 765 s and 555 s; its reader, gpt-6-sol xhigh: 870 s, 685 s, 405 s and 495 s over four rounds, the fourth importable; final gate green on sykora in 603.2 s with 2,742 tests). The row takes an optional `runs_root`, an absolute path without a dotted component, anywhere the owner names; the registry command prints it, `verify` reports it, and the entry's driver-launch form takes its root from it. A declared root is checked at launch for owner, group, kind and mode as the standard roots are; the root and the run directory are resolved once by descriptor without following links, by one routine `verify` and the launch share; a root beneath an ancestor another identity can replace is refused with the rule in the refusal; and a registry without the field keeps every refusal's words, held by one table of 26 conditions asserted on the exact bytes and the status. The field names the directory the lane box's definition of a run gives as the durable runs root. One gate round at the first tip was red on sykora on the permission-triage topology probe, an untouched package, while another gate ran on the node (first reported green before it was read, then corrected); the later tips were green on both nodes. It was the second untouched-package test that day to fail under two concurrent gates (the first, the lane gateway's parked command at the chat export's ninth step), which opened the item for timing-bound tests.

Bite 2's entry half landed 2026-09-29 as tools main c27e10d, with no binary deployed, so the live entry does not yet accept the fields and no row carries them (lane r002d13, gpt-6-sol high: read-back 65 s, work 1,245 s of 5,400, two amendment steps of 2,206 s and 540 s; its reader, gpt-6-sol xhigh: 700 s, 560 s and 365 s over three rounds, the third importable; a first gate red in 52.6 s and a second green in 599.6 s at earlier tips, the final tip green on orel in 648.5 s with 2,748 tests). The row takes three optional fields: `claude_argv` and `codex_argv`, each the argument vector a backend's agent program runs as (a non-empty list of non-empty strings without NUL, first an absolute path without a dotted component or a trailing separator), and `default_driver`, a backend's name that requires that backend's vector in the same row; the registry command prints declared fields and omits absent ones, and `verify` reports for each declared program its path and whether it exists, is a regular file and is executable. The first gate failed to compile another package's tests because the brief called every other package unchanged; the first inspection looked from the owner's side and could not enter a loop-private directory, the second found its helpers through a search path whose first directory the loop can write, the third uses fixed system paths.

Residue: the ordinary probe, which predates bite 2, still finds `id`, `env`, `sort`, `sed` and conditionally `git` and `grep` through the entered search path, carried by item ``entry:homes:verify-becomes-a-read``; and bite 1's refusal table covers the form's own conditions, not the shared registry validation and entry preflight it passes through.

**Problem (The tools install by Meson's native rules and are prepared for distribution packaging; the install maps and the install primitive retire)** · `prob:build:native-meson-install`

**OPEN.** Re-read against the three trees on 2026-11-03 (tools 486558ce, generic e8b83958, estate 5213fe40). Met in this repository and closed in the estate's closed record: bite 1, native destinations (``prob:build:native-install-bite-1-native-destinations``), and bite 3, paths that follow the configured prefix (``prob:build:native-install-bite-3-paths-follow-the-prefix``). Bite 4's half for this repository's own programs is met with the Meson-route deploys from #47, and its remainder, the stale host names, is the estate's; bite 5 is the generic repository's (``prob:build:generic-documents-install-natively``) and bite 6 the estate's (``prob:build:instance-publication-installs-natively``), each with its repository's half of bite 8. What remains here: bite 2, the setup of loop homes moved into commands of the tools, since the estate's charter map still writes each loop's Claude home through the install primitive; bite 7's offline package build, whose home-prefix upgrade half the retirement baseline met (``entry:build:first-native-install-inventory-baseline``); and this repository's half of bite 8, the install primitive retired once neither the generic repository nor the estate installs by map. The earlier status follows.

Earlier status: OPEN — Decided 2026-09-29; the record (generic `bd53858c`), the maintainers' requirements (`7046f220`) and fifteen further statements (`1092db08`) are imported. Open: the migration's eight bites, the first next, and the distribution test suite the tools repository is to build; until the 67 statements describing the maps and the primitive are amended the record controls where they differ, and the host-deployment exception below holds until the native install can stage and install one package.

`meson install` is the front door but a program of this estate does the work: the build registers one install script that runs the install primitive over the maps, which carry what Meson's own rules would (source, destination, mode). A packager expects `meson setup --prefix`, `meson install --destdir` and files under the prefix, and a private installer with its own manifest is a second packaging system, so the tools install by Meson's native rules and the maps and the primitive retire. What the primitive guaranteed (an equal target left untouched, the outgoing bytes kept before a replacement, every target read again, a retired name removed) is the package manager's work once the tools ship as a package; what a package cannot carry (the hooks a harness registers under a person's home, the worker charter in each lane home, a loop's rows and settings) is set up by a command of the installed tools. Packaging recipes live downstream: neither repository carries a distribution's packaging directory or spec file, since a recipe no maintainer tests is a guess, and upstream publishes what any maintainer needs (a staged tree that is right, the inventory of files by role and licence, release archives with checksums). A template a program reads in order to work is the tools repository's data, installed once under its data directory; the generic repository carries no copy, declares its dependency on the tools repository and installs documents only (its generated label export stays a check artifact). Rust sources come from the distribution's crates from the first package on, a vendored source being an exception declared crate by crate by the distributor; outside a distribution the publication is source at a tagged release with Cargo-managed dependencies, and no archive of vendored sources is published; the distance between the lock file's 142 crates and what a distribution carries is measured by a check the repository is to provide and closed by moving to a version the distributions carry, dropping the dependency, or naming the crate as one to be packaged.

Settled with the design: the package is split by role and licence from the start; programs find their helpers and data by paths set at build time, with an override; a project option names the documentation directory beneath the data directory; the install stays passive and an explicit command activates user units under a home prefix; a rulebook change is detected by the hook's stamp comparison and the command that syncs a rulebook guarantees a new stamp; under a home prefix a file list is kept per release and old files are removed by a narrow command that refuses on conflict, since Meson's log knows only the latest install; and the rename bites of record 033 run after the native install exists, so no map is changed once for a name and again for its removal. Each maintainer requirement is a predicate with what makes it fail and names the side that checks it: a check that fetches from the network cannot sit in the default test suite, since a distribution's package build runs that suite with the network denied, so such checks are an opt-in release route; what only a package can show (its declared build and run-time dependencies, how an upgrade or a removal treats an administrator's edits, a bundled crate's declaration, a service's activation under the distribution's policy) belongs to a downstream package's tests, which the document specifies and upstream does not pretend to run.

Host deployments go through the install route with a staging pass, with one bounded exception: while other loops' lanes run, one program that no running lane depends on is deployed alone, built package-scoped from the imported commit, probed from its staged copy and renamed over the live one with the previous file kept. Whatever removes a file on the word of a manifest keeps the kinds the reviews of lane r002d14 found: a move and not an unlink, identity from a reviewed digest, a recovery place nobody else can write, no second link to the recovered bytes.

Decided at the desk 2026-09-29, leaving for the design the prefix on a host whose system image is read-only (a per-user prefix, a layered package, or a system extension), what the generic repository's and the instance's publications become, how record 033 retires an old name (which its fourth bite had given to a map's retirement form), and the migration order. Lane r002d14, the map's retirement form (gpt-6-sol high: read-back 20 s, work 2,371 s, amendment 3,111 s, a second amendment stopped at 635 s; its reader 615 s and 940 s over two rounds, neither importable), was stopped unlanded for this reason. Two host deployments that day (the chat export at 26cdd91 and the entry at db7364c) were made by a package-scoped build and a hold-and-rename script rather than through `meson install`, under the exception.

Design lane r002d18 (gpt-6-sol xhigh, reads only: read-back 30 s, work 1,485 s of 5,400) counted 95 targets installed by default (46 from the tools, 49 from the generic repository) and four more behind an option, and laid out eight bites: native destinations declared beside the maps; the setup of homes moved into commands of the tools; paths following the configured prefix; live callers switched family by family; the generic repository a documents-only install; the instance's publication converted; an offline package build and a home-prefix upgrade proved; the maps and the primitive removed last. What stops a packager, by size: the Cargo build can reach the network; maps install into homes outside any prefix; units name fixed paths; licences differ between packages; names lack the family prefix; server kits are copied by hand; seventeen binaries owe a version line; there are no manual pages. Three of its choices were then replaced by the downstream recipes, the run-time data in the tools repository, and distribution crates in place of its reviewed vendored archive.

The record `adr/034` landed as `bd53858c` (lane r002d19, gpt-6-sol through the Codex lane driver: read-back 165 s, work 1,716 s of 5,400 with a first gate green in 3.6 s, three amendment steps of 1,851 s, 1,030 s and 580 s; its independent reader, gpt-6-sol xhigh: 1,030 s, 1,310 s and 770 s over three rounds, the third importable with one line replaced, stating the vendored-source exception; gates green in 3.8 s at the reviewed tip and in 4.0 s at the amended one): nine files, one commit, 50 statements minted and 126 more citations resolving than at the base (1,283 to 1,333 mints, 1,027 to 1,153 resolved citations, no warning and no failure); it assigns a fate to 67 labelled statements describing the maps and the install primitive (56 amended, 5 retired, 5 staying with a scoped meaning, 1 historical).

The maintainers' requirements landed as `7046f220` (lane r002d20, gpt-6-sol xhigh: read-back 20 s, work 1,926 s, three amendment steps of 970 s, 940 s and 1,395 s; its reader, gpt-6-sol xhigh: 1,345 s, 1,470 s and 775 s over three rounds, the first not importable because most checks could not fail, the third importable with ten lines replaced; gates green in 3.7 s at the reviewed tip and in 3.7 s at the amended one): one document with its registration, twenty-seven requirements, 37 statements minted (1,333 to 1,370 mints, 1,207 citations resolving, no warning and no failure).

Fifteen further statements landed as `1092db08`, minted by lane r002d19 like the first thirty-eight (a step of 940 s; its reader 430 s, importable with no finding; gate green in 3.8 s): the rules of construction the day's reviews taught (a built program is probed where it will run, from its staged copy, before it replaces the live one; a fixed path says which program starts and the environment what is loaded into it; a system program provided through links is trusted by the ownership of its whole chain; the shape of a value says nothing about where it came from; a reader who opens pages from disk is an ordinary reader; a removal that depends on nobody using a thing closes the door before it counts who is inside; a gate keeps the output of its failing tests; a change to what a browser does names its browser test), the continuation of a step that ended from outside, the operations profile ``entry:estate:operations-profile``, and the host-deployment exception with its reason.

**Problem (The Claude hook binary's digest alternates between two values across deploys with no change to its sources)** · `prob:build:claude-hook-digest-not-reproducible`

`libexec/hooks/superloop-claude-hook` was 902b9d76326b8ac5 at deploys #66 and #67, 9ed4f8c2bccdecca at #68 (tools main 4f29ba74) and 902b9d76326b8ac5 again at #69 (tools main ebd880b2), while neither lane r002g05 nor lane r002g06 touched the hook's package, and the builds ran in the same host-dev toolbox with the same packaged toolchain and target directory. The build is not reproducible for this binary (candidates: embedded build paths, timestamps, or the order of a dependency's codegen units), so a digest change in a deploy's record cannot be read as a behaviour change for it, and the #68 and #69 letters had to state that the move asks no per-loop step. The two binaries are compared (strings, section sizes, `.comment`, panic paths) from the #68 and #69 staging directories, and the build is made deterministic or the non-deterministic input is named.

**Problem (The host build's release linter check runs under Meson's fixed 30 s default, which a loaded host crosses)** · `prob:build:release-linter-check-timeout-from-measured-wall`

**OPEN.** Measured at deploy #70 on 2026-10-29; no fix has landed.

Run 1 of the deploy #70 host build, in the host-dev toolbox from tools main 7bdf7d3d, went red on `release - superloop-tools:release-linter-check` at Meson's default 30 s timeout (30.11 s, killed by SIGTERM), while the same test took 20.9 s at deploy #67 and its sibling `release-pack-consumer` took 47.6 s against 31.1 s then, with the host's one-minute load at 12 from other loops' Codex lanes; the relaunch was the retry, green in 691 s. The check lints the whole tree, which grows with every import, so its margin under a fixed limit shrinks on its own. The test declares a timeout derived from its measured wall with headroom, or `timeout: 0` with the check's own wall reported, rather than Meson's default, as the gate's lint suites already do ``entry:gates:meson-lint-suite-timeouts``; the release harness's other load-shaped reds are (`prob:gates:release-harness-cases-red-under-load`), and tripod's gate-form finding of a fixed per-item limit without load scaling, which its own backlog carries, has the same shape.

**Problem (The lockless census reads the lock that cargo metadata has just written, and an unlocked build leaves a lock that makes a release configure refuse)** · `prob:build:lockless-census-reads-the-generated-lock`

**OPEN.** From review 1 of lane r002g36 on 2026-11-01; no change has landed.

In a tree without ``Cargo.lock``, `cargo metadata` runs unlocked and writes a ``Cargo.lock`` as a side effect, which `lock_census` (``census.rs:620`` at tools main 4f2dc76a) then reads unconditionally while reporting `absent; resolved through cargo metadata`, a description true only of the moment before; and ``workspace.py``'s unlocked build writes the lock too, so a built public clone is left with an untracked ``Cargo.lock``, the next Meson configure takes the lock-present branch, and a configure with `-Drelease_tag` refuses as a dirty checkout (``meson.build:9-10``) ``entry:pack:lockless-public-cut-version-and-public-tag``. A lane has the lockless census use the `resolve` that `cargo metadata` returns, or report that the lock was generated, and has the release tag's cleanliness check disregard an untracked ``Cargo.lock``; a ``.gitignore`` entry is rejected because a vendoring consumer keeps its own lock under version control.

Make it refuse (rabe, without a manifest, already refuses and is tested).

**Problem (The installer gains tree targets selected from the tracked inventory, and an index writer)** · `prob:build:installer-tree-targets-and-index-writer`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a slice of the documentation-install set, whose lead is the estate's ``dec:build:documentation-installs-like-a-package``) ``superloop-install`` applies `[[targets]]` of kind `copy`, one file each. It gains kind `tree`, with a `source` directory, a `destination` directory, `include` globs (by default every Markdown file) and `exclude` globs, selected from `git ls-files` of the clone at the installed commit so that untracked files never install; and an `index` step that writes ``INDEX.md`` at a documentation root from each installed document's first heading and first minted label, with the provenance file naming the source commit and the install time.

Tests: a fixture repository with nested documents, an excluded archive and an untracked file; the index content; idempotence, a second run changing nothing; and the removal of files no longer tracked, recorded in the provenance. The linter's declared-surface rows learn the `tree` kind, and the installer refuses an install whose documentation graph has an orphan, its half of (`prob:linter:documentation-graph-check`).

**Problem (The tools repository installs its documentation under the package documentation root)** · `prob:build:tools-documentation-installs-under-the-package-doc-root`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a slice of the documentation-install set) Every tracked prose file of this repository installs to ``datadir/doc/superloop-tools/`` under its relative path: ``README.md``, ``CHANGELOG.md``, ``docs/**/*.md``, ``packages/*/README.md``, ``packages/*/docs/**/*.md``, ``infra/*/README.md``, and ``infra/steward/charter.md`` beside its sysconfdir copy; ``INDEX.md`` and the provenance are generated at install. The public-set manifest keeps governing the public repository and not the host install, because the public repositories are the repositories. ``share/superloop-tools/docs/`` is retired in the same deploy with no compatibility copy, and executable and build-record installation are unchanged.

Measured on 2026-11-05: the Meson layer installs only the public specification set under ``share/superloop-tools/docs/``, so every package README and package document is absent from the host, and a host script of the loop publishes every tracked Markdown document of the three repositories under the documentation root as a hotfix until this set lands. Each document carries the upward reference the documentation graph needs (`prob:linter:documentation-graph-check`).

**Problem (The deployed commands carry section 1 manual pages)** · `prob:build:man-pages-for-deployed-commands`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, a follow-on of the documentation-install set) Each deployed command (``superloop``, ``superloop-lane``, ``superloop-install``, ``superloop-lint``, the lane drivers) gets a section 1 page generated at build from its command contract and its `--help`, installed to ``datadir/man/man1``, so that `man superloop-lane` answers on a host as a packaged tool's page does; the earlier names ``rulebook-install`` and ``orchestration-linter`` get no page and retire with the binary names (`prob:build:binary-names-and-commands`).

### The linter · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-linter`

**Problem (The overlay files' mints are outside the label check)** · `prob:linter:overlay-mints-outside-the-check`

**OPEN.** Partly closed by bite 6 (2026-09-28): the overlay rulebooks' mints join the instance check. Open: A render-time kind count over the rendered pair (refuse a kind repeated outside the containers) in the install primitive; none found on tools main 3c0f1a13, and the live environment render repeats req and rule.

Earlier status: ACTIVE. **PARTLY CLOSED BY BITE 6 2026-09-28.** Since the instance repository declares its surface, its one partition covers the whole tree, so the overlay rulebooks' mints join the check under the instance owner and a duplicate or malformed mint fails the instance gate. What stays render-time is the kind uniqueness across the rendered pair (the environment rulebook and the orchestrator rulebook together), which the rendering step's own check proves; a render-time kind count in the install primitive is the remaining piece, sized with the install-targets bite of ADR 032.

Opened 2026-09-27. The mints in `local/rulebooks/environment.md` and `local/rulebooks/orchestration.md` (the build-toolbox recipe, the temporary root, the document-locations register, the superloop's name and door) are verified only by the rendering lane's own script, because the overlays' owner partition takes no part in `check`; the unique-kind convention of ADR 030 is therefore proven at render time, not at the gate. The fix is a linter bite: the overlays join the check under their owner with the conventions the rendered files carry, or a render-time check in `rulebook-install` counts kinds over the rendered text and refuses a duplicate outside the containers.

**Problem (The census reports its wall, reads an empty list and names its list ambiguities; the stamp pair's refusals are each tested)** · `prob:linter:census-and-stamp-pair-follow-ups`

**OPEN.** Item (7) landed with bite 14 (r002c94); the census now names `declared_pattern_overlap` and the required-list refusals, but items (1) to (6) are not evidenced as landed. Open: Census timed report and completion wall, skipping empty list lines, named refusals for declared-and-excluded and duplicate-excluded paths, fixtures for the missing-path and traversal refusals and the stamp-pair forms, the kit list built from the shell subset, and the finishing-step doc comment.

Earlier status: ACTIVE. **ITEM (7) LANDED 2026-09-28 (lane r002c94, bite 14).** The Meson record's stamps bullet names the install-input slice among the check stamp's inputs; items (1) to (6) stay open.

Opened 2026-09-28; the review follow-ups of the stamps bite (lane r002c87), none blocking. (1) The census result carries no top-level elapsed field and no completion log line with a wall, while the linter README promises both for every judging command and now lists the census as one: the census wraps its report in the timed report form the other judging commands use and logs its completion with the wall. (2) The census reads its list files with a line iterator, so an empty list rendered from the configuration template is one empty line and the path type rejects it as an empty component, which would exit 1 for a repository with no exclusions: empty lines are skipped. (3) A path both declared and excluded, an excluded path listed twice and an excluded path the categorical pattern also matches pass silently; each is an ambiguity in the lists and gets a named refusal. (4) No fixture exercises the census refusals for a tracked path missing from the checkout and for a traversal failure, and no test covers `fmt` without `--check` given the stamp pair, `assemble --write` given the pair, or a lone `--stamp`; each gets its fixture. (5) The infra build definition repeats seven kit scripts in a second list instead of building the kit list from the shell subset, so a new kit script must be added in two places and the census does not audit the subset: the shell subset is declared first and the kit list built from it. (6) The shared command library's doc comment on the finishing step still says it runs only on success, while the runner now calls it for a dirty direct-mode verdict: the comment follows the code. (7) The stamps decision of ADR 031 names three input lists for the check stamp, while the check also reads the shell tools; the review proved a stale stamp over an edited installer, and the candidate's input list now carries the install inputs, so the record's sentence follows in the generic repository's next documentation lane.

**Problem (Activating the values-absent policy makes every plain check refuse without the private document)** · `prob:linter:values-absent-policy-plain-check`

**OPEN.** A check of the tree found `public-pack.values-absent` still absent from ``.linter/policies.toml``; what remains is first a linter step that lets a plain check run without the private-value document, then the approved register kept untracked under an ignored path with a corpus-shape `ignore` row and its public occurrences removed before activation.

``.linter/policies.toml`` of the tools repository and ``packages/public-pack/README.md:61`` (f04 round two, item 3): activating `public-pack.values-absent` so a pack's linter run reads the supplied register needs, first, a linter step that lets a plain check run without the private-value document (an activation scoped to public-pack mode, or a plain-mode absence under the policy's excluded tree reported as an unmeasured census rather than a refusal), because activation in the shared policy file makes every plain check refuse without the document and would put the private inventory into worker lane clones, node gate checkouts, review clones and the host build checkout; then the approved register lives untracked under an ignored path with a corpus-shape `ignore` row (`build` refuses any untracked path, ``lib.rs:317-318``; the tools universe is `tracked-exclusive`, ``.linter/shape.toml:5``), its public occurrences removed before activation; the fixture's committed register (``src/tests.rs:88-92``) is not a model for it.

**Problem (the claims-conform profile is not active for the lane package, so door claims establish no coverage)** · `prob:linter:lane-package-claims-conform-activation`

**OPEN.** The claims-conform profile is still not active for the `SUPERLOOPLANE` owner, so the door tests' citations of the record's labels resolve but establish no coverage; what remains is sizing the activation (the unclaimed labels and the tests that would claim them) and activating it in its own lane with the claims that make it clean.

the claims-conform profile is not activated for the `SUPERLOOPLANE` owner, so citations of the door record's labels from the door's tests (added by lane r002f36 for ``rule:capability:agent-failure-refers``, ``rule:capability:expiry-no-effect``, ``rule:capability:agent-home-isolation``) resolve under the linter but establish no coverage; activating it surfaces every unclaimed label of the package at once (974 of 981 test labels unclaimed, 7 claims, counted by lane r002f36 on 2026-10-23). Fix: size the activation (the count of unclaimed labels and the tests that would claim them) and activate it in its own lane with the claims that make it clean. Where: .linter profiles, packages/superloop-lane tests; with items ``entry:door:door-unknown-shape-reaches-judgement`` and ``entry:docs:capability-record-mints-tier-statements``.

**Problem (the linter carrier lane leaves blockquoted table counts, stale glosses and unfixtured profile claims)** · `prob:linter:linter-carrier-lane-review-follow-ups`

**OPEN.** The round-2 review of the linter lane (r002f50) left three follow-ups, none of them landed: counting table cells over the parser's TableHead and TableRow ranges with a blockquoted-table fixture, test glosses in ``tests/corpus.rs`` that state the current behaviour, and fixtures for the three code-backed claims of `packages/linter/adr/profiles.md:19`.

follow-ups of the linter lane (r002f50, round 2 of 2026-10-23, fit for the next step): (a) `packages/linter/src/fmt.rs:417`, `:434`: `validate_table_rows` splits the table's byte range into lines, so body rows inside a blockquote keep their `> ` prefix and `table_cell_count` counts one cell too many (`> \|1\|2\|` gives 3 cells for 2 columns), so every blockquoted table with a leading-pipe body row refuses as `table-code-pipe-row` where the base formatted it, and a file whose lines end in a lone CR refuses every multi-row table the same way; named refusals, not loss, and this tree has none (0 of 118 Markdown files), but another loop's tree turns red on deploy if it has one; count cells over the parser's own TableHead and TableRow ranges, which start after container markers, with a fixture for a blockquoted table; (b) ``tests/corpus.rs:297``, `:340`, `:419`: the test glosses still state round-1 behaviour and name neither the cross-language shared registry, the missing interpreter, repair refusal, nor the line-ending and locale cases the tests assert and the profiles record cites; (c) `packages/linter/adr/profiles.md:19`: "tracked, uniquely owned", "without importing or executing corpus code" and "non-nested `test_` functions derive integration labels" rest on code rather than a fixture: a corpus `test_*.py` whose module body would create a sentinel file if executed, a module-level `def test_…` that mints, and a misplaced Python claim expecting `claim.defective`. Where: packages/linter; with items ``entry:linter:fmt-table-code-span-pipe-loses-cell`` and ``entry:linter:linter-scans-python-test-docstrings``.

**Goal (The linter has a mint form for a table row)** · `goal:linter:table-row-mint-form`

**OPEN.** Proposed by naturrhythmuszentrum on 2026-10-25 at orchestration-linter seed 3cca748.

A single-backtick label in a table's first column, or an inline labelled head inside a list item, fails "minted away from any environment head", so a document labelled statement by statement must double its text with a head and a citation instead of keeping its table; a conformance matrix's first-column labels are displays (row keys), not occurrences. The proposal: a row-mint form in which a single-backtick label in the first column mints the row as its environment, with the remaining cells as the statement, keeping the display form for matrices that cite.

**Problem (The formatter folds an unrecognised list marker into the previous numbered item)** · `prob:linter:fmt-folds-unrecognised-list-marker`

**OPEN.** Recorded inside ``prob:homes:signing-keys-per-loop`` and carried by no other item; not re-measured since.

An unrecognised list marker such as `6a.` is folded by `superloop-lint fmt` into the previous numbered item, joining two list items into one. The formatter either keeps such a line as its own paragraph or refuses the file by name, with a fixture.

**Problem (linter frictions from the aelix desk: check-mode abort, opaque guard message, no wall in `check`, git-bound traversal with a duplicate JSON key, and a blockquote rewrite)** · `prob:linter:linter-aelix-frictions`

**OPEN.** All five defects are imported: items 1 to 4 as `79136be` (lane linterfix1), the follow-up residuals as `db7cd13` and `3b67c62` (lane linterres), and item 5 as `2b8217c` and `7e5284a` (lane blockquote5), all on 2026-09-15 and deployed to the host and the loop home as build `7e5284a`; the blockquote review's serializer residuals and the README and formatter-test findings closed under ``entry:linter:repository-not-formatter-clean``. Still open, carried by no open item: the `serialize_markdown` route into `source_parse_failure` has no test, the verification bar's unlocked `cargo run` lacks `--locked`, the new branch near ``lib.rs:633`` and the round-trip helper's terminator fixup near ``lib.rs:1311`` carry no comment, and the repository's own formatter never reaches the alert branch that only the unit test covers.

The aelix loop reported five defects against the deployed build e377e14, reproducible from its repository at `a118634`. A check must report on every file, so `fmt --check` records an integrity-guard rejection as a finding for that file and continues rather than aborting at the first one (one ERROR line, 0.018 s, over 74 files). A guard message must be actionable, so it names what differs (kind, attribute, delimiter width, byte range) rather than "input has inline code, output has inline code". Under the wall-time rule `check` carries a duration, as `fmt` already reported `elapsed_micros`. A finding object carries each key once, since a duplicate `message` key in one JSON object is rejected by a strict parser; the git requirement behind the two `traversal_failure` findings outside a worktree is the snapshot's `universe = "git-tracked"` answer, and a pack whose snapshot declares `universe = "as-written"` is checked from the extracted tree without git (as ``entry:linter:linter-public-pack``'s archive is), so the desk's pack needs that declaration rather than a new traversal. A one-paragraph blockquote `> text` round-trips unchanged; it had been rewritten to an empty quote line and an indented one, keeping two authored letters formatter-dirty since their edition r003. The settled question: the formatter normalises `_x_` to `*x*` and pads tables to `|a|b|` with the dash run sized to the header, which is the formatter of record's serialization (the source copies of the four discipline texts in `adr/` are formatter-clean under it), so a pack carrying copies of those texts reflows them rather than recording an exception.

Reported 2026-09-14, with one earlier caveat confirmed closed: `fmt` preserves double-backtick display spans through a reflow, closing ``entry:linter:fmt-collapses-display-spans`` on that side. Each defect was planned as one bounded bite on `packages/linter` with a failing-first test, the first four sharing one lane.

Residue: the serializer residuals of the blockquote review (a quote whose first block is a fenced or indented code block emitting an empty marker line before the fence, and a loose list inside a quote separating its items with a marker carrying a trailing space) were closed by `1580293` and `1321522`, and the ``README.md`` omission of `fmt --check` and `fix` from the exit-3 set (lines 19 to 25) and the formatter test's non-root assumption (`chmod 000` denying reads) by lane fmtdocs, all under ``entry:linter:repository-not-formatter-clean``, which also carries the traversal failure that still aborts a check before the per-file loop and the repository-wide `fmt --check` that exited 3 at the base. Still open here: the `serialize_markdown` route into `source_parse_failure` has no test; the verification bar's unlocked `cargo run` reorders ``Cargo.lock`` on the server and should carry `--locked`; the new branch near ``lib.rs:633`` and the round-trip helper's terminator fixup near ``lib.rs:1311`` carry no comment where the replaced code had one; the repository's own formatter (`ENABLE_GFM` off in `parser_options`) never reaches the alert branch, which only the unit test covers.

The paragraphs of this item's evidence that name an estate's private details are kept in that estate's backlog as ``rec:linter:linter-aelix-frictions``.

**Problem (the second downstream adoption report on the 416b136 edition: the debt paid to 116, a patch for the vacuous activation, eight frictions and three questions)** · `prob:linter:linter-adoption-r002-frictions`

**OPEN.** All ten bites landed 2026-09-23 to 2026-09-25 (r002a1 b507f54b through r002c34 f96215c2), edition orchestration-linter-0.1.0-f6b263a.zip shipped and the r002 receipt sent 2026-09-25. Still open, carried by no open item: the review residue of the last paragraph, namely the whole-file external census surface that prints no `surface_paths`, untested `nothing_governed` beside `vacuous_surface` and the untested repetition-count overflow arm, the stale `fixture topology` message, the burn loader's declared set built without the registered filter, two stale private doc comments and the uncommented fixture re-layout, per-file findings for a non-repository root and a nested vendored store, the unmeasured walk into a populated host `target`, the public-pack proposal still calling the declared universe git-tracked, and r002c20's two `lane-verify` load flakes.

The coordination desk's second adoption report, ``rep:linteradoption:r002-adopted-with-the-debt-visible``, answers the receipt of r001 ``entry:linter:linter-adoption-r001-frictions`` point by point and raises frictions F13 to F26 and questions Q7 to Q9 against the `orchestration-linter-0.1.0-416b136` edition; they resolve into ten bites, each answered from the contract the code already keeps. (1) F14, the vacuous activation silent on the surface half, arrives as a contributed patch (``rep:linteradoption:proposed-patch-surface-paths``: a `surface_paths` member on `ActivationCounts` in ``report.rs``, tallied per policy in `activation_counts` in ``application.rs`` over `snapshot.declared_surface` and `corpus.readable()`), applied by a lane as data and reviewed like any candidate, its three decisions kept (the reach not intersected with the owner's share, because `legacy.todos` is declared by one owner and activated by another; not filtered by extension, because the block reports the declaration; `ungoverned_activation` untouched), and the warning on a zero reach is a second bite. (2, 3) F13 and F15 are one gap between the storage and the matching model: a path is the byte string git records, any byte but NUL (`BytePath` in ``pattern.rs``), while `Grammar::matches` collected `input.chars()`, so a non-text path refused topology as `NonTextPath` and `PATHCHAR = VCHAR / %x80-10FFFF` (``abnf.rs:132``) excluded the space, leaving thirty-four space-named documents unaccounted on the platform repository; RFC 5234 is defined over octets (`OCTET = %x00-FF`), so rows match over bytes with `PATHCHAR = %x01-FF`, whether a name is text becomes a policy lint rather than a topology refusal, and a fixture writing a name the filesystem refuses fails open naming the platform, because that refusal observes the filesystem, not the linter. (4) F17, a backticked label in prose minting everywhere except alone in a parenthesis and duplicating on its second span, gets a worked line in the guide. (5) F21, the lowering writer (`bin/linter.rs:751`) that censused 1,872 files over empty registers, said it wrote, wrote nothing and left 313 growth failures, is answered from the writer's contract. (6) F24, one declaration giving two numbers on two machines (2,031 findings and six unaccounted paths against 2,025 and none), arises because `git-tracked` leaves the checkout unchecked and `as-written` leaves version control unchecked; a third answer, `tracked-exclusive`, holds the checkout to the tracked set after the declared ignore relation, forcing git's ignore and the universe's to agree. (7) F16, F19, F25 and F26 become guide sentences where each rule lives. (8) Q9, a ratchet row settled and kept rather than owed (forty-eight of their 116 are strings a machine reads or prints), becomes a `kept` marking counted by the census and excluded from the debt, so a zero obtained by declaration stays visible. (9) The edition's provenance names every commit it carries since the previous one, so an adopter verifies bites by hash. (10) The next edition and the receipt of r002 answer Q7 (a received letter is exhibited, not cited, and its head promoted in the corpus that will cite it), Q8 (prose before code and the biggest concentration first, because prose rows convert to citations that resolve while code rows are mostly strings that stay) and Q9 by bite 8, with C5, C6 and C8 read back. Bites 1 and 9 also close r001's residuals, the owner-scoped `paths` and the provenance parenthetical.

The report arrived 2026-09-23 as an archive of three files (SHA-256 prefix `be503317f79da092`). The desk built the edition as shipped (`cargo build --release --locked -p linter`, 2 min 28.79 s cold against the 78.8 s recorded) and verified every r001 bite by effect and by `diff -rq`, since the archive carries no version control. Their measurements: the coordination repository at `e4d5eef` checks clean in 1.86 s (776 sources, 5,010 mints, 2,209 citations resolved, 0 unaccounted paths, failures or warnings), the old-style debt down from 1,308 occurrences over 314 rows to 116 over 47 by conversion rather than deletion (citations 1,598 to 2,209 between `8438aaa` and `e4d5eef`), and the hook-injected rulebook recatalogued under fourteen heads at 9,497 bytes of a 9,500-byte cap; the operations and platform repositories wait on the 116. Recorded corrections: four activations, not two, were deactivated at `00415e3`; the two deleted documents were the archive's scenario and division sets; F1 recomputed, with `.linter/` present and every core document absent both editions name all five in one refusal, while with it absent the older edition refused with ``shape.toml`` alone; and this desk's 902 is the workspace test count, the library crate's 750.

Common to the record below unless stated: each lane ran gpt-6-sol xhigh through the Codex lane driver with its read-back and work walls given as read-back + work; each review was a read-only Opus lane, Opus 5 for r002c1's first round and Opus 5.5 from then on; each code-moving import rebuilt the deployed linter from the imported commit in both copies; and this repository's `.clean`, `.findings` and 203 activation rows stayed byte-identical before and after.

Bite 1 was imported 2026-09-23 as b507f54b (lane r002a1, 20 s + 2,190 s of 5,400; gate on sykora 599.5 s, 2,368 passed; review 65 s + 1,035 s of 1,500). Round 1 was not importable: the tally looked a surface up by policy identifier while surfaces key by census domain, so the two retired policies printed no count, a defect the patch carried unseen; a fix round of 980 s resolved it through `census_domain_of_policy` in ``burn.rs`` with two more fixtures, and round 2 amended four documentation lines. `surface_paths` counts the readable corpus paths a policy's own declared surface reaches, absent where none is declared; here it stands on six ORCHESTRATION rows, ``legacy.division-names``, ``legacy.record-references-repository``, ``legacy.scenario-numbers`` and ``legacy.section-references-repository`` at 639, ``legacy.tag-references`` at 53 and ``legacy.todos`` at 586, each beside a share of 138. Pinned by ``claim:report:every-activation-carries-what-its-surface-reaches``, ``claim:report:a-surface-naming-an-absent-tree-reaches-nothing``, ``claim:report:a-surface-reaches-beyond-its-activating-share`` and ``claim:report:a-policy-reports-the-surface-of-its-census-domain``.

Bite 1b was imported 2026-09-23 as 76a70848 (lane r002b1, 20 s + 1,325 s of 5,400; gate on sykora 613.1 s, 2,369 passed; review 65 s + 585 s of 1,500, importable with four non-blocking notes). A zero reach raises the warning `vacuous_surface` (`VacuousSurface { policy, domain }`), never a failure, because a declaration copied from another repository mid-adoption should be pointed at rather than refused: once per policy over the rows whose `surface_paths` is `Some(0)`, in deterministic order, naming the policy and the census domain that declares the surface and no owner, never on an absent count, with `nothing_governed`, `ungoverned_activation` and `clean` unchanged. This repository's rows reach 53 to 639 paths and print none; the desk's ``legacy.todos`` gains exactly this line. Pinned by ``claim:report:a-vacuous-surface-is-named-once-per-policy``; the `census_domain_of_policy` guard in the warning path is unreachable and commented so.

Bites 2 and 3, first half, were imported 2026-09-23 as 012ad31b (lane r002c1, 20 s + 1,711 s of 5,400, fix round 845 s of 3,600; gates on sykora 657.7 s at 3113fe43 and 600.7 s at b78b94d6, both 2,370 passed, with one rerun between after an untouched-package timing case failed under load; review round 1 60 s + 1,040 s of 1,500, not importable with two amendments, round 2 after the CLI update 50 s + 705 s of 1,200, importable). `Grammar::matches` takes `&[u8]`: a quoted string contributes its bytes, a numeric value above `%xFF` is refused as `names no byte`, and `PATHCHAR = %x01-FF`; on the desk's repository the thirty-four space-named documents become accountable by one `*PATHCHAR` row. Round 1's blocking finding kept the openings contract, that an opening is the whole of what its arm admits, by reading a numeric value as text only when its bytes are well-formed UTF-8 and otherwise as an opening that commits to nothing, so such a rule roots at the corpus root. Pinned by ``claim:abnf:numeric-values-name-bytes-ranges-and-sequences``, ``claim:spdx:a-row-decides-a-path-that-is-not-text-by-its-bytes``, ``claim:partition:the-path-tail-core-rule-accounts-for-every-byte-but-nul`` and ``claim:declaration:a-pattern-decides-a-path-over-its-bytes``; package tests 809 to 810.

The second half was imported 2026-09-23 as 1188d50d from candidate aa0b1258 with six prose amendments (lane r002c2, 15 s + 1,691 s of 5,400; gates on sykora 750.5 s at the candidate and 624.8 s amended, both 2,370 passed; review 50 s + 650 s of 1,500). The `NonTextPath` topology refusal is gone: `CorpusPlan::compile` returns the corpus, `TopologyDefect` and the topology variant of `PlanError` are removed, partition and selection retain paths by their bytes, and each participating path carries its native form where the platform has one (total on unix, UTF-8 names elsewhere), a path without one partitioned and selected but read by no content reader. ``claim:plan:a-path-that-is-not-text-enters-the-corpus``, ``claim:partition:a-path-that-is-not-text-is-accounted-by-its-bytes`` and ``claim:catalogue:future-plan-decides-a-non-text-path-by-its-bytes`` replace the refusal tests and ``claim:partition:a-path-that-is-not-text-refuses-topology``, the count staying at 810, and the byte-named fixtures go through a test-support helper that fails open naming the platform and error. The review's material finding became the lossy-readers bite: a finding in a non-text-named file printed its path lossily in `Location`, the traversal and parse failures and the burn tally, and the tally keyed lossily while declared rows use the reversible display, so a registered name with a space never met its tally and growth fired every run.

The text-names lint was imported 2026-09-24 as 483144e1 from candidate b11cc163 with three amendments (lane r002c3, 15 s + 2,036 s of 7,200; gates 672.6 s on orel at the candidate and 636.9 s on sykora amended, 2,379 passed both; review 40 s + 690 s of 1,500). ``paths.names-well-formed`` is a path-set policy, activated nowhere here, reading an optional per-owner encoding from `.linter/policy-paths.toml`, `utf-8` by default, and raising one failure per participating path of an activating owner whose bytes are not well-formed in it (`path name: opaque%FF: governed under quarry and not well-formed utf-8`) unless the pair's `paths` list tolerates it. The amendments named the fourth path-set policy in the per-pair tally's match and in the coherence-list exemption of the append door and the audit; nine tests, package 810 to 819, the `LINTER` share up one path for the new source.

Its follow-ups were imported 2026-09-24 as 2cd0a778 from candidate 211cf91c with six amendments (lane r002c4, 30 s + 1,766 s of 5,400; gates on orel 649.8 s and 646.2 s, 2,381 passed both; review 40 s + 510 s of 1,500). The `utf-16le` and `utf-16be` forms were withdrawn rather than repaired, because they decoded the whole path with its separators and no step on the way into the corpus converts a name into another encoding; `encoding` keeps `utf-8` as its one value and refuses any other where written. **Problem (The linter's documentation of the root-owner declaration names the downward-closed import rule it brings into force and the documents-owner shape)** · `prob:linter:root-owner-declaration-names-the-documents-owner-shape`

**OPEN.** Measured by naturrhythmuszentrum on 2026-11-01 after its root-owner declaration and answered at 09:07Z; no change to the linter's documentation has landed.

Declaring `root_owner` for the deploy #91 linter made naturrhythmuszentrum's `docs/` root prose, and 908 `policy_import` refusals became visible that were silent before, because no prose had been attributed to the root corpus ``entry:waves:deploy-91-version-0-1-0-relicensed-linter-and-required-root-owner-2026-11-01``. The refusal is the intended model of the linter's layer graph: upward is open, every corpus reaching the root corpus's repo-wide policy without a row, because policy that binds every member must be citable by every member; downward is closed, the root corpus's prose importing no package prefix even along a may-cite row, because a common policy resting on a package's private premise is not common. A design corpus that cites hundreds of package statements is then not root policy but a member corpus of its own: a documents owner over `docs/` and the guides, distinct from the carrier, with a may-cite row from it to each package it cites, while the root owner carries only the paths outside every partition. naturrhythmuszentrum measured that shape clean, the 908 imports becoming admitted sideways imports, and dispatched it. The root-owner declaration's README paragraph names this consequence and the documents-owner shape, so that the next repository to declare a root owner knows before its first refusal which corpus its design documents are.

**Problem (Every file the generic label export names is a copy target of the publication map)** · `prob:linter:exported-label-files-are-map-targets`

**OPEN.** Measured by wabe on 2026-10-31 and reported in its letter of 20:28:15Z; the missing copy target landed on generic main e98e0894 and was published at 20:31:39Z, and the linter's check is open.

`docs/commit-signing.md` existed at generic main with every signing label exported to `labels.toml` under that file name, and both rulebooks cite the labels, but `maps/generic-map.toml` carried no copy target for it, so the installed tree had the citations without the text. wabe, minting its first signing key, worked from the precedent of an existing key and produced a certify-and-sign root with a live certify half, which the document's invariant names as a finding, and it re-established its key on the published shape at about 20:33Z. The fix added one copy target, the map's 41st, published as the document's installed copy beside the others. A label export that names a file the map does not install is wrong by construction, so the generic linter checks that each exported file name resolves to a map target.

**Problem (The unpublished-labels register's schema is defined twice, and the writer checks less than the reader refuses)** · `prob:linter:unpublished-register-schema-defined-once`

**OPEN.** From review 2 of lane r002g32 on 2026-10-31; no change has landed.

The pack's writer of the register (``cut.rs:421-435`` at tools main 0cb157cb) checks only `path_class`, while the linter's reader (``export.rs:26-76``) refuses malformed labels, unknown owners and duplicates, so a register the cut writes can be one the linter refuses at check time ``entry:estate:cut-tree-acceptance-before-first-publication``. A lane defines the schema once in the shared library, consumed by both, or has the writer validate through the reader's rules.

**Problem (A declared nested root with no tracked paths is accepted vacuously)** · `prob:linter:nested-root-with-no-tracked-paths-is-accepted-vacuously`

**OPEN.** (r002g44 review 1, N1, 2026-11-01 15:24Z). `nested_directory` accepts a declared nested root with no tracked paths because the containment check is `all()` over nothing, and the child snapshot that follows reads `.linter/` from disk, where it may be untracked; a nested declaration can then rest on an untracked child snapshot. Remedy: require a tracked ``.linter/owners.toml`` under the root (one message, one test through the built binary). Companion N2: a `git ls-files` failure inside the function refuses with the wrong reason (the normalized-relative-directory message); give it its own reason.

**Problem (The deferred-mode list rewrite keeps a deactivated pair's stale catalogued table)** · `prob:linter:deferred-mode-rewrite-keeps-a-stale-catalogued-table`

**OPEN.** (r002g44 review 1, N3). In deferred mode `rewrite_lists` keeps every section whose pair it is not rendering, so a stale catalogued table of a deactivated pair survives a rewrite, while the non-deferred mode drops it. Remedy: drop catalogued tables of pairs no longer activated in both modes, keeping only deferred tables verbatim; a test with a deactivated catalogued pair beside a deferred table.

**Problem (Every catalogued burn family's policy accepts an exclude relation)** · `prob:linter:catalogued-burn-families-accept-an-exclude-relation`

**OPEN.** (torrust-index 2026-11-01 18:29Z). A catalogued burn family's policy document declares per-owner `prose` and `code` surfaces only; the one `exclude` datum (BURN_EXCLUDE_DATUM, ``snapshot.rs:165``, excluded_patterns at :3155) belongs to the external public-pack.values-absent burn. A repository whose own linter package necessarily names the burned forms (its patterns and fixtures) has no declaration to leave that package out of a corpus-spanning family's count other than accepting the count into the burn list. Remedy: the same optional `exclude` ABNF relation on every burn family document's owner table (and on the corpus-spanning families' root table), applied by the census before counting, reported in the family's `excluded` field (the report already carries `excluded: []` per family); tests through the built binary; the runbook sentence. Patch class.

**Problem (The reach reconciliation supplies the structural root as it supplies self)** · `prob:linter:reach-reconciliation-supplies-the-structural-root-like-self`

**OPEN.** (torrust-index 2026-11-01 18:33Z, confirmed in source). `Reach::admissible` (``layers.rs:143-156``) materializes self, the crate-bearing workspace root and the manifest edges; `reconcile_declaration` (``layers.rs``:~600-630) inserts self into the declared set because self reach is structural and no row may state it, but not the root, so every built owner must spell a `may_cite` row naming the root although the root is reached structurally and the row carries no information (torrust-index: ASSAYER's divergence lists INDEX with no manifest edge; four owners with no manifest edges are omissions only because of the root). Remedy: the reconciliation supplies the structural root exactly as it supplies self, and a row naming the structural root is refused like a self row; tests; a note in the README's reach paragraph. **OPEN.** (2026-11-04, from siedelweber) A non-participating owner for unchanged upstream trees: an owner in ``owners.toml`` whose files are census-accounted but take part in no labels.* policy, declared by an explicit flag whose rows must name a reason, while the inactive_prose_owner pre-check (an owner has Markdown but activates no labels.mints-well-formed row) keeps holding for every other owner. Found by siedelweber vendoring unchanged upstream crates bound byte for byte to registry archives by a content manifest: bitcoin 0.32.102's unchanged ``CHANGELOG.md`` has two unpaired backticks (lines 153 and 656) that mints-well-formed reads as malformed mints, so an unchanged upstream tree cannot be carried whole. **Problem (The linter checks that the documentation is one connected graph rooted at the rulebook)** · `prob:linter:documentation-graph-check`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, the organizing rule of the documentation-install set, landing with it) A `doc-graph` check over a repository's tracked prose and over the installed documentation roots builds the graph from label citations and relative file links, takes the rendered ``ORCHESTRATION.md`` as the root, and reports by path every document reachable from nowhere and every document without an upward reference; the installer refuses an install whose graph has an orphan (`prob:build:installer-tree-targets-and-index-writer`), so a host never carries an undiscoverable document. References are label citations where the target mints a label and relative links otherwise, so the existing check that every citation resolves is the edge check, and the index writer's ``INDEX.md`` hubs make the leaves reachable by construction.

The requirement is the generic specification's (``prob:docs:documentation-graph-requirement-in-the-architecture-part`` in the generic backlog). Acceptance: the check passes over the three repositories and the installed roots, and a fixture with an orphan and one with a missing upward reference each fail with the path named.
