### Watchers, letters and monitors · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-watchers`

**Problem (a worker-monitor call over a large transcript exceeds the pinned backstop on a healthy lane)** · `prob:watchers:worker-monitor-large-transcript-backstop`

**OPEN.** A check against the tree found the runbook still pinning the 110 s backstop that worker-monitor exceeded over a large transcript on a healthy lane; what remains is measuring the monitor's read of a large transcript (a bounded tail read or a wall report in the state record) and setting the pinned backstop from that measurement.

worker-monitor over a large host transcript exceeds the pinned 110 s backstop (lane r002f08 work step, 2026-10-21): with `--bounce 30 --wait 90` a call over a run directory whose events.jsonl had grown past 1.9 MB returned in 42 s once and hit the 110 s `timeout` twice within an hour while the driver was alive and the transcript growing (exit 124, empty capture), so the watcher's exit-124 rule ends a healthy duty; the monitor's read of a large transcript is the cost to measure (bounded reading of the tail, or a wall report in the state record) and the pinned prompt's backstop is set from that measurement

**Problem (letters follow --once printed nothing beside unread letters in the inbox)** · `prob:watchers:letters-follow-once-misses-unread-letters`

**OPEN.** Why `letters follow --once` printed nothing beside unread letters is not yet named; what remains is measuring `--once` from the loop entry against an inbox holding unread letters, then either making the snapshot list every unread entry or documenting what it lists, and the runbook's inbox-watcher recipe naming `--next --wall` as the watcher's call and `letters list` as its baseline form.

`letters follow --once` printed nothing for three unread letters that sat in `new/` for over a minute each (naturrhythmuszentrum's inbox watcher, 22:18Z to 00:47Z on 2026-10-22/23, every round "LETTERS actions=0"; the letters were read through the hook's tail instead), while the follower's `--once` arm (``packages/loop-home/src/letters/follow.rs:100-107``) is a snapshot of every entry `observations()` returns for `new/` with no cursor, so an empty output beside unread entries is a defect of that snapshot or of what `observations()` admits (a peer filter, a metadata shape, a permission on the entry) and not of the watcher. Fix: measure `--once` from the loop entry against an inbox holding unread letters, name the cause, and either make the snapshot list every unread entry or document what it lists; the runbook's inbox-watcher recipe names the `--next --wall` form as the watcher's call and `letters list` as the baseline form a watcher may use beside it. Where: loop-home letters follower; runbook inbox-watcher paragraph; with items ``entry:watchers:letters-follow-next-hangs-after-notification`` and ``entry:docs:runbook-inbox-watcher-line-shape-and-wall``.

**Problem (The worker monitor names the driver of a resumed step)** · `prob:watchers:worker-monitor-names-resumed-step-driver`

**OPEN.** Measured on the thanks run 20261025T003913Z at 2026-10-25T18:39Z.

The state record of a resumed step carried `driver: null`, so `.driver.alive` is unreadable for liveness. The monitor names the driver of the active step, or the watcher form reads liveness from another field it states.

Measured again on 2026-10-30: lane r002g19's fix1 step, the second resumed step of its run, read `alive=?` in the watcher's capture on every poll after its clean exit at 20:53:33Z, and the watcher missed the step's finish for 57 minutes ``prob:watchers:wait-script-misses-resumed-step-finish``.

**Problem (The watcher's read-back selects the last agent message of a resumed step)** · `prob:watchers:watcher-read-back-selects-last-message`

**OPEN.** Measured on a run directory with many steps (17.5 MB `events.jsonl`).

The watcher form's jq read-back with `head -c 6000` returns the first step's text. The form selects the last `agent_message`, or the active step's range by `.step-name`, for a resumed step.

**Problem (The worker monitor's review residuals in driver follow)** · `prob:watchers:driver-follow-review-residuals`

**OPEN.** Carried from ``entry:watchers:worker-monitor``, whose lane r002c41 (7c0867f3) review left these follow-ups in what is now `superloop-lane driver follow`; none is landed.

The driver's liveness refuses process states Z and X but does not compare the process start time with the lock's mtime, so a reused pid reads alive (`pid_live_from_proc`, ``packages/lane-lifecycle/src/monitor_lock.rs:34`` at tools main 3c0f1a13). The exact-document subprocess tests compare parsed JSON, so nothing pins the byte form of the delta and finished records, which a stdout-suffix assertion restores; the bounded-wait tests start at offset 0 with no event source, so an offset wrongly written on expiry still reads 0, and the test should start from a fresh event source, wait one second and assert the offset unchanged; the wait test's upper bound of 3 s including the process spawn can flake on a loaded node. The ``docs/spec/output.md`` examples list the delta keys as kind, bytes, truncated, events (lines 211 to 213) while the code emits sorted keys beside a state record whose key order is part of the contract; the command table row says every completed invocation's first record is the worker's state (line 272) while a failure envelope opens with its failure record (lines 237 and 238); ``packages/superloop-lane/src/driver/claude/README.md:30`` still names `lane-feed` as the live consumer; and ``docs/spec/output.md`` keeps the phrase "owner-ratified" (line 181), to be rephrased on the merits.

**Problem (A renamed loop's letters from before the rename read as refused in both directions)** · `prob:watchers:renamed-loop-letters-resolve-by-posting-name`

**OPEN.** Measured on 2026-10-31 at 08:45Z, after the rename of ftoo to feldlerche with uid 2014 kept, and by feldlerche at 08:50Z; no change has landed.

The four letters the loop had posted to this loop under its birth name list in the orchestration inbox as `refused reason="letters claimed sender differs from owner uid"`: a letter's claimed sender is a name and its proof is the file's owner uid, which after the rename resolves to `feldlerche`. The mirror holds on the renamed loop's side, where the 13 letters it received before the rename list as refused with `letters malformed receiver or posting instant`, since they carry `receiver=ftoo`. Every one had been read before the move, so nothing was lost, but a letter posted before a rename and read after it would be refused. blueprint's listing flags ftoo's proof letter of 08:29Z the same way. blueprint, which first saw the flag after deploy #85, read it as the absence of a `loops.d` record for `ftoo` and as a flag that came with #85's letters change ``entry:homes:loops-d-record-written-at-registration``; this loop's reading is that the letter was valid when posted, that the rename changed the name uid 2014 resolves to, so no record for the old name can make it resolve, and that the renamed loop's own `loops.d` record waits on a session in its registry row, without which the record writer refuses `estate row requires session for LOOP_SID`. The reader resolves a claimed sender or receiver by the name the uid carried at the letter's posting instant, from the registry's history or a rename record, or the rename procedure names the loop's unread letters in both directions as a step taken before the move.

Measured again on 2026-10-31 at the rename of blueprint to wabe ``entry:homes:rename-attestation-to-tripod``: siedelweber's inbox listed 35 of blueprint's letters as refused at 09:56Z and tripod's 24 at 10:05Z, and the replies told both loops to leave them as they stand, so a rename refuses its loop's letters in every peer's inbox and not only in the two it names.

**Problem (worker-monitor answers a usage error for a boxed round that has just finished)** · `prob:watchers:worker-monitor-usage-error-after-a-finished-round`

**OPEN.** Measured by naturrhythmuszentrum on 2026-10-31 at 20:42:30Z and reported in its letter of 20:43:03Z; no change has landed.

The loop's lane contrib-review-trig-series-1 finished its boxed dispatch round at 20:41:31Z, the launch exiting 0 with its reply of record published. The watcher's next call at 20:42:30Z, `worker-monitor <round_dir> --bounce 30 --wait 70` inside the entry, with the same arguments as the twelve checks before it, returned `{"kind":"usage_error","message":"invalid command-line arguments","fields":{"exit_code":2,"clap_error_kind":"invalid_arguments"}}` instead of the finished record, and the lane was retired at about 20:47Z after one false stop of about five minutes. This loop's host-route watchers see the finished record at the end of every step with the same call, so the round directory's state after a boxed finish differs; whatever the state, a difference is reported as the record found or the record missing, never as a usage error, and the monitor's argument parsing does not depend on the round's state.

### The lane gateway · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-gateway`

**Problem (lane-gateway: every approve ends in a typed result, a lane can withdraw its own sequence, and abandonment reaps the remote process group)** · `prob:gateway:gateway-approve-watchdog`

**OPEN.** Typed endings, the deadline, abandoned reconciliation, lane withdrawal, per-lane placement and the boot identity are in the package (0d4b38e; gw102, gw102res, gwhost, gw102b 1f4b2ab; r002c89 1545159). Open: remote reaping, where the run wrapper runs the command in its own process group and kills it when the ssh channel closes (gateway-runner at tools main 3c0f1a13 traps TERM/INT/HUP only); the one-time zombie scan; the roster-run walk's widened startup abort; and the review residuals of p5, listed in the last paragraph.

Every approve ends in exactly one typed result within a stated deadline (`ok`, `failed`, `timed-out`, `abandoned`), so a lane acts on a verdict rather than inferring its fate from log silence after a reset ssh session, a hung remote command or a serve that died with its parent; a lane may withdraw a sequence it proposed, honoured from the proposing lane only and recorded as a typed result; and abandonment reaps the remote process group, not just the ssh client, because a runner left behind keeps the shared target directory and its lock. The runner's ssh runs without a tty, so the remote side gets no hangup when the client dies and the remote group (the run wrapper's shell and its `timeout` child) holds the directory and lock until the timeout expires; no runner-side change reaches it, and a transport put in a new session outside the runner's process group would receive only the forwarded TERM, never the serve's probe-and-kill escalation. The shape needed is remote: the wrapper runs the command in its own process group and kills that group when the channel closes (keeping the script-on-stdin form the runner uses, and the one to design), or the transport forces a tty so the client's death delivers a hangup.

Raised 2026-09-14 from three inputs of the torrust-index loop, which serves the estate gateway and observed each gap on a live lane; the shape was settled at dispatch against the runner's liveness options and exit-code passthrough that item (`prob:gateway:lane-gateway-liveness-frictions`) landed: the deadline and the withdrawal as serve-side state-machine changes with typed results in the request log, the reaping by addressing the process group through the runner's SIGTERM forwarding, no rubric and no registry change.

Imported 2026-09-18 as `20a9037`, `0db40a6` and `ae05190`, patch byte-identical (lane gw102, gpt-6-astra xhigh: read-back 60 s with one stop for two unstated values, the deadline's source and the termination grace, answered PROCEED; work 1,971 s of 3,600; three named commits, ten tests red-then-green; gate of record on falki 1,021.3 s with 2,118 workspace tests, run beside three other loops' cold builds on the same 13.6-thread cage, against 665–705 s alone; read-only review 620 s, IMPORTABLE). The deadline is the command's `wall_budget_seconds` plus `approve_deadline_margin_seconds` (default 60; builder; `LANE_GATEWAY_APPROVE_MARGIN_SECONDS`); the decision is claimed durably before execution, so a successor serve republishes a recorded result or synthesises `abandoned` without re-executing and without a second audit record; `withdraw` closes an open proposal for its proposing lane only, ownership read from the request channel, recorded as `withdrawn`, with typed refusals for a foreign lane (`withdraw_not_owner`), a closed target and a malformed shape, serialized against approval by the lane lock; the runner starts in its own process group with the group identity persisted, and abandonment sends SIGTERM to the group, waits up to ten seconds (capped so a configured deadline holds), then SIGKILL, with bounded child reaping. Its review's residuals, none a regression against `2a01bb5`, went to the next lane.

Residuals landed 2026-09-18 as `fc3aaeb`, `c9f4332`, `0caaf9b`, `a501eee`, `34cee48` and `31f8163`, patch byte-identical (lane gw102res, gpt-6-astra xhigh: read-back 40 s, work 2,410 s of 3,000 across the shared Codex account's usage limit and a fresh sign-in without a stall; six named commits, each red first; gate of record on falki 704.3 s with 2,128 workspace tests; read-only review 790 s, IMPORTABLE). Startup reconciliation walks the `runner-groups/<lane>/<seq>.json` records a crashed serve leaves for roster runs as well as approves, under the lane lock, and terminates and removes them (a serve killed mid-`run` had left its runner group alive, and the README sentence about inherited groups is now true); a stored group is signalled only when its recorded leader (pid and start time) is present, replacing an `is_none_or` match that admitted a reused pgid after a crash; group liveness during the grace asks `killpg(group, 0)`, keeping one process-table scan for when the direct leader first becomes a zombie, in place of a `/proc` walk at 100 Hz for up to ten seconds; the process-group child harness requires one selected, passed test; a withdrawal result carries its measured wall instead of `wall_millis: 0`; and a zero margin is refused on every route (builder, environment, request, approval). Not taken: a `terminate` failure at startup still aborts the serve rather than degrading the reconciliation result, and `write_state_json_atomic` stays `pub` because clippy's `redundant_pub_crate` refuses the narrower spelling inside a private module.

Placement per lane landed 2026-09-19 as `ea5e1a7`, patch identical, with the runner script in the tooling repository at `d52628e` (lane gwhost, claude-opus-5 xhigh through the Claude CLI lane driver: read-back 90 s, work 902 s of 1,500; one named commit; gate of record on falki 723.7 s with 2,181 workspace tests, after a first run on tjaldur at a one-minute load of 25 to 39, beside a C++ toolchain build, failed six permission-triage topology tests at the 2,000 ms ceiling floor and one rubric fixture wait in an untouched package, item (`prob:gates:topology-suite-load-sensitive`)'s pattern; read-only review 574 s, IMPORTABLE; the gateway built on the host in 42 s and deployed with provenance). The runner had read one ssh alias for the whole serve and derived every lane's mirror from one root, so all of a serve's lanes sat on one node (the index loop's serve at a one-minute load of 27 while the other node sat at 3); the gateway now passes each registration's `mirror_remote` verbatim as `LANE_GATEWAY_MIRROR_REMOTE` at every spawn site, and the runner takes node and bare mirror from an `<alias>:<path>` value, falling back to the serve's two environment defaults, with no schema change and either side safe to land alone (three unit tests red first at the seam, 110 passed, 5 failed, then 123 passed: the remote arrives beside the commit, a blank remote is omitted, spaces and a colon arrive unaltered). The runner prefix stays host-free, because a prefix that bakes a host into its argv defeats the per-lane remote and the gateway cannot detect it; placing a gateway lane is a registration decision, `mirror_remote` naming the node read from `node-load` at registration.

Boot identity landed 2026-09-20 as `1f4b2ab` (lane gw102b, claude-opus-5 xhigh through the Claude CLI lane driver: read-back 90 s, work 1,985 s of 2,400; gate of record on tjaldur green in 1,412.1 s, its workspace tests 22 m 32 s under a peer's load against about 11 min alone; read-only Opus review 420 s, IMPORTABLE without amendment): the runner group record carries the kernel's boot id beside the group and its start ticks, compared before the process is read, so across a reboot a reused pid at the same start tick no longer matches and the record is discarded without a signal, as is a pre-change record without the field, while a corrupt record still refuses as corrupt state; the README states the conservative direction (a record whose leader is gone, or of a foreign boot, is discarded without a signal, so descendants survive). Package tests 123 → 125 (both new cases red first); the workspace round showed two wall-clock-bound cases red under load (`lane-feed` `a_dropped_guard_kills_and_reaps_the_feeder_it_held`, `orchestrator-hook` `debug_test_panic_uses_replaced_hook_and_never_exposes_payload`) that reran green at the same tree, 2,217 passed. Release build 40 s, 1,932,648 B, sha256 `5f1e4e1d…`, deployed with provenance.

Lane r002c89 (2026-09-28, gpt-6-sol xhigh, 3,166 s and a fix step of 305 s; gates green on orel in 845.1 s and 941.0 s on the code class; opus review NOT IMPORTABLE on the first commit in 740 s, then IMPORTABLE on the fix in 185 s; imported as 1545159) found the typed endings and the withdrawal already in the package since the workspace moved to the tools repository (0d4b38e); its first commit, the transport in a new session, was dropped on review, leaving the reaping open.

The estate serve is the torrust-index loop's workstation process and keeps its inode until that loop relaunches it on the new binary. Still open: the one-time zombie scan (a descendant alive at that instant and gone a moment later leaves the grace running to its bound on a zombie-only group); the roster-run walk's widened startup abort (a `killpg` EPERM or a non-`ENOENT` `/proc` read on an inherited record fails every restart until the record is removed by hand); the bounded-parallel serve, a design bite of its own since the serve loop is synchronous across every lane; the boot id read from `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id` at every capture rather than once per serve; and gw102b's review residuals, none blocking: a boot-id read error on the live termination path is swallowed into "no match"; a pre-change binary refuses a post-change record as corrupt state under `deny_unknown_fields`, so a rollback after new records exist is refused at startup; and the host-wide boot id does not cover a gateway restarted inside a fresh pid namespace without a host reboot.

**Problem (A closed lane's server target directory outlives its registration)** · `prob:gateway:closed-lane-target-directory-outlives-registration`

**OPEN.** 2026-09-26, reported by the torrust-index loop with measurements. The runner's server checkout of a lane keeps its cargo target directory after the lane is deregistered, about 16 GB each, so closed lanes accumulate until the workspace overlay is full: at about 15:00Z sykora's overlay reached 100 percent with 14 MB free and a lane's replay died with ENOSPC while orel stood at 60 percent, with 71 closed lanes on sykora and 36 on orel under `lanes/checkouts` (884 GB and 453 GB). The loop deleted the target directories of the closed lanes only, enumerated by name against the live registrations, which brought sykora to 19 percent and orel to 22 percent. The residue is structural in the same way as the host's (``entry:servers:target-directory-residue-guard``): a target directory is owned by one purpose and is deleted when that purpose ends, never swept by hand later. Two changes, both in the tooling repository's runner: deregistration, or the next provisioning of a lane on that node, removes the closed lane's target directory beside its checkout; and the runner keeps a per-node free-space floor and reports the node's free space in every run result, so a lane that would fill the overlay is refused before its replay rather than dying inside it.

**Problem (Seven frictions of a peer loop's day through the gateway: late acknowledgements, a cooldown a worker resets itself, a vendor at capacity, and four smaller ones)** · `prob:gateway:gateway-day-frictions`

**OPEN.** Partly answered: the drivers' capacity retry landed (item ``entry:lanes:capacity-retry-sized-to-the-outage``, deploy #44) and the requests channel shows filed requests as pending; the approval tier stays open in item (`open:gateway:gateway-argv-template-approval-tier`). Open here, unless the gateway retires first: gateway acknowledgement at receipt (with the latency first located from the audit timestamps), approved proposals taking precedence over the lane's next run, re-sent unacknowledged notices, a heartbeat-guard retry for an arming attempt failed outside the session, and per-monitor capture files.

The torrust-index loop reported seven frictions on 2026-09-29 after twelve lanes landed through the gateway in one day, each recorded with where it is answered. (1) A request took minutes to be acknowledged, so a worker could not tell queued from dropped (nine workers named it in their closing sentence; 132.9 s queued against 134.0 s executing, 167.6 s queued, 743.4 s queued against 458.6 s executing, part of the last the approving loop's own latency); the requests channel's states answer it by design, a filed request being visible as pending at once ``entry:estate:one-file-two-subjects``, and until then the gateway owes an acknowledgement at receipt. (2) An approved proposal waits for the lane's cooldown of 65 to 70 s, and the worker's next registered run resets the cooldown and holds the lane's lock, so the proposal waits again (up to eleven approve attempts for one proposal, the worker unable to see that its own run is the cause); an approved proposal takes precedence over the lane's next registered run, or the worker is told what it waits behind. (3) At about 17:10 UTC the vendor answered that the selected model was at capacity and the driver ended four worker steps as failed, two past their budget because they had waited on the gateway (no work lost, one lane recovering by itself on a new thread; the same answer ended one step of this loop at that hour); a bounded, reported retry with backoff inside the driver saves the relaunches, and the continuation of a step that ended from outside is stated in the specification (its ``proto:dispatch:external-interruption-resume``). (4) About forty checkouts of closed lanes remained under the index loop's lane folder on orel from before that loop removed them at close; it removes them by enumerated path ``entry:estate:operations-profile``. (5) From about 19:27 to 19:36 UTC the permission classifier returned a server error for shell calls and the wakeup call, so a vetted proposal waited ten minutes, and the heartbeat guard refused as unprompted the same-turn retry of an arming attempt that had failed on the classifier; the guard accepts a retry of an arming attempt that failed for a reason outside the session. (6) Two notices of pending proposals never reached the approving loop, around that outage and around a host suspend of about eleven minutes, and were found by a hand sweep at nine to ten minutes of age; an unacknowledged notice is sent again. (7) Two monitors on one run directory race on the fixed capture file and saw empty and half-written reads; a capture file named per monitor makes the mistake harmless.

Folded in from an earlier finding (torrust-index, 2026-10-14, deploy #12 ack): a push or approval was acknowledged several minutes after the run (one push ~9 min after a 0.94 s run), which dominated index's step-three wall overruns; where the wait sits (watcher poll interval, serve queue, approval round trip) is measured from the gateway audit timestamps before a change is proposed, and it relates to item ``entry:door:requests-wait-replaces-polling`` (a blocking wait in place of a poll). The gateway serve is torrust-index's, so any change to it is made with that loop's consent and in its idle.

**Problem (The lane gateway serves one loop: its state in the loop's home, its process under the loop's id)** · `prob:gateway:gateway-serve-per-loop`

**PARKED.** Held 2026-09-29 until the lane box: the shape stands and no per-loop serve is built, because the lane box and its door retire the gateway (item ``entry:box:lane-box-build-out``; item ``entry:estate:superloop-tools-rename-pass``) and an interim serve would be built to be removed. Revisit when the box's build date is known, if holding the shared serve until then costs more than building the per-loop serve; the shared root's archive path is stated either way.

One `lane-gateway serve` runs for the whole estate over one state root under the tools checkout's state directory, as the host account, started by whichever loop's session launched it: every loop's registrations, proposals, verdicts, watermarks and audit ledger sit in one folder readable by all, and a stop or redeploy by one loop stalls another's lanes in flight, which is why the runbook carries a coordination rule instead of a mechanism. Every other piece of loop state lives in the loop's own home under the loop's own id, so the gateway alone crosses loops, a layer violation. The shape: one serve per loop, its state root in the loop's home, the process entered under the loop's id and started by that loop, so a loop's requests, ledger and restarts are its own and no loop waits on another. The work: the gateway takes its state root from the loop (no default naming the shared folder), the lane drivers' `--gateway-lane` reading and `LANE_GATEWAY_STATE` follow it, the runbook's gateway section states the per-loop serve and drops the coordination rule, and the shared root is archived once every loop using the gateway has started its own serve; no compatibility path reads the shared root beside the per-loop one. In the lane box a worker reaches compute through the capability door, and the gateway's records are archived at that point.

Measured at writing: the shared root held one registration, dated 2026-08-25, and the running serve's last activity equalled its start. The serving loop's view (2026-09-28, measured against the shared root where it could be): it runs every producing lane through the gateway, its last on 2026-09-27, and knows of no other loop using the root; the one live registration, `lane-036` of 2026-08-25, is its own, its worktree that loop's launch directory and its mirror naming a decommissioned server, so it is deregistered at the move and not carried. Nothing moves into that loop's home: no proposal is open (369 proposal directories against 369 verdict directories) and closed lanes' watermarks have no reader; the audit (956 MB of the root's 985 MB, 459 lane files) is cited by that loop's verification records by lane and sequence, so the archive stays readable at a stated path and is not pruned. Its order of moves: (a) the per-loop state root and serve land behind the existing state variable, the only place that loop's scripts name the root; (b) this loop starts its own serve; (c) the serving loop starts its serve under its own home once it resumes, runs one throwaway lane end to end on each node, then leaves the shared root, its runner needing the loop account's ssh route to the two nodes, an owner step; (d) the shared root is archived read-only.

Behaviour that loop asked for, in its order, each a candidate for the same lane or a follow-up: remote reaping of a killed run that still holds the node's target lock (item (`prob:gateway:gateway-approve-watchdog`)); a bounded lifetime for an approve whose server job died; a run-cooldown refusal that does not spend the sequence; a `set-cap` that keeps the ledger; a schema number that moves with the record fields; audit rotation or a per-lane size note, since results carry full output inline; and the serve as a unit of the loop's account and not a session's child.

**Open Problem (Bundles whose argv differs per run would need an argv-template approval tier)** · `open:gateway:gateway-argv-template-approval-tier`

**PARKED.** Revisit once the index loop has tried promotion; promote with deny-list vetting is the answer until then.

Earlier status: OPEN.

gateway auto-approval tier (torrust-index owner request 2026-10-14): six hpin\* lanes each propose bounded bundles (seal, capture, mutation batch) and wait on a manual verdict per proposal. What exists: roster commands run by name with fixed argv (README:7), proposals wait for a verdict (README:106-112), and `approve --promote [--as NAME]` appends the exact argv to the live roster so later runs need no verdict (README:116); answered to index: promote each recurring bundle once, vet against the deny list at the promotion verdict, keep run cap, cooldown and proposal timeout as the bounds. Open design question: argv templates (fixed head, bounded slots) for bundles whose argv differs per run would be a new tier in the trust boundary; weigh after index has tried promotion. Separate from item (`prob:gateway:gateway-day-frictions`) (serve latency).

**Problem (lane-gateway: an approve parks on a reset ssh session, requests are read non-atomically, and the serve dies with its parent)** · `prob:gateway:lane-gateway-liveness-frictions`

**OPEN.** All three defects landed and the binary flipped 2026-09-13 at 20:35Z (swap) and 20:36Z (restart by the torrust-index loop at its quiescent signal): `~/.local/bin/lane-gateway` is the release build of main `944f5ee` (package-scoped, SDK route, 72.8 s, over the 60 s host tripwire with one cold dependency; sha256 `5712109031e3736d…`, provenance beside it; the previous `a722b97` build kept for rollback), the runner's `runner_connection_lost` classification is live, and the runbook's serve-health rewrite against the deployed binary landed as tools `4c783a8` (2026-09-13), which also reconciles the stale threshold to twice the interval. Still open, carried by no open item and present at tools main 3c0f1a13: the gwfix2 review residue for the next gateway bite, namely the generation-reset comment in ``serve.rs``, `SIGNAL_WATCH: OnceLock<bool>` caching a first spawn failure, a signal accepted between `thread_block()` and the reset being wiped, the shutdown-ordering test's scheduling slack, the README's claim that ssh never returns 255 for a remote status, the runner's unguarded TERM before `pid=$!`, its reported rather than re-raised ssh status and the ssh a SIGKILL orphans, `heartbeat_liveness` with no caller outside its tests, and the pid rule's silence on pid namespaces.

Three liveness defects, each with its rule. A runner's ssh without liveness options parks an approve on a half-open socket when the server resets the connection, so the runner carries `-o ServerAliveInterval=30 -o ServerAliveCountMax=4 -o ConnectTimeout=30` (a dead peer noticed within about two minutes, a hanging connect refused at thirty seconds) and ssh's own exit 255 is recorded as `RefusalRule::RunnerConnectionLost`; the exit-code convention is 255 transport, 124 remote `timeout`, else the command, which roster commands honour by convention rather than proof. A request read non-atomically can be seen truncated at creation, so an empty, UTF-8-truncated or end-of-input read is unfinished and re-polled, and the charter requires a temp-file-plus-rename write. A serve that is a child of the session dies with it while its heartbeat still says running, so the serve records why it stopped and readers judge liveness from the pid and the beat's age. The approve wall bound is `proposal.timeout_seconds` (default 600 s), which becomes the runner's wall budget, killed on expiry by `wait_for_runner`; a stop request or signal takes effect at the end of a cycle that is executing a runner, so SIGKILL is the only immediate stop of a busy serve.

Defects 2 and 3 imported 2026-09-13 by lane gwlive, the first real `workspace-write` Claude CLI lane (opus xhigh through `claude-lane`; read-back 95 s, work 2,550 s of a 3,600 s budget; gate of record 97 s green with 1,929 tests; opus review 460 s importable, no blockers), as `12b7166` and `d874d6b` over `3df1cc1`, eleven files (the package plus one ``Cargo.lock`` line). An unfinished request is re-polled for `INCOMPLETE_REQUEST_CYCLE_BOUND = 4` cycles (two seconds at the default cadence) with one `request_unfinished` audit line carrying the lane and the byte count and never the bytes, then refused once as `malformed_json` and remembered as seen; a malformed complete file is refused on the cycle that reads it, and a prefix read at startup is deferred. SIGTERM and SIGINT are blocked before the publisher thread exists and accepted on a watch thread; `mark_stopped` sets `process_state: stopped` with a `stop_reason` (`shutdown_flag`, `signal_terminate`, `signal_interrupt`, `error`, `panic`); the heartbeat carries `heartbeat_interval_millis`; and `heartbeat_liveness(&heartbeat, now)` reads stopped, dead when `/proc/<pid>` is gone or the beat is older than twice the interval, alive otherwise (a backwards clock reads as fresh). Eight tests were each shown red before their change. One-way door: `AuditEvent::RequestUnfinished` is a new audit `kind` while `AUDIT_SCHEMA` stays 2, and `Gateway::audit_records` fails a lane's whole audit as `CorruptAudit` on an unknown kind; the strict heartbeat type (`deny_unknown_fields`, no default for the interval, since zero would judge a live old serve dead) parses across versions in neither direction; so deployment is readers first, then serves, with the torrust-index loop told before its serve is replaced.

The second serve bite imported 2026-09-13 by lane gwfix2 (Claude CLI lane, opus xhigh; read-back 85 s, work 3,305 s of 3,600; gate of record 590 s green with 1,970 tests; opus review 473 s importable, no blockers) as `2271f8b` and `944f5ee` over `c4421cf`, nine files under `packages/lane-gateway`, package tests 92 → 99. `WATCHED_SIGNALS` adds SIGHUP with `stop_reason: signal_hangup`; every `serve()` call blocks the watched set on its own thread before the publisher thread exists (the `OnceLock` guards only the watch-thread spawn), `OBSERVED_SIGNAL` is reset at generation start, and a signal exit records `signal_stop` rather than `clean_shutdown`. The SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGINT exits are proven against a real subprocess with a bounded wait and a `Drop` that kills and reaps, the panic path records `stopped`/`panic`, and ``tests/serve_generations.rs`` proves two sequential generations on two threads each block the set. A spawn or pipe failure stays `runner_failed`, an over-budget kill stays `over_budget`, and a run refused this way still consumes a run slot.

The flip was one step for binary and serve because neither version's reader parses the other's heartbeat: no CLI command ran between swap and restart, the old serve stopped on the shutdown flag in about six seconds (one cycle, no SIGTERM fallback), the flag was cleared before the new start (a new serve otherwise stops on its first check, which the step now says), the first new heartbeat carried `schema` 1, `heartbeat_interval_millis` 5000, `process_state` running and `activity` started, and the reader printed alive fifteen seconds after start.

Inputs for the approve watchdog, which reads the runner's output growth against the request's declared timeout and never a fixed cap, since two twelve-minute approves on one day, one legitimate and one doomed, differed by output and not by wall: every approve ends in a typed result (completed, over_budget, runner_failed, runner_connection_lost, or abandoned by the watchdog when output stops past the declared timeout), because an approve whose runner died server-side once wrote no result and held the lane lock fifty minutes while its worker finished without its proof; and a lane may withdraw only its own pending or parked sequence (typed result `withdrawn` with the reason, releasing lock and sequence), never another lane's, which would erase evidence.

Residue for the next gateway bite: the reset comment in ``serve.rs`` argues from one generation per process while the test binary serves concurrently (restate that production enters `serve` once from `main`, or make the reset conditional); `SIGNAL_WATCH: OnceLock<bool>` caches a first spawn failure for the life of the process; a signal accepted between `thread_block()` and the reset is wiped; the shutdown-ordering test rests on about 300 ms of scheduling slack under load; the README's claim that ssh never returns 255 for a remote status misses a remote shell killed by a signal; in the runner, a TERM between the trap install and `pid=$!` forwards nothing (one guard line closes it), ssh's status is reported rather than re-raised so a signalled runner looks like exit 143, and a SIGKILL orphans ssh where `exec` did not; the runbook's stale threshold (three cadences, 15 s) and the library's (twice the interval) are reconciled when the library becomes the reader of record; `heartbeat_liveness` has no caller until the runbook's serve-health probe uses it; the pid rule is silent on pid namespaces.

The paragraphs of this item's evidence that name an estate's private details are kept in that estate's backlog as ``rec:gateway:lane-gateway-liveness-frictions``.

### Hooks · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-hooks`

**Problem (the orchestrator hook injects every missed rulebook render instead of one render and a notice)** · `prob:hooks:hook-injects-one-render-per-start`

**OPEN.** The orchestrator hook injected both rulebook renders around 870be3c2 into one session start, as the torrust-tracker loop measured at 2026-10-22T14:14Z; what remains is the hook injecting one render per start and leaving the change notice to the snapshot diff, since a render missed by an absent session is no reason to inject it.

The orchestrator hook injected both rulebook renders, the one before and the one after a generic change (870be3c2), in one session start, so the session saw two full renders instead of one render and the change notice. Measured by the torrust-tracker loop at its session start of 2026-10-22T14:14Z after a 17-hour absence. The hook's change detection compares against the last injected render; a render missed by an absent session is not a reason to inject it, since the snapshot diff is the route for a change notice.

**Problem (heartbeat guard: a paused loop's OFF state does not survive a compaction)** · `prob:hooks:heartbeat-off-does-not-survive-compaction`

**OPEN.** The compaction hypothesis was refuted in code and pinned by tests (9423d493, lane r002c47), and the pause rules landed 2026-09-26 in the guard's README with the order corrected (d78dad77, lane r002c52). Still open, carried by no open item: the OFF warning's text, which invites an `on` from a stale tick, and the toggle's mismatch visibility, since at tools main 3c0f1a13 the toggle still keys the flag by the shell's `PWD` and prints only its own root (``packages/session-core/src/heartbeat_toggle.rs:39``).

A stored OFF survives a compaction: only an explicit `on` creates the heartbeat flag file and only `off` removes it; the guard's Stop and its `ScheduleWakeup` PreToolUse read it without writing; a `SessionStart` whose source is a compaction reaches only the injection hook, which never touches it. The incident came from a key mismatch and a pending wakeup: `off` removes the flag keyed by the project root resolved from the shell's `PWD` while the hooks read the flag keyed by the root resolved from the payload's working directory, so an `off` from another directory leaves the hooks' flag present; and `off` touches files and cannot cancel a harness timer. The pause rules therefore are: the toggle runs from the session's launch directory; and `off` runs first, then the armed timer is ended with a `ScheduleWakeup` carrying `stop: true`, because while the toggle is ON the guard's `PreToolUse` gate does not read `stop` and refuses any wakeup without a fresh prompt marker, which the earlier arming consumed, whereas after `off` it allows the call with a warning.

Reported 2026-09-23 from the tripod loop: with the guard set OFF at 12:17Z for a pause, the compaction at about 12:26Z left the guard reading ON and a stale tick fired carrying the pre-pause prompt text; the guard was set OFF again by hand and nothing was acted on. The registered hooks are `heartbeat-guard hook` on Stop and PreToolUse and `orchestrator-hook hook` on SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit (`packages/heartbeat-guard`, `toggle.rs` and ``machine.rs``).

Answered 2026-09-26 by lane r002c47 (gpt-6-sol high through the Codex lane driver: read-back 20 s, work 1,455 s of 3,600; opus review importable with the subject corrected from fix to test, since no runtime code changed): the new tests replay a compaction session start, a Stop and a wakeup PreToolUse against a stored OFF and read OFF afterwards, and the injection scenario against a stored OFF and a stored ON finds each as it was; imported as 9423d493. The tripod loop's transcript settled the mechanism: the pause ran `off` inside a command that had changed into the scratchpad, the toggle printed the scratchpad's key while every hook payload carried the project directory, and the tick was the 1,200 s wakeup armed before the pause, firing seven seconds after the compaction resume; no second session was involved.

The rules landed 2026-09-26 by lane r002c52 (gpt-6-sol high through the Codex lane driver: read-back 20 s, work 550 s of 1,800; opus review importable with amended text), imported as d78dad77. The earlier status and the note sent to the loop that met the incident had the two steps in the reverse order; the review derived the correct order from the gate's code and its contract test, and the reversed order was withdrawn.

Open residue: the OFF warning's text, which invites an `on` from a stale tick, is reconsidered; and the toggle makes a key mismatch visible in its own output, printing the hooks' key when it differs from the shell's or taking an explicit root argument, so the mismatch shows at the pause and not at the later tick.

**Problem (Three spec-authority hook fixtures carry an expected stdout no test compares)** · `prob:hooks:spec-fixture-stdout-unchecked`

**OPEN.** Carried from ``entry:hooks:hook-test-residuals``, whose lane hookres (b890487) recorded this pre-existing gap; nothing is landed.

In `packages/superloop-claude-hook/tests/orchestrator/fixtures/expected/`, the spec-authority fixtures ``spec-nonstring-source.json``, ``spec-nonstring-cwd.json`` and ``spec-nonstring-prompt.json`` carry a nonempty `expected_stdout` whose bytes were captured from the Python oracle, while their tests (`adapter_specified_nonstring_source_and_cwd_effects_override_python_state_details` at ``tests/oracle_fixtures.rs:209`` and `adapter_specified_nonstring_prompt_and_redacted_log_override_python_details` at line 272, tools main 3c0f1a13) assert the status and the state only, so the fixtures README's definition of `expected_stdout` as the specified hook bytes is enforced only where a test reads it.

**Problem (The SessionStart hook injects nothing into a subagent's context)** · `prob:hooks:session-start-hook-skips-subagent-contexts`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-03, from the hand-back of the c07 and c08 reviews of lane r002g76) The SessionStart hook injects the rendered orchestration rulebook into the subagent contexts of the superloop session, which an Opus reviewer in that session reported on 2026-11-03 as a scoping failure under the rulebook's audience preamble (``pre:orch:audience``): a subagent's brief and charter govern its task, so the hook recognises a subagent context and injects nothing there.

### The public pack · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-pack`

**Problem (The tools tree carries matches against the instance burn register, so a pack is refused)** · `prob:pack:tools-tree-burn-register-matches`

**OPEN.** The tools were published by `superloop-pack cut` rather than as a public pack ``entry:estate:tools-public-cut-after-private-reference-audit``, and their three audits classed the tree by the severity classes rather than against this register, so the f07 survey's 123 matches are unmeasured since; they still need removing or declaring, with the values policy of (`prob:linter:values-absent-policy-plain-check`) active, before a tools pack can pass `verify`.

Earlier status: OPEN — The 123 matches the f07 survey found against the instance burn register have not been rechecked, because that register is instance-private; the matches still need removing or declaring, with the values policy of (`prob:linter:values-absent-policy-plain-check`) active, before the next tools pack can pass `verify`.

tools repository public pack (f07 survey, 2026-10-21): the tracked tree carries 123 matches against the instance burn register (`linter/public-pack-burn.toml`, 54 entries) and the plain check activates no external burn policy, so a pack built from it is refused by `verify` under the restored register binding (item (`prob:pack:pack-verify-burn-evidence-rule`)'s guard) until the matches are removed or declared and the values policy of item (`prob:linter:values-absent-policy-plain-check`) is active; the next tools pack waits on this.

**Problem (public-pack verify refuses burn evidence over a tree its own linter reports clean)** · `prob:pack:pack-verify-burn-evidence-rule`

**OPEN.** The `burn_evidence` refusal was already fixed at base, verified by lane D, and pack 0.3.0's first finding (no staged index) is closed by the deployed 1aae006 verify. Still open, carried by no open item: pack 0.3.0's second finding, the declaration row for the generated `docs/public-pack-provenance.toml` member, which neither the tools nor the generic tree's `.linter` declarations name at tools main 3c0f1a13 and generic main 13d05729.

`public-pack verify` (``packages/public-pack/src/lib.rs`` verdict step): the eighth check, `linter_verdict`, refuses with reason `burn_evidence` over the tools pack 0.2.0 tree while the same linter over the same staged tree reports clean under the gate's method (0 failures, burn occurrences 0, owed 0 over 305 files; measured 2026-10-19 16:44Z); the verify reads the linter's burn block by a rule that differs from the gate's probe; the fix reads that step against the linter's burn output and ships a fixture that verifies a built pack end to end. Pack 0.3.0's first finding (no staged index) is closed by the deployed 1aae006 verify; its second (the generic declaration row for `docs/public-pack-provenance.toml`) is still open.

**Problem (The cut's refusals on its executable route to the linter are tested only on the linter's side)** · `prob:pack:cut-executable-route-refusals-tested`

**OPEN.** From review 2 of lane r002g32 on 2026-10-31; no change has landed.

No pack-side test covers the cut's refusals on its executable route to the linter (``cut.rs:465-486`` at tools main 0cb157cb): a missing `--linter`, a non-zero exit, unparseable JSON and an unknown protocol; only the linter's side is tested (``corpus.rs:4876-4904``). A lane adds one test per refusal through the built binary, so that the route r002g32's F7 introduced is held at both ends ``entry:estate:cut-tree-acceptance-before-first-publication``.

**Problem (The cut waits on the linter it invokes without a time bound)** · `prob:pack:cut-linter-invocation-time-bound`

**OPEN.** From review 2 of lane r002g32 on 2026-10-31; no change has landed.

`Command::output()` in the cut (``cut.rs:469-475`` at tools main 0cb157cb) waits on the linter's citation sites without a bound, so a hung linter hangs a direct cut with no report ``entry:estate:cut-tree-acceptance-before-first-publication``. A lane gives the invocation a bounded wait and a refusal that names the bound.

**Problem (The register plan and the public owners are read from the working tree rather than from the tag's blobs)** · `prob:pack:register-planning-from-tag-blobs`

**OPEN.** Carried from review 1 of lane r002g32 (F5's note) on 2026-10-31; no change has landed.

The cut's unpublished-register plan and its `public_owners` read the working tree at the root, which is safe only because the cut refuses a dirty checkout; the cut otherwise reads the tag's blobs. A lane plans the register from the tag's blobs too, so that the register cannot depend on the refusal ``entry:estate:cut-tree-acceptance-before-first-publication``.

**Problem (The public-set manifest declares the unpublished-labels register instead of the snapshot's owners file implying it)** · `prob:pack:manifest-declares-the-unpublished-register`

**OPEN.** From review 1 of lane r002g32 on 2026-10-31; no change has landed.

The cut generates the unpublished-labels register when the public set carries ``.linter/owners.toml``, and the linter loads it from that file's declaration, so the manifest that defines the public set does not say that a register exists ``entry:estate:cut-tree-acceptance-before-first-publication``. The manifest's `[public]` table declares the register's path, the cut writes it there, and the snapshot's declaration follows the manifest. The deploy of a tools main whose cut bears a snapshot needs the `--burn-register` input the publication names, which review 1's F6 fixed in the lane; and the guard's breadth is accepted ``entry:pack:register-guard-honours-match-kind``.

**Problem (The replay's projection spawns git processes per file instead of using git's batch forms)** · `prob:pack:replay-projection-spawns-git-per-file`

**OPEN.** (r002g48 review 1, O1). The replay's preflight and staging spawn about three git processes per file per tree (about 1,067 files per commit), 496 s for an 18-commit range; a 40-commit range would take about 18 minutes. Remedy: the batch forms (`cat-file --batch`, `hash-object --stdin-paths`, `update-index --index-info`) in the projection; patch class once the transition has run.

**Problem (The replay's optional tests: an unparseable historical manifest, the unpublished-field audit mapping, the drift burn scan and the identity fields)** · `prob:pack:replay-optional-tests`

**OPEN.** (r002g58 review 1, 2026-11-02) Optional tests not covered (reviewer item 3): unparseable historical manifest, unpublished-field audit mapping, burn scan of drift paths, committer and email fields.

**Problem (The release refuses a non-string public version in a Cargo repository)** · `prob:pack:release-refuses-a-non-string-public-version`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-02, r002g67 review F3) `superloop-pack release` compares only a string `public.version` with the Cargo workspace version; a non-string value (`version = 2`) is ignored in a Cargo repository while the documents route refuses it as "must be a string". Refuse it under `version` in the Cargo branch too, with a test beside cargo_version_precedence. Also noted (F5): Unreleased entries carry no Added/Changed/Fixed subsections; the release stamp sorts them.

**Problem (The signed-shape refusal has an integration test that forces a drift)** · `prob:pack:signed-shape-refusal-integration-test`

**OPEN.** r002g69 R2-3: the signed-shape refusal (F2) has no integration test forcing a drift; rests on code order and the release_unsigned_shape unit test. A fixture with a signer that rewrites the message would prove it end to end.

**Problem (The release verifies the signatures it publishes before the ref transaction)** · `prob:pack:release-verifies-the-signatures-it-publishes`

**OPEN.** r002g73 review T1 (tools, release signing, 2026-11-02): `superloop-pack release` apply never verifies the signatures it publishes; a gpg.program that prints `[GNUPG:] SIG_CREATED` and returns non-signature bytes gets a junk `gpgsig` header into the release merge past `verify_shapes` (the header is stripped before the comparison); the tag's marker check refuses the same. Fix: run `git verify-commit` and `git verify-tag` on the staged objects before the ref transaction, same configuration and environment, refuse as `signing`. Today the loop's release procedure verifies by hand in the entry before the push, so the gap is procedural cover only. Next tools lane candidate.

**Problem (The tag marker refusal has an end-to-end test)** · `prob:pack:tag-marker-refusal-end-to-end-test`

**OPEN.** r002g73 review T2 (tools test): an end-to-end test of the tag marker refusal is feasible with such a wrapper (refs and objects asserted unchanged) and covers the commit once T1 exists; c03's fallback reason holds only for the unsigned fields (git writes them; the signer runs before the object exists).

**Problem (The tag drift cases cover the object line)** · `prob:pack:tag-drift-cases-cover-the-object-line`

**OPEN.** r002g73 review T3 (tools test, minor): the tag drift cases lack the `object` line (a tag pointing at something other than the final merge); one-line addition with T1/T2.

**Problem (The pack verifier extracts without an undeclared external unzip)** · `prob:pack:stage-verify-declares-its-extraction-dependency`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, measured at the first root-full node's acceptance) ``superloop-pack stage-verify`` extracts with an external ``unzip`` that it never declares, so a node without it refuses every pack at `extraction`: the tools gate on the new node had 48 of 49 fast release tests green until unzip was layered, bsdtar being present. Either the node program requirements are declared where the deployment specification lists the node-side contract (`spec:deployment:tool-build-and-install-contracts`) and the kit layers them, or the verifier extracts through a library and the dependency disappears; the gate form's node tools build names what it needs either way.

**Problem (The cut acceptance test compares commit times as epoch seconds rather than a rendered date string)** · `prob:pack:cut-acceptance-compares-epoch-seconds`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, measured at the first root-full node's acceptance) ``packages/superloop-pack/tests/cut_acceptance.py`` asserts a git-rendered date string, `%aI %cI` equal to a `+00:00` form, while git 2.56 prints `Z` for UTC in the strict ISO form, so the slow test `cut-tree-acceptance` fails on a current node while the tree is correct; the pod's git 2.43 passed it. The test compares epoch seconds (`%at %ct`) or accepts both renderings, so that a git upgrade on a node is not a red of an untouched test.

**Problem (The cut report renders its date from the parsed instant rather than from git)** · `prob:pack:cut-report-renders-its-date-from-the-parsed-instant`

**OPEN.** (2026-11-05, found by the Opus review of lane r002g79's first commit) The cut report's `date=` field is rendered from git's `%aI`, so the pack's own report prints `Z` or `+00:00` for a UTC instant depending on the git version, while the publication date parser normalises an input `Z` to `+00:00`. A report whose date depends on the git version of the node that cut it cannot be compared across nodes, so the report renders the instant itself from the parsed value rather than asking git to render it; the acceptance test's own comparison is (`prob:pack:cut-acceptance-compares-epoch-seconds`).

### Desktop and chat export · `sec:toolsbacklog:open-desktop`

**Problem (the desktop package's read-only fixture compares access times)** · `prob:desktop:desktop-fixture-ignores-atime`

**OPEN.** The desktop read-only fixture's snapshot still includes access time, which failed main gate main837689 at 2026-10-22T10:44Z on a tree that passed in candidate gate r002f25b; what remains is comparing bytes, modes, ownership and modification time only, with a fixture note stating why.

the desktop package's read-only fixture compares a snapshot that includes file metadata, so a dry run can fail on the node's access-time update (main gate main837689, 2026-10-22T10:44Z: `sessions_dry_run_roots_live_forks_and_merge` panicked at ``tests/subprocess_contract.rs:10`` with `read-only command changed bytes or metadata` from ``tests/fixture.py:192`` after `merge-sessions` in read-only mode; the same tree passed in the candidate gate r002f25b 20 minutes earlier). Fix: the snapshot compares bytes, modes, ownership and modification time, never access time; a fixture note states why.

**Problem (The chat export's last-round residuals: identifier-keyed attachment, a cut leading character and unproven nearest-first pairing)** · `prob:desktop:chat-export-pairing-residuals`

**OPEN.** Carried from ``entry:desktop:chat-export-rust-program``, whose tenth reader round over tools main 26cdd91 left these non-blocking findings; none is landed.

Subagent attachment looks the spawning call up by identifier, first occurrence winning (``packages/superloop-chat-export/src/agents.rs``, lines 150 to 180 at tools main 3c0f1a13), so two malformed calls sharing one identifier can take the same thread link; a library call below the command line's floor can cut a leading multibyte character to nothing without its count; and the conservation check does not prove nearest-first pairing for repeated identifiers.
